The persistent image of Fang’s overthreatening act still corroded the merchant’s throat with bitterness, for on his right he saw the extinction of his business as unremunerative if he agreed, and on his left he saw the extinction of his business as undependable if he refused to agree.
Furthermore, the omens were ill-arranged.
On his way outwards he had encountered an aged man who possessed two fruit-trees, on which he relied for sustenance. As Wong Ts’in drew near, this venerable person carried from his dwelling two beaten cakes of dog-dung and began to bury them about the root of the larger tree. This action, on the part of one who might easily be a disguised wizard, aroused Wong Ts’in’s interest.
“Why,” he demanded, “having two cakes of dung and two fruit-trees, do you not allot one to each tree, so that both may benefit and return to you their produce in the time of your necessity?”
“The season promises to be one of rigour and great need,” replied the other. “A single cake of dung might not provide sufficient nourishment for either tree, so that both should wither away. By reducing life to a bare necessity I could pass from one harvest to another on the fruit of this tree alone, but if both should fail I am undone. To this end I safeguard my existence by ensuring that at least the better of the two shall thrive.”
“Peace attend your efforts!” said Wong Ts’in, and he began to retrace his footsteps, well content.
Yet he had not covered half the distance back when his progress was impeded by an elderly hag who fed two goats, whose milk alone preserved her from starvation. One small measure of dry grass was all that she was able to provide them with, but she divided it equally between them, to the discontent of both.

===Chinese===

“然而，”她语气斩钉截铁地续道，“倘若这手握王秦立身根本之人，正巧渴盼得到王秦手中之至宝以安身立命，那么在后者应允归宿之前，前者自当紧锁门扉、秘而不宣。”
留下这番切中要害的机锋，佳人翩然隐去，独留韦昌一人在心底对她八面玲珑的大智慧五体投地。
且说不多时王秦折返之际，他对这天地造化可委实提不起半点好脸色。恶霸方那步步紧逼的要挟，仍如鸩毒般腐蚀着这商贾的咽喉：若答允其苛求，右侧眼见商号无利可图而倒闭；若断然拒绝，左侧眼见工潮瘫痪而倾覆。
况且，一路上遇到的征兆亦乱七八糟、大逆不道。
在出府途中，他曾撞见一位老者，倚靠2株果树（两株果树）维系生计。王秦走近时，只见这位风烛残年的老汉正从草棚中端出2块捶打结实的干狗粪（两块狗粪），着手埋在那株较粗硕的大树根部。此举出自一个极可能是高人乔装之老者手中，登时激起了王秦的深切兴味。
“老人家，”他探询道，“既然手头有2块狗粪、2株果树，为何不一树分上一块，好让两树皆得滋养，待至青黄不接时皆能结实以济尊驾之急？”
“今岁年景眼看严酷艰难，极缺口粮，”老者答道，“若一树仅分一块，这点养料只怕哪一株也喂不饱，反倒致使双双枯死。若将度日生计缩减至极处，仅凭这一株大树的收成，老朽尚能勉强捱至下一茬收割；可若两株皆枯竭，老朽便死无葬身之地矣。为此，老朽但求保全其间更强的一株蓬勃生长，以保残生。”
“愿吉星护佑老人家心血！”王秦听罢心满意足，抚掌折返。
然而回程尚未走完一半，他的去路又被一位老妪阻隔。那老妪喂养着2只山羊（两只山羊），全靠羊乳维持性命免于饿死。她手头所能筹措者，唯有一小捧干枯的衰草，但她却将其均分给了两只山羊，结果两头牲口皆吃不饱，双双咩咩悲鸣，怨怼不已。

===Original===

“The season promises to be one of rigour and great need,” remarked Wong Ts’in affably, for the being before him might well be a creature of another part who had assumed that form for his guidance. “Why do you not therefore ensure sustenance to the better of the two goats by devoting to it the whole of the measure of dry grass? In this way you would receive at least some nourishment in return and thereby safeguard your own existence until the rice is grown again.”
“In the matter of the two goats,” replied the aged hag, “there is no better, both being equally stubborn and perverse, though one may be finer-looking and more vainglorious than the other. Yet should I foster this one to the detriment of her fellow, what would be this person’s plight if haply the weaker died and the stronger broke away and fled! By treating both alike I retain a double thread on life, even if neither is capable of much.”
“May the Unseen weigh your labours!” exclaimed Wong Ts’in in a two-edged voice, and he departed.
When he reached his own house he would have closed himself in his own chamber with himself had not Wei Chang persisted that he sought his master’s inner ear with a heavy project. This interruption did not please Wong Ts’in, for he had begun to recognize the day as being unlucky, yet Chang succeeded by a device in reaching his side, bearing in his hands a guarded burden.
Though no written record of this memorable interview exists, it is now generally admitted that Wei Chang either involved himself in an unbearably attenuated caution before he would reveal his errand, or else that he made a definite allusion to Fa Fai with a too sudden conciseness, for the slaves who stood without heard Wong Ts’in clear his voice of all restraint and express himself freely on a variety of subjects. But this gave place to a subdued murmur, ending with the ceremonial breaking of a plate, and later Wong Ts’in beat on a silver bell and called for wine and fruit.
The next day Fang presented himself a few gong-strokes later than the appointed time, and being met by an unbending word he withdrew the labour of those whom he controlled.

===Chinese===

“今岁年景眼看严酷艰难，极缺口粮，”王秦和颜悦色地搭讪道——因眼前之人极可能是另一方神明所化以点拨迷津，“老人家何不将那一整捧干草尽数喂饲其中较健硕的一只山羊以保其生计？如此至少能自其身上挤得些许羊乳糊口，以保全自身性命，捱至稻谷再熟之时？”
“提及这2只山羊，”老妪答道，“哪有什么强弱之分，皆是一般刁顽劣性，无非一只稍具卖相更爱卖弄罢了。若老身偏袒其一而饿死另一头，万一弱者死了，而强者挣脱绳索逃之夭夭，老身岂非竹篮打水一场空？唯有兼顾二者，老身在世上方能握有双重指望，纵使两头皆派不上大用场，亦胜过孤注一掷。”
“愿冥冥苍天称量尊驾之苦心！”王秦吐出一句语带双关的嗟叹，遂拂袖而去。
待回到府中，他本欲闭门谢客，独处内室，偏生韦昌执意求见，声称有重若千钧之大计面呈。王秦正觉今日运数不利，本不欲相见，奈何韦昌使了个机巧，捧着一包严密包裹之物闯至跟前。
尽管此番密谈并无片纸留存，然世人皆传，韦昌初时若非拐弯抹角过甚，便是将求娶法斐之意吐露得过于突兀，以致门外侍立之奴仆清晰听见王秦震怒之下破口大骂，诸般辞令喷薄而出。然而叫骂声旋即化作低声絮语，继而传来一声摔碎瓷盘之脆响；随后，王秦竟摇响了银铃，唤仆役速奉陈年美酒与果品上席。
次日，恶霸方迟到了数声锣方才大摇大摆登门，不料迎头遭了一通生硬训斥；恶霸遂当场率领全班人马撂挑子罢工。

===Original===

Thenceforth these men, providing themselves with knives and axes, surrounded the gate of the earth-yards and by the pacific argument of their attitudes succeeded in persuading others who would willingly have continued at their task that the air of Wong Ts’in’s sheds was not congenial to their health. Towards Wei Chang, whose efforts they despised, they raised a cloud of derision, and presently noticing that henceforth he invariably clad himself in lower garments of a dark blue material (to a set purpose that will be as crystal to the sagacious), they greeted his appearance with cries of: “Behold the sombre one! Thou dark leg!” so that this reproach continues to be hurled even to this day at those in a like case, though few could answer why.
Long before the stipulated time the tenscore plates were delivered to Hien Nan. So greatly were they esteemed, both on account of their accuracy of unvarying detail and the ingenuity of their novel embellishment, that orders for scores, hundreds and even thousands began to arrive from all quarters of the Empire. The clay enterprise of Wong Ts’in took upon itself an added lustre, and in order to deal adequately with so vast an undertaking the grateful merchant adopted Wei Chang and placed him upon an equal footing with himself. On the same day Wong Ts’in honourably fulfilled his spoken word and the marriage of Wei Chang and Fa Fai took place, accompanied by the most lavish display of fireworks and coloured lights that the province had ever seen. The controlling deities approved, and they had seven sons, one of whom had seven fingers upon each hand. All these sons became expert in Wei Chang’s process of transferring porcelain embellishment, for some centuries elapsed before it was discovered that it was not absolutely necessary to sit upon each plate to produce the desired effect.
This chronicle of an event that is now regarded as almost classical would not be complete without an added reference to the ultimate end of the sordid Fang.
Fallen into disrepute among his fellows owing to the evil plight towards which he had enticed them, it became his increasing purpose to frequent the house beyond the river. On his return at nightfall he invariably drew aside on reaching the bridge, well knowing that he could not prudently rely upon his feet among so insecure a crossing, and composed himself to sleep amid the rushes.

===Chinese===

自此而后，这伙人手持砍刀利斧，团团围住瓷土工棚的大门，借着这般“和颜悦色”的剑拔弩张阵势，成功“劝导”了其他原本乐于继续上工的匠人，使他们深信王秦棚内的空气大为不利于身心康泰。对于韦昌那点微末手艺，他们更是极尽嘲弄贬损之能事；不久之后，他们注意到韦昌从此必定穿着一身深青色下裳（其深意在明眼人看来自然如水晶般通透澄澈），便一见他露面便齐声鼓噪讥呼：“瞧那青皮乌腿！好一条黑腿！”以致此等斥骂即便流传至今，凡遇类似情境仍被好事者用来喝倒彩，尽管个中原委知之者已寥寥无几。
早在约定限期之前，200只（两百只）精美绝伦的瓷盘便已如期运抵贤南。因其图案纤毫不差、纹样精妙绝伦，名声大噪，以致天下四面八方的订单纷至沓来，动辄数十、数百、乃至数千只。王秦的瓷土商号自此如日中天，威震四海；而为了从容料理如此浩繁的买卖，这位感激涕零的东家毅然认韦昌为义子，使其平起平坐共掌大局。同日，王秦信守昔日诺言，为韦昌与法斐隆重完婚；大婚之日，全省燃放了有史以来最璀璨壮丽的焰火花灯。执掌姻缘的列祖列宗与神明皆垂青庇佑，二人共育有7个儿子（七子），其中一子每只手皆生有7指。所有儿子日后皆精通韦昌的瓷器转印绝技，因数百年后世人方才悟出，若要在每只瓷盘上拓出合意的图景，原本大可不必次次亲自坐上去。
这桩如今已被奉为经典的轶事若不交代恶霸方的最终下场，便难称圆满。
因率领同仁陷入绝境，方在画工中名声扫地，威信全无；他越发沉湎于流连江对岸的野肆借酒浇愁。每逢夜幕降临醉意熏熏而归，他行至桥头必横卧一旁——深知在如此险窄的无栏断桥上，自己的双脚实难托付，遂自行倒在芦苇丛中呼呼大睡。

===Original===

While in this position one night he was discovered and pushed into the river by a devout ox (an instrument of high destinies), where he perished incapably.
Those who found his body, not being able to withdraw so formidable a weight direct, cast a rope across the lower branch of a convenient willow-tree and thus raised it to the shore. In this striking manner Fa Fai’s definite opinion achieved a destined end.

===Chinese===

在一个这般的漆黑之夜，他倒卧在芦苇丛中，被一头虔敬的黄牛（天命的殊胜工具）一角顶落江中，就此窝囊溺毙。
待众人寻获其尸身，因其体态沉重难当，无法径直拖上岸来，便将麻绳抛过近旁一株垂柳的低枝，合力将其吊上岸滩。法斐当日那断然不移的论断，便以这般触目惊心的手段，达成了其注定的终局。

## III The Degraded Persistence of the Effete Ming-Shu / III 孱弱明恕之卑劣纠缠
===Original===

At about the same gong-stroke as before, Kai Lung again stood at the open shutter, and to him presently came the maiden Hwa-mei, bearing in her hands a gift of fruit.
“The story of the much-harassed merchant Wong Ts’in and of the assiduous youth Wei Chang has reached this person’s ears by a devious road, and though it doubtless lost some of the subtler qualities in the telling, the ultimate tragedy had a convincing tone,” she remarked pleasantly.
“It is scarcely to be expected that one who has spent his life beneath an official umbrella should have at his command the finer analogies of light and shade,” tolerantly replied Kai Lung. “Though by no means comparable with the unapproachable history of the Princess Taik and the minstrel Ch’eng as a means for conveying the unexpressed aspirations of the one who relates towards the one who is receptive, there are many passages even in the behaviour of Wei Chang into which this person could infuse an unmistakable stress of significance were he but given the opportunity.”
“The day of that opportunity has not yet dawned,” replied the Golden Mouse; “nor has the night preceding it yet run its gloomy course. Foiled in his first attempt, the vindictive Ming-shu now creeps towards his end by a more tortuous path. Whether or not dimly suspecting something of the strategy by which your imperishable life was preserved today, it is no part of his depraved scheme that you should be given a like opportunity again. Tomorrow another will be led to judgment, one Cho-kow, a tribesman of the barbarian land of Khim.”
“With him I have already conversed and shared rice,” interposed Kai Lung. “Proceed, elegance.”
“Accused of plundering mountain tombs and of other crimes now held in disrepute, he will be offered a comparatively painless death if he will implicate his fellows, of whom you will be held to be the chief. By this ignoble artifice you will be condemned on his testimony in your absence, nor will you have any warning of your fate until you are led forth to suffer.”
Then replied Kai Lung, after a space of thought: “Not ineptly is it written: ‘When the leading carriage is upset the next one is more careful,’ and Ming-shu has taken the proverb to his heart. To counteract his detestable plot will not be easy, but it should not be beyond our united power, backed by a reasonable activity on the part of our protecting ancestors.”

===Chinese===

约莫在前番同一记更锣敲响之际，凯龙（Kai Lung）再次伫立在敞开的木窗前。不多时，少女华梅（Hwa-mei）款款而至，手中托着一份作为心意的鲜果。
“那位备受纷扰的商人王秦与勤勉少年卫长的事迹，已由迂回途径传至小女子耳中。虽说辗转传述间不免损却了几分精妙韵味，然而最终那场悲局听来倒颇具令人信服的气势。”她笑语盈盈地说道。
“一个终其一生都在官伞庇荫下度日之人，自难指望其胸臆中藏有明暗交错的幽微对照之妙，”凯龙宽和地答道，“虽说以此向听者传达述者那未宣之情愫，绝难与泰克公主（Princess Taik）与乐师程（Ch’eng）那无可企及的传奇同日而语，然而即便在卫长的举止行藏中，亦有多处关节——只要给小人以机缘，定能注入令人心领神会的深意。”
“此番机缘降临之日尚未破晓，”金鼠（the Golden Mouse）答道，“而横亘在前的那漫漫长夜亦未行至尽头。怀恨在心的明恕（Ming-shu）首谋受挫，如今正循着一条更为阴鸷刁钻的歧途暗中逼近其企图。无论他是否已隐隐起疑，窥出了你今日保全这不灭性命的谋略，依其险恶心肠，断无容你重获此等良机之理。明日堂上将押解另一人受审，那人唤作巧寇，乃蛮荒禽地之部众。”
“小人已与他搭过话，并分食过米饭，”凯龙插言道，“佳人请接着讲。”
“他被指控盗掘山陵及其他为人不齿之罪愆；官府许以相对少受皮肉之苦的死法，诱其供出同伙，而你便将被指认为匪首。借由此等卑劣伎俩，你将在毫不知情的缺席境况下凭其口供被定罪，直至被押赴刑场受戮之际，方知大祸临头。”
凯龙沉吟片刻，应道：“古训有云：‘前车既覆，后车当诫’，实非虚言；明恕已然将此箴言铭记于心矣。若要破除其阴险图谋自非易事，然而集你我二人之力，辅以庇佑先祖之恰当护持，料想亦非人力所不及之事。”

===Original===

“The devotional side of the emergency has had this one’s early care,” remarked Hwa-mei. “From daybreak tomorrow six zealous and deep-throated monks will curse Ming-shu and all his ways unceasingly, while a like number will invoke blessings and success upon your enlightened head. In the matter of noise and illumination everything that can contribute has been suitably prepared.”
“It is difficult to conjecture what more could be done in that direction,” confessed Kai Lung gratefully.
“Yet as regards a more material effort﻿—?” suggested the maiden, amid a cloud of involving doubt.
“If there is a subject in which the imagination of the Mandarin Shan Tien can be again enmeshed it might be yet accomplished,” replied Kai Lung. “Have you a knowledge of any such deep concern?”
“Truly there is a matter that disturbs his peace of late. He has dreamed a dream three times, and its meaning is beyond the skill of any man to solve. Yet how shall this avail you who are no geomancer?”
“What is the nature of the dream?” inquired Kai Lung. “For remember, ‘Though Shen-fi has but one gate, many roads lead to it.’ ”
“The substance of the dream is this: that herein he who sleeps walks freely in the ways of men wearing no robe or covering of any kind, yet suffering no concern or indignity therefrom; that the secret and hidden things of the earth are revealed to his seeing eyes; and that he can float in space and project himself upon the air at will. These three things are alien to his nature, and being three times repeated, the uncertainty assails his ease.”
“Let it, under your persistent care, assail him more and that unceasingly,” exclaimed Kai Lung, with renewed lightness in his voice. “Breathe on the surface of his self-repose as a summer breeze moves the smooth water of a mountain lake﻿—not deeply, but never quite at rest. Be assured: it is no longer possible to doubt that powerful Beings are interested in our cause.”
“I go, oppressed one,” replied Hwa-mei. “May this period of your ignoble trial be brought to a distinguished close.”
On the following day at the appointed hour Cho-kow was led before the Mandarin Shan Tien, and the nature of his crimes having been explained to him by the contemptible Ming-shu, he was bidden to implicate Kai Lung and thus come to an earlier and less painful end.

===Chinese===

“此番危局在祈愿禳灾一面，小女子早已作下绸缪，”华梅说道，“自明日拂晓起，便有6位嗓门洪亮、虔诚恳切的僧人不住咒诅明恕及其一切做派，同时另有6位同样数目的高僧为你那开明头脑祈求福佑与亨通。至于钟鼓梵呗与香烛灯火，凡能助势之物，皆已妥帖备齐。”
“在这一桩事上，委实难以想象还能有何更周全的操持了，”凯龙由衷感激地坦承道。
“然而若论更切实际之筹谋……？”少女在一阵疑虑迷雾中探询道。
“若能寻得某一桩事物，足以再度将善天（Shan Tien）县令的心神思绪羁绊其中，此事便大有可为，”凯龙答道，“佳人可知晓有何等深切之事足以乱其心曲？”
“近来的确有一桩事扰他神安。他已连续3次做同一个怪梦，其梦兆之意纵是能人异士亦莫能参破。然而你并非风水堪舆之士，这于你又有何裨益？”
“那是怎样一个梦境？”凯龙探询道，“切记，‘申飞之城虽只辟一门，通途却有千万条’。”
“梦境大略如此：梦中寝卧之人赤身裸体漫步于众目睽睽之途，寸丝不挂，却丝毫不觉难堪屈辱；大地深处秘而不宣之幽隐万象，皆尽收其明澈眼底；且能凌空虚渡，任意穿行于烟霞苍穹之间。这3件事全然悖逆其寻常天性，复又接连3次入梦，疑惧难定遂大损其安适。”
“在你的着意料理下，且教这份疑惧更深地纠缠于他，休教止息，”凯龙嗓音中重又透出轻快，“轻拂其安逸心境，宛若夏日微风吹皱高山湖泊之澄澈碧水一般——入水虽浅，却教波澜难平。切莫忧心：如今已断无可疑，冥冥中定有神通广大的神明正垂青庇佑于你我之事。”
“小女子这便告退，蒙冤之人，”华梅答道，“愿你这番卑微磨难早日以光彩之局收场。”
翌日到了既定时辰，巧寇被押解至善天县令阶前。卑劣的明恕向其陈明所犯罪状后，便命其攀诬指认凯龙，以换取提早且少受痛苦的痛快死法。

===Original===

“All-powerful,” he replied, addressing himself to the Mandarin, “the words that have been spoken are bent to a deceptive end. They of our community are a simple race and doubtless in the past their ways were thus and thus. But, as it is truly said, ‘Tian went bare, his eyes could pierce the earth and his body float in space, but they of his seed do but dream the dream.’ We, being but the puny descendants﻿—”
“You have spoken of one Tian whose attributes were such, and of those who dream thereof,” interrupted the Mandarin, as one who performs a reluctant duty. “That which you adduce to uphold your cause must bear the full light of day.”
“Alas, omnipotence,” replied Cho-kow, “this concerns the doing of the gods and those who share their line. Now I am but an ill-conditioned outcast from the obscure land of Khim, and possess no lore beyond what happens there. Haply the gods that rule in Khim have a different manner of behaving from those in the Upper Air above Yu-ping, and this person’s narration would avoid the semblance of the things that are and he himself would thereby be brought to disrepute.”
“Suffer not that apprehension to retard your impending eloquence,” replied Shan Tien affably. “Be assured that the gods have exactly the same manner of behaving in every land.”
“Furthermore,” continued Cho-kow, with patient craft, “I am a man of barbarian tongue, the full half of my speech being foreign to your ear. The history of the much-accomplished Tian and the meaning of the dreams that mark those of his race require for a full understanding the subtle analogies of an acquired style. Now that same Kai Lung whom you have implicated to my band﻿—”
“Excellence!” protested Ming-shu, with a sudden apprehension in his throat, “yesterday our labours dissolved in air through the very doubtful precedent of allowing one to testify what he had had the intention to relate. Now we are asked to allow a tomb-haunter to call a parricide to disclose that which he himself is ignorant of. Press down your autocratic thumb﻿—”
“Alas, instructor,” interposed Shan Tien compassionately, “the sympathetic concern of my mind overflows upon the spectacle of your ill-used forbearance, yet you having banded together the two in a common infamy, it is the ancient privilege of this one to call the other to his cause.

===Chinese===

“青天大老爷，”巧寇面朝县令答道，“方才所言之辞，皆是用心险恶之圈套。我等部族族民生性朴拙，昔年行径固然多有这般那般。然而诚如古语所云：‘昔者田（Tian）赤身露体，目穿九地，身翔太虚，然其苗裔后人，不过徒留此梦而已。’我等不过是些微末子孙……”
“你方才提及一位名唤田之人，具有这般异能，其后世族人亦常做此梦，”县令打断道，神色宛若在勉为其难克尽职守，“凡你引以为自身抗辩之据，必须昭然公布于青天白日之下。”
“启禀大老爷，”巧寇答道，“此事牵涉神明行迹及其血脉苗裔。小人不过是禽地蛮荒僻壤中一个命途乖蹇之弃民，除却本地见闻别无长识。想来执掌禽地之神明，其行止派头或与高居榆平（Yu-ping）上方穹苍之神祇大相径庭，小人此番叙述恐有悖常理实情，反倒累及自身名声受损。”
“休教这等顾虑迟滞了你将发之宏辩，”善天和颜悦色地应道，“大可宽心，诸神在普天之下各方水土，其行止脾性皆一般无二。”
“再者，”巧寇运起耐性的智巧续道，“小人乃口操蛮夷乡谈之人，满嘴言语足有半数入不得老爷尊耳。若要透彻领会博学多能的田之往事，及其同族后裔异梦之底蕴，非得仰赖深谙文墨之士以幽微譬喻敷衍方可。如今蒙老爷将那位凯龙指为小人同党……”
“大老爷！”明恕喉头一紧，慌忙急切抗辩道，“昨日因开下前例，容犯人供述其原拟开讲之言，已教我等一番辛劳尽付东流。如今竟又要容许一个掘墓之徒召唤弑亲逆伦之犯，代为吐露其自身懵然无知之事！伏乞大老爷当机立断，落以雷霆断决……”
“哎呀，记室先生，”善天满含同情地插言道，“目睹你那屡受辜负的克制隐忍，本官心中亦不胜怜惜眷顾之意；然而既然是你将二人纠集为一丘之貉，依古制旧理，此人便有名正言顺之特权唤另一人为其佐证。

===Original===

We are but the feeble mouthpieces of a benevolent scheme of all-embracing justice and greatly do I fear that we must again submit.”
With these well-timed words the broad-minded personage settled himself more reposefully among his cushions and signified that Kai Lung should be led forward and begin.

===Chinese===

我等不过是那普被万物、宏阔公正之慈悲大局下微不足道的喉舌罢了，本官深恐今日又得委曲成全了。”
吐露了这番恰逢其时的言辞后，这位心胸开阔的尊贵大人更为从容地倚入锦垫深处，示意将凯龙押至堂前，即刻开讲。

### The Story of Ning, the Captive God, and the Dreams That Mark His Race / 被囚之神宁与其后裔之异梦
===Original===



===Chinese===

本篇记述被囚之神宁与其后人梦境之因由。

## I The Malice of the Demon, Leou / I 恶魔娄之怨毒
===Original===

When Sun Wei definitely understood that the deities were against him (for on every occasion his enemies prospered and the voice of his own authority grew less), he looked this way and that with a well-considering mind.
He did nothing hastily, but when once a decision was reached it was as unbending as iron and as smoothly finished as polished jade. At about the evening hour when others were preparing to offer sacrifice he took the images and the altars of his Rites down from their honourable positions and cast them into a heap on a waste expanse beyond his courtyard. Then with an axe he unceremoniously detached their incomparable limbs from their sublime bodies and flung the parts into a fire that he had prepared.
“It is better,” declared Sun Wei, standing beside the pile, his hands buried within his sleeves﻿—“it is better to be struck down at once, rather than to wither away slowly like a half-uprooted cassia-tree.”
When this act of defiance was reported in the Upper World the air grew thick with the cries of indignation of the lesser deities, and the sound of their passage as they projected themselves across vast regions of space and into the presence of the supreme N’guk was like the continuous rending of innumerable pieces of the finest silk.
In his musk-scented heaven, however, N’guk slept, as his habit was at the close of each celestial day. It was with some difficulty that he could be aroused and made to understand the nature of Sun Wei’s profanity, for his mind was dull with the smoke of never-ending incense.
“Tomorrow,” he promised, with a benignant gesture, turning over again on his crystal throne, “some time tomorrow impartial justice shall be done. In the meanwhile﻿—courteous dismissal attend your opportune footsteps.”
“He is becoming old and obese,” murmured the less respectful of the demons. “He is not the god he was, even ten thousand cycles ago. It were well﻿—”
“But, omnipotence,” protested certain conciliatory spirits, pressing to the front, “consider, if but for a short breath of time. A day here is as threescore of their years as these mortals live. By tomorrow night not only Sun Wei, but most of those now dwelling down below, will have Passed Beyond. But the story of his unpunished infamy will live. We shall become discredited and our altar fires extinct. Sacrifice of either food or raiment will cease to reach us.

===Chinese===

当孙威（Sun Wei）切实体会到神明正与他作对之时（因每次仇敌皆亨通得势，而他自身的威信却日渐式微），他满腹思量，左顾右盼。
他行事向来不急不躁，可一旦拿定主意，便如生铁般难以扭转，又似温玉般圆熟周详。约莫在黄昏众人筹备祭祀的时分，他将堂上供奉的仪轨神像与祭坛从尊位上撤下，尽数弃置于院外荒野废墟之上。随后，他挥起一把利斧，不由分说地斩落神像那无与伦比的四肢与至高无上的躯干，并将残块抛入预先升起的烈火之中。
“与其似半株拔根之桂树般慢慢枯萎，”孙威伫立在火堆旁，双手笼在袖中，朗声宣称，“倒不如立刻被天雷击毙来得痛快。”
这番逆天抗上之悖逆行径传至上界，众小神的义愤呐喊登时充斥天宇；他们穿越茫茫太虚、疾驰至至高天神恩古克（N’guk）驾前带起的呼啸声，恰如无数匹上好锦缎被持续撕扯碎裂一般。
然而在那弥漫着麝香香气的重霄之上，恩古克正沉沉入睡——这向来是他度过每个天界白昼后的宿习。众神好不容易才将他唤醒，并使之领会孙威犯下的渎神重罪，因其神智早被那无休止的缭绕香烟熏得昏沉迟滞。
“明日罢，”他在水晶宝座上翻了个身，带着慈祥的手势许诺道，“明日某个时辰，自会降下不偏不倚之公道。眼下——且容诸位适时退步，各安其位。”
“他老态龙钟，身形痴肥了，”一些不够敬畏的凶神恶煞咕哝道，“他已不再是神威赫赫的天主了，哪怕倒退10000个劫波之前亦非如此。我看莫如……”
“万能的天尊啊，”若干善意周旋的灵官抢步向前，抗辩道，“伏乞略加思忖片刻。上界之一日，下界凡尘已流转60年之久。待到明晚，不仅孙威早已下世，下界大半生灵亦将早已归天。然而其未遭惩戒之恶名却将长存世间。届时我等将威信扫地，坛前香火亦将断绝。不论血食供飨抑或锦绣衣冠，皆再难奉至座前。

===Original===

The Season of White Rain is approaching and will find us ill provided. We who speak are but Beings of small part﻿—”
“Peace!” commanded N’guk, now thoroughly disturbed, for the voices of the few had grown into a tumult; “how is it possible to consider with a torrent like the Hoang-Ho in flood pouring through my very ordinary ears? Your omniscient but quite inadequate Chief would think.”
At this rebuke the uproar ceased. So deep became the nature of N’guk’s profound thoughts that they could be heard rolling like thunder among the caverns of his gigantic brain. To aid the process, female slaves on either side fanned his fiery head with celestial lotus leaves. On the earth, far beneath, cyclones, sandstorms and sweeping waterspouts were forced into being.
“Hear the contemptible wisdom of my ill-formed mouth,” said N’guk at length. “If we at once put forth our strength, the degraded Wun Sei is ground﻿—”
“Sun Wei, All-knowing One,” murmured an attending spirit beneath his breath.
“﻿—the unmentionable outcast whom we are discussing is immediately ground into powder,” continued the Highest, looking fixedly at a distant spot situated directly beyond his painstaking attendant. “But what follows? Henceforth no man can be allowed to whisper ill of us but we must at once seek him out and destroy him, or the obtuse and superficial will exclaim: ‘It was not so in the days of﻿—of So-and-So. Behold’ ”﻿—here the Great One bent a look of sudden resentment on the band of those who would have reproached him﻿—“ ‘behold the gods become old and obese. They are not the Powers they were. It would be better to address ourselves to other altars.’ ”
At this prospect many of the more venerable spirits began to lose their enthusiasm. If every mortal who spoke ill of them was to be pursued what leisure for dignified seclusion would remain?
“If, however,” continued the dispassionate Being, “the profaner is left to himself he will, sooner or later, in the ordinary course of human intelligence, become involved in some disaster of his own contriving. Then they who dwell around will say: ‘He destroyed the alters! Truly the hands of the Unseen are slow to close, but their arms are very long. Lo, we have this day ourselves beheld it. Come, let us burn incense lest some forgotten misdeed from the past lurk in our path.’

===Chinese===

白雨之季（Season of White Rain）接踵而至，届时我等势必衣食维艰。我等发言者不过是些微末仙官……”
“肃静！”恩古克喝令道，此时他已颇感烦扰，因少许细语已然演变成一片喧哗；“耳畔奔涌着宛若黄河决堤般的嘈杂巨浪，叫本尊如何凝神裁度？尔等无所不知却毫无济于事的主宰，须得静思一番。”
遭此斥责，鼎沸人声骤然止息。恩古克玄妙的思绪沉浸得如此之深，以致在其巨大脑海洞穴深处，回荡起宛若滚滚春雷般的轰鸣。为助其运思，左右两侧的女侍以天界荷叶制成的羽扇，轻摇拂掠其炽烈的天庭。在极深极远的下界凡尘，旋风、沙暴与通天水龙卷顿受感召，应运而生。
“且听本尊拙劣之口所吐露的鄙陋之智罢，”恩古克良久方道，“倘若我等立刻施展无上法力，将那个卑劣的温赛立时碾成……”
“启禀全知天尊，那人唤作孙威，”一位伺候在旁的灵官屏息低声提醒道。
“……将我们所议论的那位不足挂齿的弃民立时碾成齑粉，”至高天神凝视着侍臣身后极远处的一点，续道，“然而其后又将如何？从此但凡有人私下非议我等，我等便须立时前去索命诛灭；否则那些冥顽浅薄之徒便会嚷嚷：‘想当年某某在位之时，可不是这般光景。瞧瞧吧’——说到此处，这位大神忽向方才埋怨他的那帮神灵投去怨怼的一瞥——‘瞧瞧诸神吧，又老又肥，早无昔日神威了。不如改换门庭，去拜别的神坛罢了。’”
思及此等前景，许多年高德劭的神官顿时心灰意懒。倘若每一个口出妄言的凡夫俗子皆须一一降罪追索，那神仙享有的体面清修之乐又安在哉？
“然而，”这位不动声色的至尊续道，“若任由那渎神者自生自灭，依凡人寻常智识之常情，他早晚必将作茧自缚，自取灭亡。届时环居四周之人便会叹道：‘是他亲手毁了神坛啊！隐秘神明之巨手握拢虽迟，其臂膀却极长。看呐，今日我等亲眼得见。快些焚香献祭罢，免得往昔被遗忘的罪愆暗中横阻我等去路。’

===Original===

”
When he had finished speaking all the more reputable of those present extolled his judgment. Some still whispered together, however, whereupon the sagacious N’guk opened his mouth more fully and shot forth tongues of consuming fire among the murmurers so that they fled howling from his presence.
Now among the spirits who had stood before the Pearly Ruler without taking any share in the decision were two who at this point are drawn into the narration, Leou and Ning. Leou was a revengeful demon, ever at enmity with one or another of the gods and striving how he might enmesh his feet in destruction. Ning was a better-class deity, voluptuous but well-meaning, and little able to cope with Leou’s subtlety. Thus it came about that the latter one, seeing in the outcome a chance to achieve his end, at once dropped headlong down to earth and sought out Sun Wei.
Sun Wei was reclining at his evening rice when Leou found him. Becoming invisible, the demon entered a date that Sun Wei held in his hand and took the form of a stone. Sun Wei recognized the doubtful nature of the stone as it passed between his teeth, and he would have spat it forth again, but Leou had the questionable agility of the serpent and slipped down the other’s throat. He was thus able to converse familiarly with Sun Wei without fear of interruption.
“Sun Wei,” said the voice of Leou inwardly, “the position you have chosen is a desperate one, and we of the Upper Air who are well disposed towards you find the path of assistance fringed with two-edged swords.”
“It is well said: ‘He who lacks a single tael sees many bargains,’ ” replied Sun Wei, a refined bitterness weighing the import of his words. “Truly this person’s friends in the Upper Air are a never-failing lantern behind his back.”
At this justly-barbed reproach Leou began to shake with disturbed gravity until he remembered that the motion might not be pleasing to Sun Wei’s inner feelings.
“It is not that the well-disposed are slow to urge your claims, but that your enemies number some of the most influential demons in all the Nine Spaces,” he declared, speaking with a false smoothness that marked all his detestable plans. “Assuredly in the past you must have led a very abandoned life, Sun Wei, to come within the circle of their malignity.”
“By no means,” replied Sun Wei.

===Chinese===

”
待至尊言毕，在场一应体面仙班皆交口称颂其圣明决断。然而仍有少数神明暗自交头接耳，明察秋毫的恩古克遂将巨口张得更阔，朝那群窃窃私语者喷出烈焰火舌，吓得他们狂嚎逃离宝座。
当时侍立在玉皇案前而未参与决断的诸神中，有两位自此刻起被卷入此番传奇，那便是娄（Leou）与宁（Ning）。娄乃一尊睚眦必报之凶神，常与各路仙真结怨，时刻图谋陷害诸神堕入绝境。宁则属上品仙真，性喜安逸声色却心怀善意，全然不是刁钻狡黠的娄之对手。于是，后者窥见个中机锋，深知可借此达成自身私欲，当即头下脚上直坠凡尘，寻访孙威而去。
娄寻得孙威之时，孙威正斜倚几旁享用晚膳米饭。这恶魔化为无形之气，钻入孙威手捏的一枚红枣之中，化作一颗枣核。孙威齿尖咬触之际便察觉这枚枣核形态可疑，正欲将其啐出，奈何娄兼具长蛇那刁钻之灵动，刺溜一下便滑入喉咙，咽下肚去。借此手段，恶魔得以与孙威倾心私语，而全无走漏风声之虞。
“孙威啊，”娄在腹中发声道，“你所选的乃是一条绝路；上界之中眷顾于你的神明，深觉援手之途布满了双刃利刃。”
“俗话说得好：‘荷包缺了一两银，眼里尽是俏便宜’，”孙威答道，话音中透出一股幽微冷峭的怨艾，“上界那些待在下友善的神真，委实是在下身后一盏永不熄灭的照路明灯呐。”
面对这番恰中要害的带刺冷嘲，娄禁不住一阵局促颤动，直至记起腹内震颤恐有伤孙威脾胃，方才勉强克制。
“并非眷顾你的神明迟迟不肯替你申诉，实乃你的对头囊括了九天（Nine Spaces）之中几位权势熏天的凶神，”他巧舌如簧地分辩道，字里行间透着其所有阴谋所独有的虚伪温存，“昔日你定是作恶多端、荒淫无度，孙威，方会招惹他们这般深仇大恨。”
“断无此事，”孙威答道。

===Original===

“Until driven to despair this person not only duly observed the Rites and Ceremonies, but he even avoided the Six Offences. He remained by the side of his parents while they lived, provided an adequate posterity, forbore to tread on any of the benevolent insects, safeguarded all printed paper, did not consume the meat of the industrious ox, and was charitable towards the needs of hungry and homeless ghosts.”
“These observances are well enough,” admitted Leou, restraining his narrow-minded impatience; “and with an ordinary number of written charms worn about the head and body they would doubtless carry you through the lesser contingencies of existence. But by, as it were, extending contempt, you have invited the retaliatory propulsion of the sandal of authority.”
“To one who has been pushed over the edge of a precipice, a rut across the path is devoid of menace; nor do the destitute tremble at the departing watchman’s cry: ‘Sleep warily; robbers are about.’ ”
“As regards bodily suffering and material extortion, it is possible to attain such a limit as no longer to excite the cupidity of even the most rapacious deity,” admitted Leou. “Other forms of flattening-out a transgressor’s self-content remain however. For instance, it has come within the knowledge of the controlling Powers that seven generations of your distinguished ancestors occupy positions of dignified seclusion in the Upper Air.”
For the first time Sun Wei’s attitude was not entirely devoid of an emotion of concern.
“They would not﻿—?”
“To mark their sense of your really unsupportable behaviour it has been decided that all seven shall return to the humiliating scenes of their former existences in admittedly objectionable forms,” replied the outrageous Leou. “Sun Chen, your venerated sire, will become an agile grasshopper; your incomparable grandfather, Yuen, will have the similitude of a yellow goat; as a tortoise your leisurely-minded ancestor Huang, the high public official﻿—”
“Forbear!” exclaimed the conscience-stricken Sun Wei; “rather would this person suffer every imaginable form of torture than that the spirit of one of his revered ancestors should be submitted to so intolerable a bondage. Is there no amiable form of compromise whereby the ancestors of some less devoted and liberally-inspired son might be imperceptibly, as it were, substituted?”

===Chinese===

“在被逼入绝境之前，在下不仅恪遵礼制仪轨，甚至对六大过（Six Offences）避之不及。父母在世之时，在下常伴身侧尽孝；繁衍子嗣香火不绝；从不踩踏任何益虫；敬惜一切字纸；绝不食用辛劳耕牛之肉；更兼周济饥馁无告之孤魂野鬼。”
“此等恪守固然尚可，”娄按捺住心胸狭隘的不耐烦，承认道，“若再于周身头额佩带寻常数目的护身符箓，保你在寻常微末凶险中逢凶化吉自是不在话下。然而你此番公然藐视神威，无异于引火烧身，自招官家履底那无情一脚。”
“对于一个已被推落万丈悬崖之人而言，横在路上的车辙早无威胁可言；而家徒四壁之赤贫，更不会在打更人离去时那声‘当心门户，谨防盗贼’的吆喝下战栗。”
“若论肉体苦痛与资财剥夺，受难之人确能达到令最贪得无厌之神明亦提不起兴味的境地，”娄承认道，“然而，消磨平抑作恶者自负心性的手段尚有千百种。譬如，执掌乾坤的大能已然查明，你家7代显赫先祖如今正在上界安享清修之福。”
孙威的神态中头一次浮现出真切的不安之色。
“他们该不会……？”
“为彰显尔之恶行委实不可容忍，天界已然决议，命你家7代先祖尽数重返前尘屈辱之境，沦为公认之卑贱形骸，”狂悖的娄答道，“你那位受人景仰的严父孙琛（Sun Chen），将化作一只蹦跳的蚂蚱；你那举世无双的祖父渊（Yuen），将形同黄毛野羊；至于你那位生性闲雅、曾官居要津的先祖璜（Huang），则将化为一只老鳖……”
“住口！”深感天良受谴的孙威疾呼道，“在下宁愿领受世间万般酷刑，亦不愿尊祖之神魂蒙受如此奇耻大辱之羁绊！难道便无某种委婉周旋的通融之法，可悄无声息地将某个孝心欠缺、少受德化之逆子的先祖暗中调包么？”

===Original===

“In ordinary cases some such arrangement is generally possible,” conceded Leou; “but not idly is it written: ‘There is a time to silence an adversary with the honey of logical persuasion, and there is a time to silence him with the argument of a heavily-directed club.’ In your extremity a hostage is the only efficient safeguard. Seize the person of one of the gods themselves and raise a strong wall around your destiny by holding him to ransom.”
“ ‘Ho Tai, requiring a light for his pipe, stretched out his hand towards the great sky-lantern,’ ” quoted Sun Wei.
“ ‘Do not despise Ching To because his armour is invisible,’ ” retorted Leou, with equal point. “Your friends in the Above are neither feeble nor inept. Do as I shall instruct you and no less a Being than Ning will be delivered into your hand.”
Then replied Sun Wei dubiously: “A spreading mango-tree affords a pleasant shade within one’s courtyard, and a captive god might for a season undoubtedly confer an enviable distinction. But presently the tree’s encroaching roots may disturb the foundation of the house so that the walls fall and crush those who are within, and the head of a restrained god would in the end certainly displace my very inadequate rooftree.”
“A too-prolific root can be pruned back,” replied Leou, “and the activities of a bondaged god may be efficiently curtailed. How this shall be accomplished will be revealed to you in a dream: take heed that you do not fail by the deviation of a single hair.”
Having thus prepared his discreditable plot, Leou twice struck the walls enclosing him, so that Sun Wei coughed violently. The demon was thereby enabled to escape, and he never actually appeared in a tangible form again, although he frequently communicated, by means of signs and omens, with those whom he wished to involve in his sinister designs.

===Chinese===

“依寻常之例，此等通融倒也并非绝无可能，”娄让步道，“然而古训有云：‘有时当以巧簧蜜语折服辩者，有时则当以沉重巨棒使之噤声’，实非虚言。在尔这等绝境之中，唯有人质方为唯一克敌之保障。尔当亲手擒拿一位神明真身，以此扣为人质勒索赎金，方能在命运周围筑起铜墙铁壁。”
“‘何泰（Ho Tai）欲引火点烟袋，竟向九天皓月伸手’，”孙威引谚讽道。
“‘切莫因荆拓（Ching To）铠甲隐形而小觑其威’，”娄针锋相对反唇相讥，“尔在天界之友人既非软弱之辈，亦非无能之徒。且遵我吩咐行事，届时尊贵如宁者，亦必落入尔掌握之中。”
孙威将信将疑地答道：“庭院中有一株繁茂的芒果树固然可纳凉荫蔽，而俘获一尊神明更可在一段时日内博得令人艳羡之殊荣。然而时日一久，树根滋蔓侵蚀屋基，势必致使墙倾屋塌，压毙居于其间之人；而拘押一尊神明的头颅，终将掀翻在下这微薄寒舍之屋脊。”
“滋蔓过盛之根须自当修剪削抑，”娄答道，“而受制之神祇其神通亦能予以有效扼制。如何行事，稍后自会于梦境之中向尔昭示：切记依计而行，不得有毫厘之差。”
布下这番见不得人的阴谋诡计后，娄在禁锢他的肚腹四壁上连击两记，引得孙威剧烈咳嗽不止。恶魔借机脱身逃遁，此后便再未以真身显形，然而他却时常借助诸般征兆与凶兆，暗中向那些他欲拖入险恶图谋之人传达授意。

## II The Part Played by the Slave-Girl, Hia / II 奴隶少女夏所为之事
===Original===

Among the remaining possessions that the hostility of the deities still left to Sun Wei at the time of these happenings was a young slave of many-sided attraction. The name of Hia had been given to her, but she was generally known as Tsing-ai on account of the extremely affectionate gladness of her nature.
On the day following that in which Sun Wei and the demon Leou had conversed together, Hia was disporting herself in the dark shades of a secluded pool, as her custom was after the heat of her labours, when a phoenix, flying across the glade, dropped a pearl of unusual size and lustre into the stream. Possessing herself of the jewel and placing it in her mouth, so that it should not impede the action of her hands, Hia sought the bank and would have drawn herself up when she became aware of the presence of one having the guise of a noble commander. He was regarding her with a look in which well-expressed admiration was blended with a delicate intimation that owing to the unparalleled brilliance of her eyes he was unable to perceive any other detail of her appearance, and was, indeed, under the impression that she was devoid of ordinary outline. At the same time, without permitting her glance to be in any but an entirely opposite direction, Hia was able to satisfy herself that the stranger was a person on whom she might prudently lavish the full depths of her regard if the necessity arose. His apparel was rich, voluminous and of colours then unknown within the Empire; his hair long and abundant; his face placid but sincere. He carried no weapons, but wherever he trod there came a yellow flame from below his right foot and a white vapour from beneath his left. His insignia were those of a royal prince, and when he spoke his voice resembled the noise of arrows passing through the upper branches of a prickly forest. His long and pointed nails indicated the high and dignified nature of all his occupations; each nail was protected by a solid sheath, there being amethyst, ruby, topaz, ivory, emerald, white jade, iron, chalcedony, gold and malachite.

===Chinese===

在这诸般变故发生之时，神明的敌意留给孙威的残存家财之中，尚有一名多才多艺、风姿绰约的年轻女奴。她被起名为夏（Hia），然因其性情极尽温柔欢悦，众人通常唤她作“情爱”（Tsing-ai）。
就在孙威与恶魔娄交谈的次日，夏如往常劳作发热后那般，在一处僻静幽深的水潭阴凉处戏水纳凉；忽有一只凤凰振翼掠过林间空地，将一颗硕大圆润、光华夺目的珍珠坠入溪流之中。夏拾得这枚明珠衔入口中，以免阻碍双手的动作；她正欲攀上水岸，忽惊觉岸上不知何时伫立着一位形貌宛如尊贵统帅之人。那人正凝眸注视着她，目光中流露出极尽赞赏之色，又含蓄地暗示因其双眸之绝世光华过于耀眼夺目，以致他无法看清她身形样貌的任何其余细处，甚至觉得她仿佛超脱了凡俗轮廓一般。与此同时，夏虽始终将目光投向全然相反的方向，却也暗中打量分明：若有必要，这位陌生贵客实乃一位值得她审慎倾注全部眷顾之人。其衣着华美宽袍大袖，色泽之瑰丽乃是帝国境内所未曾见闻；发丝丰盈修长；面容沉静而诚挚。他身无寸铁，然而凡其足迹所至，右足下生出一团金黄烈焰，左足下涌出一缕洁白烟霞。其仪仗徽记皆具王公之尊，开口言语之际，声如利箭穿过荆棘密林树梢之飒飒声。修长尖锐的指甲彰显出其一应起居作为皆极尽高贵尊荣；每片指甲皆覆有一枚坚实护套，计有紫晶、红宝、黄玉、象牙、翡翠、白玉、玄铁、玛瑙、黄金与孔雀石十样异宝。

===Original===

When the distinguished-looking personage had thus regarded Hia for some moments he drew an instrument of hollow tubes from a fold of his garment and began to sing of two who, as the outcome of a romantic encounter similar to that then existing, had professed an agreeable attachment for one another and had, without unnecessary delay, entered upon a period of incomparable felicity. Doubtless Hia would have uttered words of high-minded rebuke at some of the more detailed analogies of the recital had not the pearl deprived her of the power of expressing herself clearly on any subject whatever, nor did it seem practicable to her to remove it without withdrawing her hands from the modest attitudes into which she had at once distributed them. Thus positioned, she was compelled to listen to the stranger’s well-considered flattery, and this (together with the increasing coldness of the stream as the evening deepened) convincingly explains her ultimate acquiescence to his questionable offers.
Yet it cannot be denied that Ning (as he may now fittingly be revealed) conducted the enterprise with a seemly liberality; for upon receiving from Hia a glance not expressive of discouragement he at once caused the appearance of a suitably-furnished tent, a train of Nubian slaves offering rich viands, rare wine and costly perfumes, companies of expert dancers and musicians, a retinue of discreet elderly women to robe her and to attend her movements, a carpet of golden silk stretching from the water’s edge to the tent, and all the accessories of a high-class profligacy.
When the night was advanced and Hia and Ning, after partaking of a many-coursed feast, were reclining on an ebony couch, the Being freely expressed the delight that he discovered in her amiable society, incautiously adding: “Demand any recompense that is within the power of this one to grant, O most delectable of water-nymphs, and its accomplishment will be written by a flash of lightning.” In this, however, he merely spoke as the treacherous Leou (who had enticed him into the adventure) had assured him was usual in similar circumstances, he himself being privately of the opinion that the expenditure already incurred was more than adequate to the occasion.
Then replied Hia, as she had been fully instructed against the emergency: “The word has been spoken.

===Chinese===

那位仪表堂堂的贵客这般端详了夏片刻之后，自衣褶中取出一管竹管排箫，悠然唱起了一段曲调：唱的是两人因一场与眼下如出一辙的浪漫邂逅而互生缱绻情愫，且毫不延误地就此迈入一段无与伦比之欢悦时光。倘若口中那颗珍珠未曾剥夺她清晰表述任何言辞的权能，夏定然早已针对唱词中若干过于详尽的譬喻吐露高风亮节的斥责了；况且若不将双手从此刻所摆出的端庄守礼姿态中撤回，她便无法将珍珠取出。困于此等情境，她只得任由这位陌客那字斟句酌的阿谀之辞灌入耳中；而这一点（连同夜幕渐深后溪水刺骨的寒意），便足以令人信服地解释她最终为何默许了他那些居心叵测的殷勤邀约。
然而无可否认的是，宁（Ning，此刻不妨适时点明其真身）经营这场艳遇的手笔委实极具体面的豪阔风范；因自夏眸中捕捉到一丝并未流露阻拒之意的顾盼后，他便立时施法变出一座陈设精绝的帐幔，一队奉上珍馐美味、醇醪陈酿与名贵香氛的黑奴，数班技艺卓绝的歌姬舞女，一行服侍她更衣并伺候其起居的知礼老妇，一幅自水际直铺入帐中的金丝织锦地毯，以及豪门放浪奢靡所需的一切行头器皿。
及至夜深，夏与宁享用过无数道珍馐佳馔之后，正并肩斜倚在乌木榻上；这位神尊畅快抒发着自佳人温存陪伴中所获之欢愉，不慎脱口许诺道：“但凡在在下权能所及之列，任凭尔求取何等重赏，水中最可人的仙子啊，此愿必借一道闪电之势顷刻写就。”然而，他此番言语不过是因循奸诈的娄（正是此魔引诱他涉足这场艳遇）之保证，以为在类似情境下此乃寻常套话罢了；其内心私下里却认定，方才所耗费的排场铺张早已绰绰有余、远超规格了。
夏早受人指点如何应对此番变故，遂应道：“君子一言，驷马难追。

===Original===

But what is precious metal after listening to the pure gold of thy lips, or who shall again esteem gems while gazing upon the full round radiance of thy moon-like face? One thing only remains: remove the various sheaths from off thy hands, for they not only conceal the undoubted perfection of the nails within, but their massive angularity renders the affectionate ardour of your embrace almost intolerable.”
At this very ordinary request a sudden flatness overspread Ning’s manner and he began to describe the many much more profitable rewards that Hia might fittingly demand. As none of these appeared to entice her imagination, he went on to rebuke her want of foresight, and, still later, having unsuccessfully pointed out to her the inevitable penury and degradation in which her thriftless perversity would involve her later years, to kick the less substantial appointments across the tent.
“The night thickens, with every indication of a storm,” remarked Hia pleasantly. “Yet that same impending flash of promised lightning tarries somewhat.”
“Truly is it written: ‘A gracious woman will cause more strife than twelve armed men can quell,’ ” retorted Ning bitterly.
“Not, perchance, if one of them bares his nails?” Thus she lightly mocked him, but always with a set intent, as a poised dragonfly sips water yet does not wet his wings. Whereupon, finally, Ning tore the sheaths from off his fingers and cast them passionately about her feet, immediately afterwards sinking into a profound sleep, for both the measure and the potency of the wine he had consumed exceeded his usual custom. Otherwise he would scarcely have acted in this incapable manner, for each sheath was inscribed with one symbol of a magic charm and in the possession of the complete sentence resided the whole of the Being’s authority and power.
Then Hia, seeing that he could no longer control her movements, and that the end to which she had been bending was attained, gathered together the fruits of her conscientious strategy and fled.
When Ning returned to the condition of ordinary perceptions he was lying alone in the field by the riverside. The great sky-fire made no pretence of averting its rays from his uncovered head, and the lesser creatures of the ground did not hesitate to walk over his once sacred form.

===Chinese===

“然而听过君之双唇吐露纯金般的妙语之后，世间凡金俗银又算得了甚么？凝视过君那圆满宛若朗月般的生辉面庞之后，谁人还会将珠玉玛瑙视作至宝？唯有一事尚存遗珠之憾：恳请褪去君双手所戴的诸般甲套罢，盖因其不仅遮蔽了内中指甲那无可置疑的绝世完美，且其沉重棱角更教君那温存炽热的相拥变得令人难以承受。”
听闻这桩寻常不过的请求，宁的神情顿时掠过一阵扫兴寡味之色，他转而向夏描摹起诸多远更为丰厚实惠的奖赏，坚称那才是她理应求取之物。见夏对这些赏赐皆意兴阑珊，他复又斥责她目光短浅；再其后，因徒劳地向她陈说其败家任性之举必将教其晚年沦落至无可挽回之穷困潦倒而未见成效，他索性在帐中狠狠飞脚踢踹那些不够牢固的陈设。
“夜色渐浓，满显暴风雨将至之兆，”夏笑语盈盈地提点道，“然而那道许诺降下的闪电，似乎颇有迁延之意呢。”
“古语诚不欺我：‘一介娇柔女子所掀起的风浪，纵使12名顶盔贯甲之壮士亦莫能平息’，”宁恨恨反唇相讥道。
“倘若其中一人展露出其利甲，是否便能平息了呢？”她这般轻巧挑衅嘲弄着他，却始终暗藏机锋，恰如蜻蜓点水、振翅凌虚而不沾湿双翼。至此，宁终按捺不住心头焦躁，愤然将指尖十套一把扯下，赌气掷于夏的足畔，随即因不胜酒力而昏昏陷入酣睡——因其方才所饮佳酿之深浅与后劲，皆远超其平日宿习。若非如此，他断不至做出此等糊涂至极之举，盖因每只护套上皆铭刻有一道神符真诀，唯有集齐全部法咒密语，方维系着这位天神全身之权柄与威能。
夏见天神已无法拘束其行止，而自身处心积虑图谋的大计已然告成，遂收拾起这番苦心孤诣之谋略战果，抽身潜逃。
待宁重归寻常神智觉受之时，他正孤身一人躺卧在河畔田埂之上。九天炽阳毫不避忌地将灼热光芒直射在他毫无遮蔽的头颅上，地上的蝼蚁微末生灵亦毫不迟疑地践踏掠过他昔日神圣的躯骸。

===Original===

The tent and all the other circumstances of the quest of Hia had passed into a state of no-existence, for with a somewhat narrow-minded economy the deity had called them into being with the express provision that they need only be of such a quality as would last for a single night.
With this recollection, other details began to assail his mind. His irreplaceable nail-sheaths﻿—there was no trace of one of them. He looked again. Alas! his incomparable nails were also gone, shorn off to the level of his finger-ends. For all their evidence he might be one who had passed his days in discreditable industry. Each moment a fresh point of degradation met his benumbed vision. His profuse and ornamental locks were reduced to a single roughly-plaited coil; his sandals were inelegant and harsh; in place of his many-coloured flowing robes a scanty blue gown clothed his form. He who had been a god was undistinguishable from the labourers of the fields. Only in one thing did the resemblance fail: about his neck he found a weighty block of wood controlled by an iron ring: while they at least were free he was a captive slave.
A shadow on the grass caused him to turn. Sun Wei approached, a knotted thong in one hand, in the other a hoe. He pointed to an unweeded rice-field and with many ceremonious bows pressed the hoe upon Ning as one who confers high honours. As Ning hesitated, Sun Wei pressed the knotted thong upon him until it would have been obtuse to disregard his meaning. Then Ning definitely understood that he had become involved in the workings of very powerful forces, hostile to himself, and picking up the hoe he bent his submissive footsteps in the direction of the laborious rice-field.

===Chinese===

那座锦帐以及一应搜求寻觅夏之排场早已归于乌有，因这位天神当时出于某种心胸狭隘的俭省之念，在唤出这些物事之时曾立下严苛法限：只需维持一夜光景足矣。
忆及此处，更多惨淡细节接踵侵袭心头。他那不可替代的指甲护套——如今竟已荡然无存，连半只残影亦不可得。他复又定睛细看。天呐！他那一双世间无双的纤长利甲亦已失落，竟被齐根剪平至指尖！就凭这双手掌，他俨然成了终日从事卑贱劳作之苦力。每一瞬息，皆有崭新的受辱之状撞入其麻木凝滞的眼帘：他那丰盈齐整的美须华发被削减为一条粗糙编就的短辫；足下芒鞋粗砺难当；身上不再是那流光溢彩的飘逸霞帔，取而代之的是一袭蔽体短窄的蓝布短褐。曾经高居九天的神明，如今与田间挥汗之农夫已全无二致。唯有一处与众不同：他赫然发现自己颈项上竟套着一副沉重的木枷，由一道铁环牢牢锁死——田间农人尚有自由之身，而他却沦为遭囚受缚之奴隶。
草丛上一道投落的阴影促使他转过头来。只见孙威缓步踱近，一手执着皮鞭，一手提着铁锄。孙威指了指一块杂草丛生的稻田，连连作揖躬身，以宛若奉上无上恩宠之姿态将铁锄强塞入宁的手中。及至宁迟疑不决，孙威便挥动手中皮鞭抽打下来，其力道之沉，教人若再故作不解其意便委实显得愚钝了。至此，宁终于切实体会到，自己已深深卷入一股冲他而来的庞大敌意力量之中；他只得拾起铁锄，迈着顺从的步履，无奈走向那片劳碌艰辛的稻田。

## III The Incoming of the Youth, Tian / III 少年田之登场
===Original===

It was dawn in the High Heaven and the illimitable N’guk, waking to his labours for the day, looked graciously around on the assembled myriads who were there to carry his word through boundless space. Not wanting are they who speak two-sided words of the Venerable One from behind fan-like hands, but when his voice takes upon it the authority of a brazen drum knees become flaccid.
“There is a void in the unanimity of our council,” remarked the Supreme, his eye resting like a flash of lightning on a vacant place. “Wherefore tarries Ning, the son of Shin, the Seed-sower?”
For a moment there was an edging of N’guk’s inquiring glance from each Being to his neighbour. Then Leou stood audaciously forth.
“He is reported to be engaged on a private family matter,” he replied gravely. “Haply his feet have become entangled in a mesh of hair.”
N’guk turned his benevolent gaze upon another﻿—one higher in authority.
“Perchance,” admitted the superior Being tolerantly. “Such things are. How comes it else that among the earth-creatures we find the faces of the deities﻿—both the good and the bad?”
“How long has he been absent from our paths?”
They pressed another forward﻿—keeper of the Outer Path of the West Expanses, he.
“He went, High Excellence, in the fifteenth of the earth-ruler Chun, whom your enlightened tolerance has allowed to occupy the lower dragon throne for twoscore years, as these earthlings count. Thus and thus﻿—”
“Enough!” exclaimed the Supreme. “Hear my iron word. When the buffoon-witted Ning rises from his congenial slough this shall be his lot: for sixty thousand ages he shall fail to find the path of his return, but shall, instead, thread an aimless flight among the frozen ambits of the outer stars, carrying a tormenting rain of fire at his tail. And Leou, the Whisperer,” added the Divining One, with the inscrutable wisdom that marked even his most opaque moments, “Leou shall meanwhile perform Ning’s neglected task.”
---
For five and twenty years Ning had laboured in the fields of Sun Wei with a wooden collar girt about his neck, and Sun Wei had prospered. Yet it is to be doubted whether this last detail deliberately hinged on the policy of Leou or whether Sun Wei had not rather been drawn into some wider sphere of destiny and among converging lines of purpose.

===Chinese===

九天破晓，无边无际的至尊恩古克（N’guk）自沉睡中醒来，准备操持白昼之功业；他慈祥地环视着聚集阶前的亿万神灵，这班仙真正是奉其纶音将法旨播撒于无垠太虚之辈。在摇曳羽扇般的巨掌之后，对这位尊神口吐两面三刀之辞者向来不乏其人，然而一旦其嗓音如铜锣轰鸣般显露出至高威严，众神之膝无不发软打颤。
“我等合议之中，似乎缺了一员，”至尊言道，其目光宛若一道电光精准落在一处虚席之上，“播种之神辛（Shin）之子——宁，因何迟迟未至？”
刹那间，恩古克探询的目光引得众神面面相觑，推诿退避。旋即，恶魔娄竟肆无忌惮地迈步出列。
“据报他正忙于料理一桩私门家事，”他故作庄重地答道，“想来是一双尊足不慎缠绊于万缕青丝之中了吧。”
恩古克将慈祥的目光投向另一尊品秩更高的神祇。
“或许吧，”上位仙官宽和地承认道，“世间常有此等事。若非如此，又何以在下界生灵面庞上，屡屡得见诸神之相貌——不论善者抑或恶者？”
“他离开我等仙班之途已有几时了？”
众神将另一位神官推至驾前——那正是掌管西极外天通路之神。
“启奏至高天尊，他是在人主淳（Chun）在位15年离去的；蒙天尊开明宽宥，容那凡人坐在下界龙椅之上已有40载，若依下界凡人之寿算。诸事便这般那般……”
“够了！”至尊断然喝道，“且听本尊铁打之纶音：待那愚钝滑稽之宁自其乐此不疲的泥淖中脱身之时，此乃其注定之下场：在整整60000个劫波之中，他休想寻得归返天宫之通途，反倒须在外围寒星之冷寂轨道间漫无目的地流窜飘荡，身后拖曳着惩戒性的滚滚火雨！至于在侧进谗的娄，”这通灵至尊以其最具慧眼之幽微明睿补充道，“便由娄暂代宁所荒废之职守。”
——
整整25年间，宁颈戴木枷，在孙威的田间躬耕苦作，而孙威的家业亦日渐蒸蒸日上。然而，这最后的际遇究竟是娄处心积虑操弄之谋略，抑或孙威不知不觉已被卷入某种更为宏阔的造化洪流与诸般交汇的天命机锋之中，委实令人难以揣测。

===Original===

The ways of the gods are deep and sombre, and water once poured out will flow as freely to the north as to the south. The wise kowtows acquiescently whatever happens and thus his face is to the ground. “Respect the deities,” says the imperishable Sage, “but do not become familiar with them.” Sun Wei was clearly wrong.
To Ning, however, standing on a grassy space on the edge of a flowing river, such thoughts do not extend. He is now a little hairy man of gnarled appearance, and his skin of a colour and texture like a ripe loquat. As he stands there, something in the outline of the vista stirs the retentive tablets of his mind: it was on this spot that he first encountered Hia, and from that involvement began the cycle of his unending ill.
As he stood thus, implicated with his own inner emotions, a figure emerged from the river at its nearest point and, crossing the intervening sward, approached. He had the aspect of being a young man of high and dignified manner, and walked with the air of one accustomed to a silk umbrella, but when Ning looked more closely, to see by his insignia what amount of reverence he should pay, he discovered that the youth was destitute of the meagrest garment.
“Rise, venerable,” said the stranger affably, for Ning had prostrated himself as being more prudent in the circumstances. “The one before you is only Tian, of obscure birth, and himself of no particular merit or attainment. You, doubtless, are of considerably more honourable lineage?”
“Far from that being the case,” replied Ning, “the one who speaks bears now the commonplace name of Lieu, and is branded with the brand of Sun Wei. Formerly, indeed, he was a god, moving in the Upper Space and known to the devout as Ning, but now deposed by treachery.”
“Unless the subject is one that has painful associations,” remarked Tian considerately, “it is one on which this person would willingly learn somewhat deeper. What, in short, are the various differences existing between gods and men?”
“The gods are gods; men are men,” replied Ning. “There is no other difference.”
“Yet why do not the gods now exert their strength and raise from your present admittedly inferior position one who is of their band?”
“Behind their barrier the gods laugh at all men. How much more, then, is their gravity removed at the sight of one of themselves who has fallen lower than mankind?”

===Chinese===

诸神行事深邃莫测，覆水既泼，向北奔流与往南宣泄自无二致。智者无论遭逢何事皆顺从叩首，以此面伏于地。“敬鬼神，”不朽之圣人有云，“而远之。”孙威之行径显然大谬不然。
然而对于此刻伫立在潺潺流水畔草地上的宁而言，这等哲思早已无暇顾及。他如今已沦为一个身形干瘪、满面风霜、多毛矮小之人，皮肉之色泽肌理粗糙宛若熟透的枇杷。正当他伫立在此，眼帘中的景致轮廓勾起了他那历历在目的往昔记忆：正是在这片水畔，他初次邂逅夏，而自那番纠缠伊始，便开启了他那无穷无尽的苦难宿命。
正当他伫立于此，深陷自身愁肠百结之际，一道身影自临近的河水中涉水而出，穿过草坪缓步踱近。来人仪表非凡，宛若一位气度尊贵的少年，步履从容犹如惯受罗伞庇荫之达官；然而当宁定睛细瞧、意欲辨识其仪仗徽记以便行施恰当敬意之际，却赫然发现这名少年身上竟寸丝不挂，连半件最微薄的蔽体布帛亦未着身。
“老人家请起，”陌客和蔼可亲地说道，因宁出于审慎早已恭顺伏地，“阁下面前之人不过是田（Tian），出身微贱，自身亦无甚殊勋异能。阁下门第宗族，想来定远比在下显赫尊崇罢？”
“实情远非如此，”宁答道，“向你言语之人如今顶着寻常之极的‘刘’姓，身上烙着孙威的奴仆印记。昔日的确曾贵为神明，周行于九天之上，受善男信女供奉敬称为‘宁’，如今却遭奸计构陷，废黜至此。”
“倘若此事并未牵涉锥心之痛，”田体贴入微地言道，“在下倒极愿深切受教一二。敢问神明与凡人之间，究竟有何等千差万别？”
“神即是神，人即是人，”宁答道，“除此并无二致。”
“然而诸神如今为何不施展无上神力，将阁下这名同道自眼下显属卑微屈辱之境解救出去？”
“隔着天堑藩篱，诸神俯瞰凡人皆哂笑之。更遑论眼见同列之中竟有一人沦落至连凡夫俗子亦不如的境地，他们又何来庄重怜悯之意？”

===Original===

“Your plight would certainly seem to be an ill-destined one,” admitted Tian, “for, as the Verses say: ‘Gold sinks deeper than dross.’ Is there anything that an ordinary person can do to alleviate your subjection?”
“The offer is a gracious one,” replied Ning, “and such an occasion undoubtedly exists. Some time ago a pearl of unusual size and lustre slipped from its setting about this spot. I have looked for it in vain, but your acuter eyes, perchance﻿—”
Thus urged, the youth Tian searched the ground, but to no avail. Then chancing to look upwards, he exclaimed:
“Among the higher branches of the tallest bamboo there is an ancient phoenix nest, and concealed within its wall is a pearl such as you describe.”
“That manifestly is what I seek,” said Ning. “But it might as well be at the bottom of its native sea, for no ladder could reach to such a height nor would the slender branch support a living form.”
“Yet the emergency is one easily disposed of.” With these opportune words the amiable person rose from the ground without any appearance of effort or conscious movement, and floating upward through the air he procured the jewel and restored it to Ning.
When Ning had thus learned that Tian possessed these three attainments which are united in the gods alone﻿—that he could stand naked before others without consciousness of shame, that his eyes were able to penetrate matter impervious to those of ordinary persons, and that he controlled the power of rising through the air unaided﻿—he understood that the one before him was a deity of some degree. He therefore questioned him closely about his history, the various omens connected with his life and the position of the planets at his birth. Finding that these presented no element of conflict, and that, furthermore, the youth’s mother was a slave, formerly known as Hia, Ning declared himself more fully and greeted Tian as his undoubted son.
“The absence of such a relation is the one thing that has pressed heavily against this person’s satisfaction in the past, and the deficiency is now happily removed,” exclaimed Tian. “The distinction of having a deity for a father outweighs even the present admittedly distressing condition in which he reveals himself. His word shall henceforth be my law.”

===Chinese===

“阁下之遭遇委实堪称命途多舛，”田深表同情地承认道，“诚如诗云：‘赤金沉渊，深于渣滓。’不知寻常凡人可有何等微薄之力，足以纾解阁下受制之厄？”
“此番好意委实令人感念，”宁答道，“且眼下恰有一桩用武之地。此前不久，有一枚硕大光润、异乎寻常的珍珠自镶嵌处脱落，遗失在此地周遭。老夫搜寻良久一无所获，或许凭你那过人的敏锐双目……”
承此敦请，少年田在地上仔细搜寻，却终无所获。正当他偶一抬头之际，不由惊呼道：
“在最高那一丛修竹的顶梢，筑有一处古老的凤凰巢穴，隐于巢壁之中的，正是一枚如阁下所描摹的珍珠。”
“那显然便是老夫朝思暮想之物，”宁叹道，“然而其落于彼处，与沉入生养它的故海深渊又有何异？既无长梯可达此等绝高之处，那纤细柔脆的竹枝亦断难承载生灵之体重。”
“然而此番变故处置起来易如反掌。”伴随着这番恰逢其时的言辞，这位和蔼可亲的少年周身未见丝毫发力或动用形迹，竟自地面轻飘飘腾空而起，凌空虚渡直上云霄，探手取下明珠，稳稳奉还给宁。
当宁亲见田身兼唯有天神方能兼备的3项通天本领——能赤身裸体坦然立于人前而无羞惭之色、双目能洞穿凡人不可窥视之万物实体、且能不假外力随意凌空飞升——他心头豁然明朗：眼前之人定是某位身怀神性之神祇。于是，他极尽详尽地盘问其生平履历、出生前后的诸般祥瑞征兆，以及落地降生之时的星宿运行方位。探明此等关隘皆无冲突相悖之处，且少年生母乃是一名昔年唤作夏的奴仆，宁遂毫无保留地挑明身份，将田认作自己的骨肉亲生之子。
“过往岁月里，唯有膝下无所依托之憾压得在下意兴阑珊，如今这份欠缺终于欢喜冰释，”田欣喜叹道，“能得一位天神为生身之父，这份尊荣足以抵消他眼下所显露出的凄楚困顿。严父之言，自今而后便是在下奉行之金科玉律。”

===Original===

“The sentiment is a dutiful one,” admitted Ning, “and it is possible that you are now thus discovered in pursuance of some scheme among my more influential accomplices in the Upper Air for restoring to me my former eminence.”
“In so meritorious a cause this person is prepared to immerse himself to any depth,” declared Tian readily. “Nothing but the absence of precise details restrains his hurrying feet.”
“Those will doubtless be communicated to us by means of omens and portents as the requirement becomes more definite. In the meanwhile the first necessity is to enable this person’s nails to grow again; for to present himself thus in the Upper Air would be to cover him with ridicule. When the Emperor Chow-sin endeavoured to pass himself off as a menial by throwing aside his jewelled crown, the rebels who had taken him replied: ‘Omnipotence, you cannot throw away your knees.’ To claim kinship with those Above and at the same time to extend towards them a hand obviously inured to probing among the stony earth would be to invite the averted face of recognition.”
“Let recognition be extended in other directions and the task of returning to a forfeited inheritance will be lightened materially,” remarked a significant voice.
“Estimable mother,” exclaimed Tian, “this opportune stranger is my venerated father, whose continuous absence has been an overhanging cloud above my gladness, but now happily revealed and restored to our domestic altar.”
“Alas!” interposed Ning, “the opening of this enterprise forecasts a questionable omen. Before this person stands the one who enticed him into the beginning of all his evil; how then﻿—”
“Let the word remain unspoken,” interrupted Hia. “Women do not entice men﻿—though they admittedly accompany them, with an extreme absence of reluctance, in any direction. In her youth this person’s feet undoubtedly bore her occasionally along a light and fantastic path, for in the nature of spring a leaf is green and pliable, and in the nature of autumn it is brown and austere, and through changeless ages thus and thus. But, as it is truly said: ‘Milk by repeated agitation turns to butter,’ and for many years it has been this one’s ceaseless study of the Arts whereby she might avert that which she helped to bring about in her unstable youth.”
“The intention is a commendable one, though expressed with unnecessary verbiage,” replied Ning.

===Chinese===

“此番孝悌之忱委实难能可贵，”宁承认道，“且你如今在此被老夫撞见，极可能是九霄之上待我友善的同道暗中筹谋之妙计，旨在助老夫重登往日显赫尊位。”
“为这等功德无量之大业，在下甘愿赴汤蹈火，在所不辞，”田毫不犹豫地表态道，“唯独欠缺详尽细则，方才羁绊了在下急切前行之双足。”
“待到时机成熟，彼等自会借助诸般征兆与警示将机宜传递与我们。然而眼下当务之急，乃是先设法让老夫这双手掌重新生出长甲；盖因若以此等寒酸形貌现身于九天之上，必将招致漫天嘲笑。想当年纣王（Chow-sin）除下珠玉宝冠意欲伪装成厮役逃窜，擒获他的叛军却讥道：‘万岁爷，您的膝盖可剥落不得。’一面声称与九天仙真同气连枝，一面却向他们伸出一双分明惯于在乱石黄土中刨掘的粗糙双手，势必引得同道纷纷侧目，耻于相认。”
“若能将这份相认推及其他方向，重登昔日失落基业之重任便可大为减省，”一道寓意深长的嗓音忽自旁侧传来。
“可敬的母亲，”田惊喜呼道，“这位适时现身的陌客正是在下受人景仰的严父！他的久去不归曾是在下欢欣心头挥之不去的阴霾，如今终于云开雾散，重归我等祖堂神龛之上。”
“哎呀！”宁插言叹道，“此番大业甫一开局，便预示着凶险莫测之兆。立在老夫面前的，正是当初引诱老夫踏入一切厄运深渊的始作俑者；事已至此，又如何……”
“未尽之辞切莫吐露，”夏打断道，“女子从不引诱男子——虽说她们伴随男子涉足任何方向之时，分明毫无推诿迟疑之意。在妾身年轻之时，双足固然偶有踏上轻浮荒唐之路，盖因逢春之树叶生来嫩绿柔软，及至深秋则转为焦黄冷峻，千百年来亘古如斯。然而俗语说得好：‘牛奶久经搅动终成酥油’；多年以来，正是妾身孜孜不倦参研方术秘法，旨在破除自身在轻狂早年所促成的一切灾殃。”
“用心固然可嘉，辞藻却未免冗赘枝蔓，”宁答道。

===Original===

“To what solution did your incantations trend?”
“Concealed somewhere within the walled city of Ti-foo are the sacred nail-sheaths on which your power so essentially depends, sent thither by Sun Wei at the crafty instance of the demon Leou, who hopes at a convenient time to secure them for himself. To discover these and bear them forth will be the part allotted to Tian, and to this end has the training of his youth been bent. By what means he shall strive to the accomplishment of the project the unrolling curtain of the future shall disclose.”
“It is as the destinies shall decide and as the omens may direct,” said Tian. “In the meanwhile this person’s face is inexorably fixed in the direction of Ti-foo.”
“Proceed with all possible discretion,” advised Ning. “In so critical an undertaking you cannot be too cautious, but at the same time do not suffer the rice to grow around your advancing feet.”
“A moment,” counselled Hia. “Tarry yet a moment. Here is one whose rapidly-moving attitude may convey a message.”
“It is Lin Fa!” exclaimed Ning, as the one alluded to drew near﻿—“Lin Fa who guards the coffers of Sun Wei. Some calamity pursues him.”
“Hence!” cried Lin Fa, as he caught sight of them, yet scarcely pausing in his flight: “flee to the woods and caves until the time of this catastrophe be past. Has not the tiding reached you?”
“We be but dwellers on the farther bounds and no word has reached our ear, O great Lin Fa. Fill in, we pray you, the warning that has been so suddenly outlined.”
“The usurper Ah-tang has lit the torch of swift rebellion and is flattening-down the land that bars his way. Already the villages of Yeng, Leu, Liang-li and the Dwellings by the Three Pure Wells are as dust beneath his trampling feet, and they who stayed there have passed up in smoke. Sun Wei swings from the rooftree of his own ruined yamen. Ah-tang now lays siege to walled Ti-foo so that he may possess the Northern Way. Guard this bag of silver meanwhile, for what I have is more than I can reasonably bear, and when the land is once again at peace, assemble to meet me by the Five-Horned Pagoda, ready with a strict account.”
“All this is plainly part of an orderly scheme for my advancement, brought about by my friends in the Upper World,” remarked Ning, with some complacency.

===Chinese===

“尔之方术符咒究竟导向何等破解之法？”
“在提福（Ti-foo）城高耸城垣深处的某一隐秘角落，深藏着维系你通天神力所不可或缺的神圣指甲护套。当年乃是孙威在恶魔娄的奸诈唆使下送抵彼处，娄意在择机将其据为己有。搜寻这副护套并将其带出险境，正是分派给田的使命；且多年以来对他早年的教养训导，亦尽数聚焦于此。他将施展何等手段去成就这桩壮举，唯有让未来徐徐铺展的卷轴方能揭晓。”
“一切听凭天命裁断，依从神兆指引，”田答道，“此刻在下的面庞已然毫不动摇地投向了提福城之方向。”
“前行务必竭尽审慎之能事，”宁告诫道，“身负如此千钧重任，任尔如何小心亦不为过；然而与此同时，亦休教青草杂草缠绊了你前行的双足。”
“且慢，”夏从旁叮咛道，“且再驻足片刻。前方来了一人，其仓皇奔走的姿态分明预示着身负急讯。”
“那是林发（Lin Fa）！”宁脱口惊呼道，此时来人已跌撞奔近——“正是为孙威把守金库的林发！想来定有大祸临头在其身后追索。”
“快逃！”林发瞥见他们，脚步却未见丝毫迟滞，惊呼道：“快快逃入深山洞窟避难，直至这场浩劫平息！难道大难临头的急报尚未传至尔等耳中么？”
“我等不过是居于穷乡僻壤之边缘野民，耳中未闻丝毫风声，尊贵的林发大人！万乞详加赐教，将方才那仓皇警告描摹周全罢。”
“逆贼阿唐（Ah-tang）已然点燃迅猛叛乱之烽火，沿途扫平一切敢于挡道之土地城池！眼下郢村、娄村、良利村以及三清井（Three Pure Wells）畔的村舍，皆在其铁蹄践踏下化作齑粉，坚守彼处之人尽皆葬身火海烟灭！孙威更已被悬挂在其自身那残破衙门的屋梁之上吊死！阿唐如今正合围攻打提福城，意在据有通往北方的要冲咽喉！你等暂且替我保管这一口袋白银，盖因我身上的银两已重得超乎人力负荷；待到天下重归太平之日，尔等务必前往五角宝塔（Five-Horned Pagoda）与我会合，将账目明细分毫不差交割清楚！”
“凡此种种，分明是九天仙班中待我友善的同道暗中促成、旨在助我腾达之周密大计，”宁颇为自得地评说道。

===Original===

“Lin Fa has been influenced to the extent of providing us with the means for our immediate need; Sun Wei has been opportunely removed to the end that this person may now retire to a hidden spot and there suffer his dishonoured nails to grow again: Ah-tang has been impelled to raise the banner of insurrection outside Ti-foo so that Tian may make use of the necessities of either side in pursuit of his design. Assuredly the long line of our misfortunes is now practically at an end.”

===Chinese===

“林发冥冥中受人点化，为我等奉上了纾解眼前燃眉之急的资财；孙威更在恰逢其时的节骨眼上被铲除，以便老夫得以退隐密林僻静之所，静候这双受辱的十指重新滋生利甲；阿唐被冥冥天意驱使，在提福城外竖起义旗反戈，恰好便于田在谋划自身大计之际，翻覆利用双方之迫切需求。毫无疑问，笼罩我等门楣的长久厄运，如今大体已然走到尽头矣。”

## IV Events Round Walled Ti-Foo / IV 提福城周遭风波
===Original===

Nevertheless, the alternative forced on Tian was not an alluring one. If he joined the band of Ah-tang and the usurper failed, Tian himself might never get inside Ti-foo; if, however, he allied himself with the defenders of Ti-foo and Ah-tang did not fail, he might never get out of Ti-foo. Doubtless he would have reverently submitted his cause to the inspired decision of the Sticks, or some other reliable augur, had he not, while immersed in the consideration, walked into the camp of Ah-tang. The omen of this occurrence was of too specific a nature not to be regarded as conclusive.
Ah-tang was one who had neglected the Classics from his youth upwards. For this reason his detestable name is never mentioned in the Histories, and the various catastrophes he wrought are charitably ascribed to the action of earthquakes, thunderbolts and other admitted forces. He himself, with his lamentable absence of literary style, was wont to declare that while confessedly weak in analogies he was strong in holocausts. In the end he drove the sublime emperor from his capital and into the Outer Lands; with true refinement the annalists of the period explain that the condescending monarch made a journey of inspection among the barbarian tribes on the confines of his Empire.
When Tian, charged with being a hostile spy, was led into the presence of Ah-tang, it was the youth’s intention to relate somewhat of his history, but the usurper, excusing himself on the ground of literary deficiency, merely commanded five of his immediate guard to bear the prisoner away and to return with his head after a fitting interval. Misunderstanding the exact requirement, Tian returned at the appointed time with the heads of the five who had charge of him and the excuse that in those times of scarcity it was easier to keep one head than five. This aptitude so pleased Ah-tang (who had expected at the most a farewell apophthegm) that he at once made Tian captain of a chosen band.
Thus was Tian positioned outside the city of Ti-foo, materially contributing to its ultimate surrender by the resourceful courage of his arms.

===Chinese===

然而，摆在田面前的抉择绝非诱人美差。倘若他投奔阿唐（Ah-tang）的麾下而这位僭越叛将兵败溃散，田自身恐将永远无法踏入提福（Ti-foo）城一步；然而倘若他与提福城的守军结盟而阿唐大获全胜，他又恐将永远无法活着走出提福城。若非在沉思之际不慎误撞入阿唐的军营大帐，他定然早已将自身气运恭谨交付予通灵卜筮之神签，抑或求教于其他灵验之征兆了。这桩变故的警兆之意委实过于昭彰，教人不得不视作定数。
阿唐自幼便废弛经书典籍之修习。正因如此，史书列传之中绝难寻见其遭人唾弃之恶名，其所酿成的一应浩劫亦被仁厚地归咎于地震、天雷及其他公认的天灾异变。而他本人出于令人扼腕的辞章修养匮乏，反倒惯常宣称：自己虽在经纶文墨之微言大义上自承拙劣，然而在杀人盈野、焚荡城池上却颇具心得。他最终将至高无上的少帝逐出京城，逼入化外蛮荒之地；而当时那些满腹经纶的秉笔史官，则极尽风雅地阐释道：谦冲自抑的万岁爷乃是亲临帝国极边之蛮夷部族巡狩体察。
及至田被扣上敌营奸细之罪名押解至阿唐阶前，这名少年原拟陈说一番自身生平来历，然而这名叛酋却以自身粗鄙少文为由推脱，仅仅喝令座下5名贴身侍卫将这名囚徒拖出帐外，并责令隔着恰当工夫后提其首级来复命。田误解了这番谕令的真意，竟在既定时辰提着负责羁押他的那5名侍卫之头颅折返大帐，并辩白道：眼下荒年缺粮之际，保全1颗人头终归比填饱5颗来得省俭。这份杀伐决断之机敏深得阿唐欢心（他原本至多料想听闻一段临终偈语罢了），遂当即擢拔田为手下一支精锐之统领。
田便这般驻扎在提福城城垣之外，以其智勇双全之武功，为促成该城之最终降服立下了汗马功劳。

===Original===

For the first time in the history of opposing forces he tamed the strength and swiftness of wild horses to the use of man, and placing copper loops upon their feet and iron bars between their teeth, he and his band encircled Ti-foo with an ever-moving shield through which no outside word could reach the town. Cut off in this manner from all hope of succour, the stomachs of those within the walls grew very small, and their eyes became weary of watching for that which never came. On the third day of the third moon of their encirclement they sent a submissive banner, and one bearing a written message, into the camp of Ah-tang.

> “We are convinced” (it ran) “of the justice of your cause. Let six of your lordly nobles appear unarmed before our ill-kept Lantern Gate at the middle gong-stroke of tomorrow and they will be freely admitted within our midst. Upon receiving a bound assurance safeguarding the limits of our temples, the persons and possessions of our chiefs, and the undepreciated condition of the first wives and virgin daughters of such as be of mandarin rank or literary degree, the inadequate keys of our broken-down defences will be laid at their sumptuous feet.
> “With a fervent handclasp as of one brother to another, and a passionate assurance of mutual goodwill,
> “Ko’en Cheng,
> “Important Official.”

===Chinese===

在两军对垒交锋的历史上，他头一次驯服了野马的凶悍勇力与疾驰迅捷以供人力驱策；他将铜圈环绕于马蹄之上，将铁嚼横衔于马齿之间，率领麾下精骑如一道流转不息的坚盾铁壁般将提福城合围，以致外界片言只字皆无法穿透城垣传递入内。以此断绝了一应求援希望之后，城中守军的肚腹日渐干瘪饥馁，双眸亦因久久守望那从未降临的救兵而疲惫不堪。围城第3个月的第3日，城内终向阿唐营寨遣送了一面降旗，并附上一封手书降书：

> “我等已深切领会”（信中写道）“将军举兵之公道正义。且请于明日午时钟鸣之刻，命将军麾下6位贵勋首领，身免甲胄兵刃，亲临我等残破之提灯门前，我方自当大开城门迎入城中。只要接纳一纸盖印誓书，承诺护全我方庙宇界限、庇佑诸位首领之性命资财，并不使官居要津或身负功名者之元配发妻与待字闺女蒙受丝毫贬抑羞辱，我等这破损防务之寒酸钥匙，自当恭谨奉于诸位将军尊足之下。
> “谨以手足同胞之热切握手，并致彼此善意之深挚誓言，
> “提福城重职命官
> “柯恩成（Ko’en Cheng）谨呈。”

===Original===

“It is received,” replied Ah-tang, when the message had been made known to him. “Six captains will attend.”
Alas! it is well written: “There is often a space between the fish and the fishplate.” Mentally inflated at the success of their efforts and the impending surrender of Ti-foo, Tian’s band suffered their energies to relax. In the dusk of that same evening one disguised in the skin of a goat browsed from bush to bush until he reached the town. There, throwing off all restraint, he declared his errand to Ko’en Cheng.
“Behold!” he exclaimed, “the period of your illustrious suffering is almost at an end. With an army capable in size and invincible in determination, the ever-victorious Wu Sien is marching to your aid. Defy the puny Ah-tang for yet three days more and great glory will be yours.”
“Doubtless,” replied Ko’en Cheng, with velvet bitterness: “but the sun has long since set and the moon is not yet risen. The appearance of a solitary star yesterday would have been more foot-guiding than the forecast of a meteor next week. This person’s thumb-signed word is passed and tomorrow Ah-tang will hold him to it.”
Now there was present among the council one wrapped in a mantle made of rustling leaves, who spoke in a smooth, low voice, very cunning and persuasive, with a plan already shaped that seemed to offer well and to safeguard Ko’en Cheng’s word. None remembered to have seen him there before, and for this reason it is now held by some that this was Leou, the Whisperer, perturbed lest the sacred nail-sheaths of Ning should pass beyond his grasp. As to this, says not the Wise One: “When two men cannot agree over the price of an onion who shall decide what happened in the time of Yu?” But the voice of the unknown prevailed, all saying: “At the worst it is but as it will be; perchance it may be better.”
That night there was much gladness in the camp of Ah-tang, and men sang songs of victory and cups of wine were freely passed, though in the outer walks a strict watch was kept. When it was dark the word was passed that an engaging company was approaching from the town, openly and with lights. These being admitted revealed themselves as a band of maidens, bearing gifts of fruit and wine and assurances of their agreeable behaviour.

===Chinese===

“收下了，”阿唐获悉信函内容后答道，“命6位统领前往赴约便是。”
呜呼！古语有云：“游鱼落入鱼盘之前，往往尚有咫尺天涯”，诚哉斯言！因战功告成与提福城降服在即而心生傲岸，田麾下士卒不知不觉懈怠了防备戒心。就在当日傍晚暮色四合之际，一名身裹山羊皮伪装之人自丛林灌木间一路啃食杂草穿行，竟神不知鬼不觉摸入了城中。至此，他抖落一身伪装，向柯恩成道明了来意：
“恭喜大人！”他振臂呼道，“大人显赫受难之苦境大抵已然告终！常胜将军吴显（Wu Sien）正统帅一支兵马雄壮、意志如铁的大军星夜驰援而来！只要大人再强撑抗拒那微末阿唐3日，泼天殊荣便尽归大人所有！”
“或许罢，”柯恩成带着柔中带刺的冷嘲答道，“然而落日早已沉沦，皓月却尚未升起。昨日天上若现一颗孤星，尚能引人步履前行；而下周将落之流星预兆，又济得甚事？本官已然画押许诺，明日阿唐定要以此逼勒于我。”
当时议事列席之中，有一位身披飒飒落叶织就之斗篷之人，吐出极其圆滑低沉的嗓音，刁钻而极具蛊惑之能事；他当堂献上一策，看似既能两全其美，又能保全柯恩成之誓约信义。在场众人皆记不得此前曾在何处见过此人，正因如此，后世方有人断言：此人必定是那进谗之魔娄，深恐宁那神圣之指甲护套脱离其掌握。关于此节，先哲岂非有云：“当二人为一截葱之身价争执不下之际，谁人又能断定大禹年间之是非曲直？”然而这无名异人之论断终占了上风，众人皆叹道：“最坏之局亦不过如天命注定，万一另有转机，岂非更善？”
那夜阿唐营寨中满是欢歌笑语，兵卒纵情高唱凯歌，酒碗频频推杯换盏，唯独在外围游哨处尚有严谨防范。及至夜色深沉，前哨传报：城中正有一队风姿绰约的女子，打着灯笼坦然步出城门朝军营款款而来。守军将她们迎入营内，方知乃是一班良家少女，携奉着鲜果美酒，并温存誓言定会殷勤相伴。

===Original===

Distributing themselves impartially about the tents of the chiefs and upper ones, they melted the hours of the night in graceful accomplishments and by their seemly compliance dispelled all thought of treachery. Having thus gained the esteem of their companions, and by the lavish persuasion of bemusing wine dimmed their alertness, all this band, while it was still dark, crept back to the town, each secretly carrying with her the arms, robes and insignia of the one who had possessed her.
When the morning broke and the sound of trumpets called each man to an appointed spot, direful was the outcry from the tents of all the chiefs, and though many heads were out-thrust in rage of indignation, no single person could be prevailed upon wholly to emerge. Only the lesser warriors, the slaves and the bearers of the loads moved freely to and fro and from between closed teeth and with fluttering eyelids tossed doubtful jests among themselves.
It was close upon the middle gong-stroke of the day when Ah-tang, himself clad in a shred torn from his tent (for in all the camp there did not remain a single garment bearing a sign of noble rank), got together a council of his chiefs. Some were clad in like attire, others carried a henchman’s shield, a paper lantern or a branch of flowers; Tian alone displayed himself without reserve.
“There are moments,” said Ah-tang, “when this person’s admitted accomplishment of transfixing three foemen with a single javelin at a score of measured paces does not seem to provide a possible solution. Undoubtedly we are face to face with a crafty plan, and Ko’en Cheng has surely heard that Wu Sien is marching from the west. If we fail to knock upon the outer gate of Ti-foo at noon today Ko’en Cheng will say: ‘My word returns. It is as naught.’ If they who go are clad as underlings, Ko’en Cheng will cry: ‘What slaves be these! Do men break plate with dogs? Our message was for six of noble style. Ah-tang but mocks.’ ” He sat down again moodily. “Let others speak.”
“Chieftain”﻿—Tian threw forth his voice﻿—“your word must be as iron﻿—‘Six captains shall attend.’ There is yet another way.”
“Speak on,” Ah-tang commanded.
“The quality of Ah-tang’s chiefs resides not in a cloak of silk nor in a silver-hilted sword, but in the sinews of their arms and the lightning of their eyes. If they but carry these they proclaim their rank for all to see.

===Chinese===

少女们不偏不倚地分赴各位统领与上位头目的帐幔之中，以娴熟风雅的才艺消磨漫漫长夜，更以温存体贴的承顺驱散了诸将心头的一切生疑戒备。以此博得同伴之欢心与信赖，复借醇美醉人之佳酿麻痹了众人的警惕防范，这班女子趁着黎明前最漆黑的时刻潜行折返城中；每名女子皆暗中夹带走了曾占有她的那位将领之全副铠甲衣冠、佩剑与功勋徽记。
待到晨曦初露，号角之声催促各路将领赴约定之所集合之际，众首领的军帐内登时爆发出一阵凄厉惊怒之嚎呼；虽有无数头颅怒发冲冠地探出帐帘，然而任凭如何催请，竟无一人肯大步走出营帐！唯有下等兵卒、奴仆与负重脚夫在营中随意往来，咬紧牙关，在扑闪的眼睑下私下互抛嘲弄取笑之隐语。
时辰已逼近正午钟鸣之刻，阿唐自身仅以自营帐上撕下的一片残破粗布遮体（盖因阖营之中竟找不出一件带有贵勋印记之衣袍），好不容易才召集起众将商议。诸将或裹着类似破布，或手托亲兵盾牌、纸扎灯笼抑或一枝野花以蔽身躯；唯独田赤身露体，坦荡无遮，毫无矫饰之态。
“在某些节骨眼上，”阿唐叹道，“纵使本将身兼在20步开外以一杆投枪精准洞穿3名敌寇这等公认武功，似乎亦难解眼下之困厄。毫无疑问，我等正遭逢歹毒狡计，且柯恩成定是闻听了吴显自西方驰援之风声。倘若今日正午我等未能叩响提福城的外门，柯恩成必会讥道：‘前言作废，降约已化乌有！’若遣去之人衣衫褴褛如厮役，柯恩成更会耻笑：‘这等奴才安敢登堂！凡人岂会与野狗分食同盘？我方之请乃是恭迎6位贵勋统领，阿唐此举分明戏弄于我！’”他满面阴郁地跌坐回座，“且听诸位有何良策。”
“大帅！”田提气高声建言道，“大帅金口一开自当如生铁般不移——‘派6位统领前往赴约！’此事尚有另一破解之道。”
“讲来！”阿唐喝令道。
“阿唐大帅麾下诸将之风骨品貌，非在绫罗绸缎之斗篷，亦不在银柄宝剑之虚饰，而在其双臂刚劲之筋骨，及其眼中雷霆之精芒！只要携此威仪登堂，其尊崇位份天下谁人不知、谁人不晓？

===Original===

Let six attend taking neither sword nor shield, neither hat nor sandal, nor yet anything between. ‘There are six thousand more,’ shall be their taunt, ‘but Ko’en Cheng’s hospitality drew rein at six. He feared lest they might carry arms; behold they have come naked. Ti-foo need not tremble.’ ”
“It is well,” agreed Ah-tang. “At least, nothing better offers. Let five accompany you.”
Seated on a powerful horse Tian led the way. The others, not being of his immediate band, had not acquired the necessary control, so that they walked in a company. Coming to the Lantern Gate Tian turned his horse suddenly so that its angry hoof struck the gate. Looking back he saw the others following, with no great space between, and so passed in.
When the five naked captains reached the open gate they paused. Within stood a great concourse of the people, these being equally of both sexes, but they of the inner chambers pressing resolutely to the front. Through the throng of these their way must lead, and at the sight the hearts of all became as stagnant water in the sun.
“Tarry not for me, O brothers,” said the one who led. “A thorn has pierced my foot. Take honourable precedence while I draw it forth.”
“Never,” declared the second of the band, “never shall it be cast abroad that Kang of the House of Ka failed his brother in necessity. I sustain thy shoulder, comrade.”
“Alas!” exclaimed the third. “This person broke his fast on rhubarb stewed in fat. Inopportunely﻿—” So he too turned aside.
“Have we considered well,” said they who remained, “whether this be not a subtle snare, and while the camp is denuded of its foremost warriors a strong force﻿—?”
Unconscious of these details, Tian went on alone. In spite of the absence of gravity on the part of the more explicit portion of the throng he suffered no embarrassment, partly because of his position, but chiefly through his inability to understand that his condition differed in any degree from theirs; for, owing to the piercing nature of his vision, they were to him as he to them. In this way he came to the open space known as the Space of the Eight Directions, where Ko’en Cheng and his nobles were assembled.
“One comes alone,” they cried. “This guise is as a taunt.” “Naked to a naked town﻿—the analogy is plain.” “Shall the mocker be suffered to return?”
Thus the murmur grew.

===Chinese===

“便命6人前往赴约，既免佩剑亦免执盾，既免冠冕亦免穿芒鞋，周身寸丝不挂！‘营中尚有6000大军！’这便是向他们掷去的冷嘲；‘然而柯恩成之盛情设宴款待止于6人！他生恐我等暗藏兵刃，如今我等坦荡赤身而来，提福城大可不必吓破了胆！’”
“言之有理，”阿唐赞许道，“至少眼下别无更善之策。便派5人随你同行便是。”
田骑上一匹神骏烈马在前开道。其余5人并非其亲随部属，未曾练就这等御马腾空之绝技，只得结队徒步相随。行抵提灯门前，田骤然勒马调转，烈马扬蹄愤怒叩击城门。田回首见其余5人紧随其后，相距未远，遂策马扬长而入。
然而当这5名赤身裸体的将领行抵敞开的城门前之际，却不由齐刷刷驻足止步。只见城门之内，万千城中百姓正密密匝匝聚集围观，男女各半，而深宅内室之女眷竟毅然挤在最前列翘首观望！若要穿行而过，必须自这滚滚人潮中硬生生杀出一条去路；目睹此等阵势，5位将领心头顿时如烈日曝晒下的死水般冰凉凝滞。
“兄弟们切莫等我，”领头那位将领推诿道，“在下足底被毒荆棘扎透了。诸位且享尊荣先行一步，容在下将刺拔出。”
“断无此理！”队伍中第2位将领慨然表态道，“‘卡氏之门康某人岂会在危难之际遗弃袍泽’这等恶名，绝不可传扬于四海！兄弟，我来搀扶你的臂膀！”
“哎呀！”第3位将领惊呼道，“在下晨起空腹误食了猪油煨大黄，此刻腹内翻江倒海，委实不巧……”言罢亦抽身转入一旁。
“我等可曾思虑周详？”残存的两位将领嘀咕道，“此莫非是一记诱敌深入之连环毒计？趁我军营大寨精锐尽出、空虚无备之际，敌方强虏……”
对于这诸般细节变故，田全然未曾留意，独自一人从容前行。面对围观人群中那些放浪直率之辈毫无庄重端谨之嬉笑，他却神色自若，丝毫不觉难堪；个中缘由，部分固然因其身负重任位分所致，然而主要却在于其神异双目洞穿虚妄，全然无法理解自身赤裸之境与众人穿着又有何本质差别——盖因依其过人洞察力，众人在其眼中与他自身在众人眼中毫无分别。田便这般一路行抵被唤作“八方坪”（Space of the Eight Directions）的空旷广场，柯恩成与其一干贵勋僚属早已肃立于彼处。
“来者竟仅有一人！”众人惊呼道，“此等形貌分明是奇耻大辱之嘲弄！”“赤身裸体步入一座赤贫之空城——个中譬喻委实昭然若揭！”“岂能容这狂妄小儿活着折返？”
鼎沸怨谤之声四起。

===Original===

Then one, more impetuous than the rest, swung clear his sword and drew it. For the first time Tian understood that treachery was afoot. He looked round for any of his band, but found that he was as a foam-tossed cork upon a turbulent Whang Hai. Cries of anger and derision filled the air; threatening arms waved encouragement to each other to begin. The one with drawn sword raised it above his head and made a step. Then Tian, recognizing that he was unarmed, and that a decisive moment had arrived, stooped low and tore a copper hoop from off his horse’s foot. High he swung its polished brightness in the engaging sun, resolutely brought it down, so that it pressed over the sword-warrior’s shattered head and hung about his neck. Having thus effected as much bloodshed as could reasonably be expected in the circumstances, Tian curved his feet about his horse’s sides and imparting to it the virtue of his own condition they rose into the air together. When those who stood below were able to exert themselves a flight of arrows, spears and every kind of weapon followed, but horse and rider were by that time beyond their reach, and the only benevolent result attained was that many of their band were themselves transfixed by the falling shafts.
In such a manner Tian continued his progress from the town until he came above the Temple of Fire and Water Forces, where on a high tower a strong box of many woods was chained beneath a canopy, guarded by an incantation laid upon it by Leou, that no one should lift it down. Recognizing the contents as the object of his search, Tian brought his horse to rest upon the tower, and breaking the chains he bore the magic sheaths away, the charm (owing to Leou’s superficial habits) being powerless against one who instead of lifting the box down carried it up.
In spite of this distinguished achievement it was many moons before Tian was able to lay the filial tribute of restored power at Ning’s feet, for with shallow-witted obstinacy Ti-foo continued to hold out, and, scarcely less inept, Ah-tang declined to release Tian even to carry on so charitable a mission.

===Chinese===

此时有一人格外性急冲动，拔出腰间利剑挥舞作势。至此，田方始领悟背信弃义之叛变已然揭幕。他四下搜寻自身同伴之身影，却发觉自己宛若黄海（Whang Hai）怒涛狂澜中漂浮的一枚颠簸软木，孤立无援。怒吼与嘲弄之声震彻天宇；一双双挥舞着兵刃的手臂相互呼应鼓劲，意欲拔刀相向。那柄已出鞘的利剑高擎于头顶之上，持剑武士猛然向前跨出一步。田深知自己手无寸铁，而生死存亡之决断关头已然降临；他当即俯身探手，自坐骑马蹄上狠狠扯下一枚铜箍。在灼灼烈日照耀下，他将那闪烁寒芒的锃亮铜圈高高抡起，决然猛力砸落，不偏不倚箍在那名持剑武士被击碎的头颅之上，宛若铁项圈般牢牢挂在其脖颈之间。在当前情境之下，田已然施展了合情合理之最大杀伤，随即双足夹紧马腹，将自身凌空腾挪之神力灌注于坐骑通体；霎时间，一人一骑拔地而起，破空飞升入九霄之中！待到下方立足之人回过神来、使出浑身解数弯弓射箭、投掷长矛与诸般兵刃之际，一人一骑早已飞升至兵刃莫及的高空深处；而下方唯一结出的“仁慈善果”，便是他们自身许多同党反被坠落的如雨箭矢刺穿射杀。
田便以这般神异姿态自城中凌空飞掠而过，直至飞临水火神祠（Temple of Fire and Water Forces）上空；只见在高耸宝塔之巅的华盖之下，一口由数种异木打造的沉重宝匣被铁链重重锁死，且被恶魔娄布下了强大咒怨禁制：严禁任何人将其自上取下。田认出内中所藏之物正是自己苦苦搜求的目标，遂策马稳稳降落在宝塔顶端；他奋力扯断铁链，将这副玄妙甲套径直带入高空——盖因娄那浅尝辄止的粗率做派，此番恶毒禁制在面对一个不仅未将宝匣“取下”、反而将其径直“托举升空”之人面前，竟彻底失效化解。
尽管立下了这等惊天动地的殊勋大业，然而田在足足历经无数个寒暑圆月之后，方得以将这件光复神力的孝顺贡礼奉至宁的尊足之下；盖因提福城凭借其浅薄冥顽的死硬脾性长久负隅顽抗，而同样愚不可及的阿唐更断然拒绝放田离去，哪怕田乃是去践行这等功德无量之慈善使命。

===Original===

Yet when the latter one ultimately returned and was, as the reward of his intrepid services, looking forward to a period of domestic reunion under the benevolent guidance of an affectionate father, it was but to point the seasoned proverb: “The fuller the cup the sooner the spill,” for scarcely had Ning drawn on the recovered sheaths and with incautious joy repeated the magic sentence than he was instantly projected across vast space and into the trackless confines of the Outer Upper Paths. If this were an imagined tale, framed to entice the credulous, herein would its falseness cry aloud, but even in this age Ning may still be seen from time to time with a tail of fire in his wake, missing the path of his return as N’guk ordained.
Thus bereft, Tian was on the point of giving way to a seemly despair when a message concerned with Mu, the only daughter of Ko’en Cheng, reached him. It professed a high-minded regard for his welfare, and added that although the one who was inspiring the communication had been careful to avoid seeing him on the occasion of his entry into Ti-foo, it was impossible for her not to be impressed by the dignity of his bearing. Ko’en Cheng having become vastly wealthy as the result of entering into an arrangement with Ah-tang before Ti-foo was sacked, it did not seem unreasonable to Tian that Ning was in some way influencing his destiny from afar.
