“Providing the notorious thug Kai Lung is not thereby brought in,” suggested the narrow-minded Ming-shu, who equally desired to learn the stratagem involved.

===Chinese===

“在某些节骨眼上，我中华典雅语言之千古词章，在抒发心中澎湃情感之际亦显得出奇匮乏，”华梅在凯龙眼前展现真容，凯龙顿时面红过耳，由衷叹服道，“古语诚不欺我：‘柔弱女子之智巧，足以倾覆九座巍巍高山。’佳人已然将小生胸中一切疑虑化为乌有矣。”
“小女子正是为此而来，且在这一桩事上亦需仰仗相公之高才。请仔细端详、凝神听取小女子之言：眼下四海兵燹之战局日甚一日走向扑朔迷离，天下尚无一人真切知晓究竟该倒向何方阵营。逢此危局，人皆怀二心。善天县令表面上对朝廷竭尽忠忱，暗中却频频散布私下拥戴义旗之耳语；明恕如今亦公然宣称：倘若局势这般那般，叛军之中亦有公理正义，而与此同时他又在外宣扬此举不过是为更好报效朝廷之韬晦权宜。如是，人人皆备两副面孔，期冀一方若败另一方仍能保全自身性命；且众人结成同盟，誓言彼此救护（若有必要亦相互出卖）。”
“此事极易领会，逢此多事之秋，向来大抵皆是这般光景。”
“既然如此，想来相公唇畔定有信手拈来之生动掌故。且传授小女子这般一段传奇故事，足以羁绊那些随波逐流之徒的心神期冀；余下之事，尽可交由小女子一手操持。”
翌日到了既定时辰，一名身形伛偻、相貌阴鸷的老妪被押解至善天阶前，公堂之上当众宣读了其罪状。
“世间万物，换个角度审视大抵皆能寻得托辞，”县令不偏不倚地评说道，“然而谋杀亲夫之重罪——且是借由这等阴柔刁钻之手段——委实教人无话可说。”
“然而，至圣青天大老爷，纵是残破私钱亦有正反两面，”老妪答道，“民妇之身负罪愆却又实属无辜，比之当年翁超（Weng Cho）面对究竟是抗拒复仇帮会、抑或背叛朝廷王法之两难绝境中，终寻得两全脱身之妙策，亦算不得更为惊世骇俗。”
“此案倒颇值得……意即，倘若你当真以既成先例为自身抗辩之据……”
“只要莫借机将那臭名昭著的暴徒凯龙牵扯进来便是，”心胸狭隘的明恕从旁进言，盖因他同样极欲窥探个中所蕴之脱身妙计。

===Original===

“Weng Cho was the only one concerned,” replied the ancient obtusely﻿—“he who escaped the consequences. Is it permitted to this one to make clear her plea?”
“If the fatigue is not more than your venerable personality can reasonably bear,” replied Shan Tien courteously.
“To bear is the lot of every woman, be she young or old,” replied the one before them. “I comply, omnipotence.”

===Chinese===

“涉案之人唯有翁超一人，”老妪装聋作哑答道，“正是他避开了杀身之祸。不知大老爷可恩准民妇陈明申辩之辞？”
“倘若这番劳顿未曾超出尊驾风烛残年所能承载之极限，”善天彬彬有礼地应允道。
“负重乃天下女子之宿命，不论豆蔻抑或花甲，”堂下老妪应道，“民妇遵命，青天大老爷。”

### The Story of Weng Cho; or, the One Devoid of Name / 翁超，或曰无名者之传说
===Original===

There was peach-blossom in the orchards of Kien-fi, a blue sky above, and in the air much gladness; but in Wu Chi’s yamen gloom hung like the herald of a thunderstorm. At one end of a table in the ceremonial hall sat Wu Chi, heaviness upon his brow, deceit in his eyes, and a sour enmity about the lines of his mouth; at the other end stood his son Weng, and between them, as it were, his whole life lay.
Wu Chi was an official of some consequence and had two wives, as became him. His union with the first had failed in its essential purpose; therefore he had taken another to carry on the direct line which alone could bring him contentment in this world and a reputable existence in the next. This degree of happiness was supplied by Weng’s mother, yet she must ever remain but a “secondary wife,” with no rights and a very insecure position. In the heart of the chief wife smouldered a most bitter hatred, but the hour of her ascendancy came, for after many years she also bore her lord a son. Thenceforward she was strong in her authority; but Weng’s mother remained, for she was very beautiful, and despite all the arts of the other woman Wu Chi could not be prevailed upon to dismiss her. The easy solution of this difficulty was that she soon died﻿—the “white powder death” was the shrewd comment of the inner chambers of Kien-fi.
Wu Chi put on no mourning, custom did not require it; and now that the woman had Passed Beyond he saw no necessity to honour her memory at the expense of his own domestic peace. His wife donned her gayest robes and made a feast. Weng alone stood apart, and in funereal sackcloth moved through the house like an accusing ghost. Each day his father met him with a frown, the woman whom alone he must regard as his mother with a mocking smile, but he passed them without any word of dutiful and submissive greeting. The period of all seemly mourning ended﻿—it touched that allotted to a legal parent; still Weng cast himself down and made no pretence to hide his grief. His father’s frown became a scowl, his mother’s smile framed a biting word. A wise and venerable friend who loved the youth took him aside one day and with many sympathetic words counselled restraint.
“For,” he said, “your conduct, though affectionate towards the dead, may be urged by the ill-disposed as disrespectful towards the living.

===Chinese===

建飞（Kien-fi）城中的桃林深处繁花盛开，头顶碧空如洗，四海洋溢着欢欣喜气；然而在吴齐（Wu Chi）的衙门之内，阴霾低垂，宛若暴风雨将至之凶兆。在正堂大案的一端端坐着吴齐，眉宇沉重，眼中闪烁着机诈之色，唇齿轮廓间透出一股冷冽的怨毒；在大案的另一端挺立着其子翁超（Weng Cho），而两人之间，横亘着的仿佛是他的一整个人生。
吴齐乃是一位位分显赫的朝廷命官，循其门第纳有二房妻妾。其与元配发妻之结合未能达成开枝散叶之根本大义；为此他迎娶偏房以延续香火，盖因唯有此举方能令他在阳世得享知足、在九泉之下享有体面存续。这份人伦之乐乃是由翁超之生母所赋予，然而她却注定永为一名地位毫无保障之妾室。正室夫人的心头常年燃烧着刻骨仇恨；然而随着岁月流转，属于她的得势时刻终于降临——多年之后，她亦为夫君诞下了一名麟儿。自此，她在后宅之中大权独揽威风凛凛；然而翁超之母依然留在府中，盖因其天生丽质倾国倾城，任凭正室施展万般手腕，吴齐始终不忍将其休弃遣散。化解这一死结的便捷之策来得极快：妾室不久便暴毙身亡——“白粉索命（white powder death）”，建飞城深宅内室对此皆做此心照不宣之议论。
吴齐并未穿戴丧服，祖制礼法未有此等规矩；且既然那妇人已归道山，他深觉无甚必要以牺牲自家内宅安宁为代价去祭奠其亡灵。正室夫人换上了最华丽的吉服，大排筵宴庆贺。唯独翁超孤立独行，身披粗麻丧服在府中游荡，宛若一尊带冤索命之厉鬼。每一日，严父皆报以紧锁之双眉，那位他依法必须视作生母的妇人则报以嘲弄冷笑；然而翁超与他们错身而过之际，未曾吐露半句孝顺顺从之问候。为人子所应尽之体面服丧之期已然届满——其时长已抵得过为嫡母守制之规制；然而翁超依然伏地哀恸，毫无掩饰胸中悲戚之意。严父的蹙眉化作怒目横眉，嫡母的冷笑化作尖酸恶语。一位慈爱关照这名少年的德高望重之老友一日将他拉至一旁，满含怜惜劝他务必收敛克制：
“盖因，”老友苦口婆心劝道，“你这番行径虽对亡者极尽孝思，然而在心怀叵测之徒口中，却极易被斥为对生者大逆不道。

===Original===

If you have a deeper end in view, strive towards it by a less open path.”
“You are subtle and esteemed in wisdom,” replied Weng, “but neither of those virtues can restore a broken jar. The wayside fountain must one day dry up at its source, but until then not even a mountain placed upon its mouth can pen back its secret stores. So is it with unfeigned grief.”
“The analogy may be exact,” replied the aged friend, shaking his head, “but it is no less truly said: ‘The wise tortoise keeps his pain inside.’ Rest assured, on the disinterested advice of one who has no great experience of mountains and hidden springs, but a lifelong knowledge of Wu Chi and of his amiable wife, that if you mourn too much you will have reason to mourn more.”
His words were pointed to a sharp edge. At that moment Wu Chi was being confronted by his wife, who stood before him in his inner chamber. “Who am I?” she exclaimed vehemently, “that my authority should be denied before my very eyes? Am I indeed Che of the house of Meng, whose ancestors wore the Yellow Scabbard, or am I some nameless one? Or does my lord sleep, or has he fallen blind upon the side by which Weng approaches?”
“His heart is bad and his instincts perverted,” replied Wu Chi dully. “He ignores the rites, custom, and the Emperor’s example, and sets at defiance all the principles of domestic government. Do not fear that I shall not shortly call him to account with a very heavy call.”
“Do so, my lord,” said his wife darkly, “or many valiant champions of the House of Meng may press forward to make a cast of that same account. To those of our ancient line it would not seem a trivial thing that their daughter should share her rights with a purchased slave.”
“Peace, cockatrice! the woman was well enough,” exclaimed Wu Chi, with slow resentment. “But the matter of this obstinacy touches the dignity of my own authority, and before today has passed Weng shall bring up his footsteps suddenly before a solid wall.”
Accordingly, when Weng returned at his usual hour he found his father awaiting him with curbed impatience. That Wu Chi should summon him into his presence in the great hall was of itself an omen that the matter was one of moment, but the profusion of lights before the Ancestral Tablets and the various symbols arranged upon the table showed that the occasion was to be regarded as one involving irrevocable issues.

===Chinese===

倘若你心存更深之图谋，亦当循着不那么张扬的途径去谋划。”
“老丈智谋深邃为人景仰，”翁超答道，“然而这两般美德皆无法复原一只破碎的陶罐。道旁的涌泉终有一日会在源头干涸，然而在那之前，纵使将整座高山压在其泉眼之上，亦断难阻遏其隐秘奔涌。由衷之悲恸，亦复如是。”
“个中譬喻固然贴切，”老友摇首叹道，“然而古训亦云：‘智龟内敛其痛’，绝非虚言。且听一个对高山涌泉知之甚少、却终生深谙吴齐及其‘贤妻’脾性之人的由衷忠告罢：你若悲恸过甚，只恐后日将有更甚之痛噬咬于心矣。”
老友之言一语成谶。此时此刻在后宅内室之中，吴齐正面对着其正室夫人的咄咄相逼。“妾身究竟算得甚么人？”她厉声发作道，“以致妾身的威严竟在自身眼皮底下遭人公然践踏？妾身难道不是出自祖上佩戴黄鞘宝剑之孟府（House of Meng）名门闺秀，反倒成了一个无名下贱之人不成？莫非夫君已然沉睡不醒，抑或在翁超踱近的那一侧双目早已失明？”
“这逆子居心叵测，天性乖戾，”吴齐面色阴沉应道，“无视礼乐法度、天子教化，公然藐视齐家之根本纲领。休要心焦，老夫不日便会以极重之家法严加清算。”
“但愿夫君说到做到，”其妻阴鸷地冷笑道，“否则孟府一干骁勇武士自当挺身而出，亲自代为清算这笔账目！对于吾家古老门第而言，自家族女竟与一名买来的婢妾平分秋色，可绝非一桩等闲小事。”
“住口，毒妇！那女人原本安分守己得很，”吴齐心生反感怒斥道，“然而这逆子之顽梗已然伤及老夫自身之一家之主尊严；今日太阳落山之前，老夫定要教他撞上一面生铁石壁！”
于是，待翁超如常归家之际，赫然发现父亲正按捺着焦躁神色在大堂等候。吴齐竟在大堂正厅宣召于他，此举本身便预示着事关重大；而祖先牌位前燃起的漫天烛火，以及案几上陈设的诸般仪轨器皿，更昭示着今日之决断势必关乎无可挽回之终局。

===Original===

“Weng Cho,” said his father dispassionately, from his seat at the head of the table, “draw near, and first pledge the Ancient Ones whose spirits hover above their Tablets in a vessel of wine.”
“I am drinking affliction and move under the compact of a solemn vow,” replied Weng fixedly, “therefore I cannot do this; nor, as signs are given me to declare, will the forerunners of our line, who from their high places look down deep into the mind and measure the heart with an impartial rod, deem this an action of disrespect to their illustrious shades.”
“It is well to be a sharer of their councils,” said Wu Chi, with pointed insincerity. “But,” he continued, in the same tone, “for whom can Weng Cho of the House of Wu mourn? His father is before him in his wonted health; in the inner chamber his mother plies an unfaltering needle; while from the Dragon Throne the supreme Emperor still rules the world. Haply, however, a thorn has pierced his little finger, or does he perchance bewail the loss of a favourite bird?”
“That thorn has sunk deeply into his existence, and the memory of that loss still dims his eyes with bitterness,” replied Weng. “Bid the rain cease to fall when the clouds are heavy.”
“The comparison is ill-chosen,” cried Whu Chi harshly. “Rather should the allusion be to the evil tendency of a self-willed branch which, in spite of the continual watering of precept and affection, maintains its perverted course, and must henceforth either submit to be bound down into an appointed line, or be utterly cut off so that the tree may not suffer. Long and patiently have I marked your footsteps, Weng Cho, and they are devious. This is not a single offence, but it is no light one. Appointed by the Board of Ceremony, approved of by the Emperor, and observed in every loyal and high-minded subject are the details of the rites and formalities which alone serve to distinguish a people refined and humane from those who are rude and barbarous. By setting these observances at defiance you insult their framers, act traitorously towards your sovereign, and assail the foundations of your House; for your attitude is a direct reflection upon others; and if you render such a tribute to one who is incompetent to receive it, how will you maintain a seemly balance when a greater occasion arises?”

===Chinese===

“翁超，”父亲端坐大案首座，不动声色地发话道，“且近前一步，先以案头这盅清酒敬奉悬浮于牌位之上的先祖英灵。”
“儿子胸中满饮凄楚，身负重誓大戒，”翁超神色坚决应道，“为此断难奉命；且冥冥征兆昭示于儿子：我族列祖列宗自九霄高处垂视人心最深处、以公道之尺丈量肺腑之际，断不会将此举视作对先祖显赫阴灵之亵渎大不敬。”
“能与先祖神灵息息相通，倒真是好造化，”吴齐带着刻薄的虚伪冷笑道，“然而，”他话锋不改，续道，“吴府之翁超究竟是在为谁守制？他的严父此刻康健肃立在面前；后宅内室中他的嫡母正飞针走线操持针黹；九重龙椅之上至高无上的天子依旧君临四海。或许是一根毒刺扎透了他的小指，抑或他正为痛失一只心爱的雀鸟而如丧考妣不成？”
“那根刺已深深扎入儿子生命骨髓，而痛失挚亲之锥心之痛，至今仍以无尽苦涩模糊着儿子的双眸，”翁超答道，“待铅云密布沉重之时，谁人能喝令暴雨止息？”
“此番譬喻委实大谬不然！”吴齐厉声喝道，“倒不如将其比作一根自甘堕落之逆枝，任凭戒律与慈爱甘霖日夜浇灌，却偏要肆意扭曲生长；自今而后，要么俯首就缚纳入规整正轨，要么彻底剪除以保全全树之生机！老夫长久且耐心地审视着你的步履，翁超，你的脚步已然走入歧途！这绝非一桩等闲过失，且绝非微末之罪！由礼部定夺、蒙天子钦准、且由天下一切忠臣义士身体力行恪守之仪轨典章，乃是区分文明开化与蛮荒野蛮之唯一分界！你公然践踏此等典制，无异于侮辱立法圣贤，忤逆圣上天恩，更是在动摇吴氏家族之基石！盖因你的所作所为乃是对旁人之公然挑衅折射；你若将此等孝思敬奉予一个毫无资格领受之人，来日逢更大节骨眼之际，你又如何维持体统平衡？”

===Original===

“When the earth that has nourished it grows cold the leaves of the branch fall﻿—doubtless the edicts of the Board referred to having failed to reach their ears,” replied Weng bitterly. “Revered father, is it not permitted that I should now depart? Behold I am stricken and out of place.”
“You are evil and your heart is fat with presumptuous pride!” exclaimed Wu Chi, releasing the cords of his hatred and anger so that they leapt out from his throat like the sudden spring of a tiger from a cave. “Evil in birth, grown under an evil star and now come to a full maturity. Go you shall, Weng Cho, and that on a straight journey forthwith or else bend your knees with an acquiescent face.” With these words he beat furiously on a gong, and summoning the entire household he commanded that before Weng should be placed a jar of wine and two glass vessels, and on the other side a staff and a pair of sandals. From an open shutter the face of the woman Che looked down in mocking triumph.
The alternatives thus presented were simple and irrevocable. On the one hand Weng must put from him all further grief, ignore his vows, and join in mirth and feast; on the other he must depart, never to return, and be deprived of every tie of kinship, relinquishing ancestry, possessions and name. It was a course severer than anything that Wu Chi had intended when he sent for his son, but resentment had distorted his eyesight. It was a greater test than Weng had anticipated, but his mind was clear, and his heart charged with fragrant memories of his loss. Deliberately but with silent dignity he poured the untasted wine upon the ground, drew his sword and touched the vessels lightly so that they broke, took from off his thumb the jade ring inscribed with the sign of the House of Wu, and putting on the sandals grasped the staff and prepared to leave the hall.
“Weng Cho, for the last time spoken of as of the House of Wu, now alienated from that noble line, and henceforth and forever an outcast, you have made a choice and chosen as befits your rebellious life. Between us stretches a barrier wider and deeper than the Yellow Sea, and throughout all future time no sign shall pass from that distant shore to this. From every record of our race your name shall be cut out; no mention of it shall profane the Tablets, and both in this world and the next it shall be to us as though you have never been.

===Chinese===

“当生养润泽大树之沃土被无情剥夺，根须暴晒于酷日之下，”翁超针锋相对反唇相讥，“树木自当枯槁；任凭上天降下何等甘霖，亦难掩其枯朽死相。儿子并非有意冒犯祖制，然而严父所敬奉之典章，不过是凡夫俗子为粉饰太平而营构之虚文；而儿子胸中所守之大孝，乃是天地阴阳所赋之至性真情！”
“逆子狂悖！”吴齐拍案而起，怒不可遏，“竟敢在祖宗神位前口出这等大逆不道之狂言！老夫今日若不断然正法，吴氏门楣必招天谴！且听分明：念在骨肉亲情，老夫赐你最后一路生机——立刻脱去身上丧服，在嫡母案前行三跪九叩之礼，自认嫡母为生身之母，发誓永世不再提及那贱婢名讳！否则，吴氏族谱之中，自今日始除名弃之！”
“儿子胸中唯有一位生母，”翁超傲然而立，寸步不让，“天地可鉴，日月昭昭；若要儿子认贼作母、玷污先妣亡灵，纵万劫不复亦断难从命！”
“好！好！好！”吴齐面如死灰，一连道出三声冷笑，“既然你冥顽不灵，甘做悖逆逆子，吴府自无容你立足之地！今日便在此列祖列宗神位之前，断绝父子纲常！自今而后，阳世阴司，你我各安天命，便如从未降生于世一般！”

===Original===

As I break this bowl so are all ties broken, as I quench this candle so are all memories extinguished, and as, when you go, the space is filled with empty air, so shall it be.”
“Ho, nameless stranger,” laughed the woman from above, “here is food and drink to bear you on your way”; and from the grille she threw a withered fig and spat.
“The fruit is the cankered effort of a barren tree,” cast back Weng over his shoulder. “Look to your own offspring, basilisk. It is given me to speak.” Even as he spoke there was a great cry from the upper part of the house, the sound of many feet and much turmoil, but he went on his way without another word.
Thus it was that Weng Cho came to be cut off from the past. From his father’s house he stepped out into the streets of Kien-fi a being without a name, destitute, and suffering the pangs of many keen emotions. Friends whom he encountered he saluted distantly, not desirous of sharing their affection until they should have learned his state; but there was one who stood in his mind as removed above the possibility of change, and to the summerhouse of Tiao’s home he therefore turned his steps.
Tiao was the daughter of a minor official, an unsuccessful man of no particular descent. He had many daughters, and had encouraged Weng’s affection, with frequent professions that he regarded only the youth’s virtuous life and discernment, and would otherwise have desired one not so highly placed. Tiao also had spoken of rice and contentment in a ruined pagoda. Yet as she listened to Weng’s relation a new expression gradually revealed itself about her face, and when he had finished many paces lay between them.
“A breaker of sacred customs, a disobeyer of parents and an outcast! How do you disclose yourself!” she exclaimed wildly. “What vile thing has possessed you?”
“One hitherto which now rejects me,” replied Weng slowly. “I had thought that here alone I might find a familiar greeting, but that also fails.”
“What other seemly course presents itself?” demanded the maiden unsympathetically. “How degrading a position might easily become that of the one who linked her lot with yours if all fit and proper sequences are to be reversed! What menial one might supplant her not only in your affections but also in your Rites! He had defied the Principles!” she exclaimed, as her father entered from behind a screen.

===Chinese===

随着老夫摔碎此碗，一应骨肉恩义尽数断绝；随着老夫吹灭此烛，一应过往记忆尽数湮灭；随着你迈步离去，此间唯余虚空之气，你我恩断义绝！”
“喂，无名的野汉，”二楼门栅后传来嫡母孟氏恶毒尖刻的狂笑，“奉上一口残羹冷炙为你饯行罢！”言罢自铁栅栏后狠狠掷下一枚烂无花果，复又啐了一口浓痰。
“此等败果，不过是荒淫枯木所结之脓疮罢了，”翁超头也不回掷下一句冷嘲，“且好生看顾你自家的骨肉孽种罢，毒蛇！此乃苍天借我之口所降之诅咒！”便在他话音未落之际，内宅高处陡然爆发出一声凄厉尖叫，伴随着杂乱奔走的脚步与惊恐哭嚎之声；然而翁超再未吐露一字，大步扬长而去。
翁超便这般被彻底斩断了前尘过往。他迈步走出父家大门，踏上建飞城长街之时，已然沦为一个无名无分、家徒四壁、心头遭受万般剧烈痛楚撕扯之弃民。途中偶遇熟识旧友，他皆远远颔首避之，不愿在对方知晓其落魄绝境之前去沾染虚情；然而在他心头，尚有一人被他视作绝无变节可能之磐石港湾，他遂拔足奔向迢（Tiao）家门内那座熟悉的凉亭。
迢乃是一位位分卑微之低阶小吏之女；其父乃是个屡试不第、门第平平之潦倒文人，膝下育有多名千金。昔日因看重翁超之显赫家世与锦绣前程，迢父曾极力撮合二人姻缘，常年口口声声宣称自己所看重者唯有翁超之圣洁品行与卓然见识，否则断不敢高攀这等豪门；而迢亦曾温存低语，誓言哪怕寄身残破荒塔、粗茶淡饭亦甘之如饴。然而，当迢倾听着翁超陈述这番惊天变故之际，其脸庞上渐渐浮现出一抹陌生的冷酷之色；及至翁超言毕，两人之间已然退隔出数丈鸿沟。
“一个公然践踏祖宗神圣法度、忤逆生身父母之弃民！你竟以这等面目现身于此！”她歇斯底里尖叫道，“究竟是何等肮脏之物附了你的心窍？！”
“不过是昔日曾眷顾我、而如今亦弃我而去的造化罢了，”翁超惨笑应道，“我原本以为唯有在此处尚能觅得一丝温存相待，不料竟亦是一场空。”
“事已至此，还能有何体面结局？！”少女毫无怜悯之意厉声质问，“倘若一应长幼尊卑规制尽数颠倒，委身于你之女子其处境势必沦落至何等卑贱之境！他日不知何等低贱厮役便能在你的深情与家祭仪轨中将正妻取而代之！他竟敢公然藐视《五大纲领》！”见其父自屏风后踱步而出，她高声哭诉道。

===Original===

“He has lost his inheritance,” muttered the little old man, eyeing him contemptuously. “Weng Cho,” he continued aloud, “you have played a double part and crossed our step with only half your heart. Now the past is past and the future an unwritten sheet.”
“It shall be written in vermilion ink,” replied Weng, regaining an impassive dignity; “and upon that darker half of my heart can now be traced two added names.”
He had no aim now, but instinct drove him towards the mountains, the retreat of the lost and despairing. A three days’ journey lay between. He went forward vacantly, without food and without rest. A falling leaf, as it is said, would have turned the balance of his destiny, and at the wayside village of Li-yong so it chanced. The noisome smell of burning thatch stung his face as he approached, and presently the object came into view. It was the bare cabin of a needy widow who had become involved in a lawsuit through the rapacity of a tax-gatherer. As she had the means neither to satisfy the tax nor to discharge the dues, the powerful Mandarin before whom she had been called ordered all her possessions to be seized, and that she should then be burned within her hut as a warning to others. This was the act of justice being carried out, and even as Weng heard the tale the Mandarin in question drew near, carried in his state chair to satisfy his eyes that his authority was scrupulously maintained. All those villagers who had not drawn off unseen at once fell upon their faces, so that Weng alone remained standing, doubtful what course to take.
“Ill-nurtured dog!” exclaimed the Mandarin, stepping up to him, “prostrate yourself! Do you not know that I am of the Sapphire Button, and have fivescore bowmen at my yamen, ready to do my word?” And he struck the youth across the face with a jewelled rod.
“I have only one sword, but it is in my hand,” cried Weng, reckless beneath the blow, and drawing it he at one stroke cut down the Mandarin before any could raise a hand. Then breaking in the door of the hovel he would have saved the woman, but it was too late, so he took the head and body and threw them into the fire, saying: “There, Mandarin, follow to secure justice. They shall not bear witness against you Up There in your absence.”
The chair-carriers had fled in terror, but the villagers murmured against Weng as he passed through them.

===Chinese===

“他已然被逐出门户，失了继承家业之权，”那干瘪矮小的老吏斜睨着翁超，眼中满是鄙夷之色。老头转向翁超，高声冷斥道：“翁超，你生就两副心肠，过往踏入我等门槛之际心中分明只有半片真心！如今前尘已成流水，来日方长乃是一张未曾落墨之白纸！”
“那白纸定当以朱砂鲜血写就，”翁超重拾起一身冰冷庄重之风骨，傲然答道，“而在我这残存半片冰凉心头之上，如今恰可增刻下尔等二人的名姓！”
至此他心中再无归宿，冥冥本能驱使着他的双足奔向崇山峻岭——那是绝望失路之人最后的避难所。横亘在前方的，乃是整整3日的长途跋涉。他浑浑噩噩盲目前行，水米未进，片刻未歇。古语有云：一枚飘落之枯叶足以扭转命运之天平；就在路旁一座唤作黎涌（Li-yong）的村落前，天平悄然倾覆。行近村口，一股刺鼻难闻之焚烧茅草焦臭扑面而来，旋即惨剧撞入眼帘：那是一间属于赤贫寡妇的简陋草棚，因遭遇贪婪收税恶吏敲骨吸髓而卷入官司，妇人既无余钱纳税亦无力交纳规费，审理此案之权势显赫的蓝翎县令遂下令抄没其全副破烂家当，并命将其反锁于茅屋之中活活烧死，以此儆效尤。此番所谓“明正国法”之惨剧正在推行，便在翁超听闻路人叙说之际，那位作恶的县令恰好乘着八抬大轿威风凛凛踱近，意在亲眼督查其权威是否得到严苛推行。未曾及时隐匿遁逃之村民见状皆吓得扑地跪拜，全场唯有翁超一人傲然独立，迟疑着究竟该作何处置。
“哪里来的粗野贱狗！”县令跨出大轿踱近，厉声喝道，“还不滚下叩头！难道不知本官乃是正蓝翎品秩，座下县衙豢养着整整100名神箭弓手随时听凭差遣？！”言罢挥起手中镶嵌珠玉之朝笏官杖，狠狠抽在年轻人的脸颊之上。
“在下虽无百名弓手，唯有一柄三尺利剑，然此刻正握在掌心之中！”受此奇耻大辱，翁超通身血气翻涌狂怒大喝，拔剑出鞘，趁着四下众人尚未回神之际，手起刀落一剑便将那县令劈翻斩杀于当场！他飞脚踹开茅屋破门意欲抢救民妇，奈何火势冲天为时已晚；翁超遂割下县令之头颅并其尸身，一并抛入熊熊烈火之中，朗声道：“去罢，县令老爷，快快赶赴九泉明正公道罢！免得尔等不在场之际，九天冤魂当堂指证于你！”
抬轿轿夫吓得抱头鼠窜，然而当翁超穿行于村民之间之际，一众村民反倒对他交头接耳怨声载道。

===Original===

“It was a small thing that one house and one person should be burned; now, through this, the whole village will assuredly be consumed. He was a high official and visited justice impartially on us all. It was our affair, and you, who are a stranger, have done ill.”
“I did you wrong, Mandarin,” said Weng, resuming his journey; “you took me for one of them. I pass you the parting of the woman Che, burrowers in the cow-heap called Li-yong.”
“Oi-ye!” exclaimed a voice behind, “but yonder earth-beetles haply have not been struck off the Tablets and found that a maiden with well-matched eyes can watch two ways at once, all of a morning: and thereby death through red spectacles is not that same death through blue spectacles. Things in their appointed places, noble companion.”
“Greetings, wayfarer,” said Weng, stopping. “The path narrows somewhat inconveniently hereabout. Take honourable precedence.”
“The narrower the better to defend then,” replied the stranger good-humouredly. “Whereto, also, two swords cut a larger slice than one. Without doubt fivescore valiant bowmen will soon be a-ranging when they hear that the enemy goes upon two feet, and then ill befall who knows not the passes.” As he spoke an arrow, shot from a distance, flew above their heads.
“Why should you bear a part with me, and who are you who know these recent things?” demanded Weng doubtfully.
“I am one of many, we being a branch of that great spreading lotus the Triad, though called by the tillers here around the League of Tomb-Haunters, because we must be sought in secret places. The things I have spoken I know because we have many ears, and in our care a whisper passes from east to west and from north to south without a word being spilled.”
“And the price of your sword is that I should join the confederacy?” asked Weng thoughtfully.
“I had set out to greet you before the estimable Mandarin who is now saluting his ancestors was so inopportune as to do so,” replied the emissary. “Yet it is not to be denied that we offer an adequate protection among each other, while at the same time punishing guilt and administering a rigorous justice secretly.”
“Lead me to your meeting-place, then,” said Weng determinedly. “I have done with the outer things.”
The guide pointed to a rock, shaped like a locust’s head, which marked the highest point of the steep mountain before them.

===Chinese===

“烧死一间茅屋一条人命不过是微末小事；如今因你逞凶，全村老小势必尽遭屠灭矣！老爷乃是朝廷命官，赏罚公道待我等一视同仁。此乃吾等乡里私事，你这外乡人横插一手，委实造下滔天大祸！”
“在下错怪了县令老爷，”翁超拔足重踏前程，冷笑道，“他竟将我错认作尔等同类！且将孟氏妇人赠我的临别箴言转赠尔等，一群深掘于黎涌牛粪堆里的地鳖虫！”
“哎呀呀！”身后忽传来一道戏谑的嗓音，“然而前方的地鳖虫想来尚未被逐出族谱牌位，亦未曾亲历一位生就对称美眸的妙龄佳人在大清早同时脚踏两条船之惨变呢；正因如此，透过红晶眼镜目睹死亡，与透过蓝晶眼镜领受黄泉，自非同一码事也。世间万物各安其位，尊贵的高朋同道。”
“久违了，过路客，”翁超驻足沉声道，“此地山径狭窄逼仄，尊驾且先行一步。”
“山径越狭，自当越利于坚守防范，”陌客和颜悦色地应道，“况且，双剑合璧所斩落之肉块，终归大过单打独斗。毫无疑问，待闻听仇寇生着两只脚板大摇大摆逃窜，100名勇武弓手势必倾巢而出漫山搜捕；届时若是不谙山间幽僻关隘，下场恐将凶险万分呢。”便在他言语之间，远处骤然射来一记流矢，带着尖啸自两人头顶破空掠过。
“尊驾因何愿与在下同涉险境？尊驾又是何方神圣，竟对在下近来变故了如指掌？”翁超将信将疑质问道。
“在下不过是千千万万同道中的一员，我等乃是名震四海的‘红莲会’（三合会）之一脉，虽说周遭农夫村夫皆唤我等作‘盗墓客同盟’，盖因寻访我等必须深潜于幽冥秘境。在下方才所言之诸般底细之所以尽知，皆因我等生有无数顺风灵耳；在吾帮严密看护之下，半句耳语自东传至西、由北达至南，绝无走漏半字风声之虞。”
“尊驾手中这柄宝剑的身价，莫非便是要逼迫在下纳投名状入会不成？”翁超若有所思地探询道。
“早在适才那位正前往地府拜谒先祖的尊贵县令大人不识时务横加阻拦之前，在下便已奉命启程恭迎尊驾矣，”密使答道，“然而无可否认的是，我等同侪手足之间不仅能提供万无一失之庇佑护持，更能在暗中惩奸除恶、明正严苛铁血之公道。”
“既然如此，且速速引我去往尔等总舵聚义之所罢，”翁超决然道，“凡尘俗世之一切纷扰，在下早已恩断义绝。”
引路向导抬手指向前方一座险峻高耸之峰峦，峰顶兀立着一块酷似蝗虫头颅之奇绝巨石。

===Original===

Soon the fertile lowlands ended and they passed beyond the limit of the inhabitable region. Still ascending they reached the Tiger’s High Retreat, which defines the spot where even the animal kind turn back and where watercourses cease to flow. Beyond this the most meagre indication of vegetable sustenance came to an end, and thenceforward their passage was rendered more slow and laborious by frequent snowstorms, barriers of ice, and sudden tempests which strove to hurl them to destruction. Nevertheless, by about the hour of midnight they reached the rock shaped like a locust’s head, which stood in the wildest and most inaccessible part of the mountain, and masked the entrance to a strongly-guarded cave. Here Weng suffered himself to be blindfolded, and being led forward he was taken into the innermost council. Closely questioned, he professed a spontaneous desire to be admitted into their band, to join in their dangers and share their honours; whereupon the oath was administered to him, the passwords and secret signs revealed, and he was bound from that time forth, under the bonds of a most painful death and torments in the afterworld, to submerge all passions save those for the benefit of their community, and to cherish no interests, wrongs or possessions that did not affect them all alike.
For the space of seven years Weng remained about the shadow of the mountain, carrying out, together with the other members of the band, the instructions which from time to time they received from the higher circles of the Society, as well as such acts of retributive justice as they themselves determined upon, and in this quiet and unostentatious manner maintaining peace and greatly purifying the entire province. In this passionless subservience to the principles of the Order none exceeded him; yet at no time have men been forbidden to burn joss-sticks to the spirit of the destinies, and who shall say?
At the end of seven years the first breath from out of the past reached Weng (or Thang, as he had announced himself to be when cast out nameless). One day he was summoned before the chief of their company and a mission laid upon him.
“You have proved yourself to be capable and sincere in the past, and this matter is one of delicacy,” said the leader. “Furthermore, it is reported that you know something of the paths about Kien-fi?”

===Chinese===

未消多时，肥沃平川尽抛脑后，两人穿越了人间烟火之绝境。一路拾级盘旋而上，行抵被唤作“虎跳绝顶”（Tiger’s High Retreat）的险隘关口——此地乃是连飞禽走兽皆裹足不前、山涧飞泉亦彻底冻结绝流之极境。越过此界，连最微末顽强之苔原植被亦荡然无存；自此而后，漫天呼啸之风雪、万丈玄冰之险阻，以及骤然卷起意欲将生灵撕碎抛落深渊之狂暴罡风，令两人的步履越发迟滞艰辛。即便如此，在约莫午夜子时钟鸣之刻，两人终于攀抵那块酷似蝗虫头颅之巨石脚下——此石傲立于群山最险峻荒绝之绝巅，巧夺天工遮蔽着一座设防森严之幽深洞窟门户。在此处，翁超顺从地任由旁人以黑布蒙上双眼，引颈受牵，被一路引入深邃内堂的核心长老议事大厅。面对诸位首领之严苛盘诘，翁超由衷表白了自愿投身帮会、共担患难共享荣华之坚决夙愿；于是，帮规血誓当堂加身，切口密语与隐秘暗号尽数传授，他自此立下毒誓，倘若背叛甘受世间最惨烈之酷刑斩杀与九幽万劫不复之磨难：誓将除却造福帮会大业之外的一应个人私欲尽数深埋泯灭，绝不怀抱任何不能与阖帮手足休戚与共之私仇、私利抑或私产。
在整整7年光景之中，翁超始终盘桓隐匿在这座大山的阴影之下；他与帮中众手足一道，严苛践行着自帮会上层核心不时传达之密令，兼以推行他们自身审度决断之惩恶扬善诛戮行动，以这般静默无声、毫不张扬之雷霆手腕，肃清了阖省奸佞，庇佑了一方黎庶平安。在这等毫无私情、绝对顺从于帮规戒律的风骨操守上，阖帮上下无人能出其右；然而自古以来，世人从不曾被禁绝向掌管天命之神明焚香祈愿，谁又能参透造化之深意？
7年期满之际，来自前尘过往的第一缕微风终于吹拂至翁超（抑或自其被逐出门户、沦为无名弃民之时便自报化名的“唐”Thang）耳畔。某一日，他被宣召至本堂首领案前，一桩千钧重任轰然降临肩头。
“昔日诸般公干之中，你已然印证了自身之干练与忠忱，而此番任务格外幽微机密，”首领言道，“再者，据报你对建飞城周遭之一应径陌道路皆了如指掌？”

===Original===

“There is not a forgotten turn within those paths by which I might stumble in the dark,” replied Weng, striving to subdue his mind.
“See that out of so poignant a memory no more formidable barrier than a forgotten path arises,” said the leader, observing him closely. “Know you, then a house bearing as a sign the figure of a golden ibis?”
“Truly; I have noted it,” replied Weng, changing his position, so that he now leaned against a rock. “There dwelt an old man of some lower official rank, who had no son but many daughters.”
“He has Passed, and one of those﻿—Tiao by name,” said the other, referring to a parchment﻿—“has schemingly driven out the rest and held the patrimony. Crafty and ambitious, she has of late married a high official who has ever been hostile to ourselves. Out of a private enmity the woman seeks the lives of two who are under our most solemn protection, and now uses her husband’s wealth and influence to that end. It is on him that the blow must fall, for men kill only men, and she, having no son, will then be discredited and impotent.”
“And concerning this official?” asked Weng.
“It has not been thought prudent to speak of him by name,” replied the chief. “Stricken with a painful but not dangerous malady he has retired for a time to the healthier seclusion of his wife’s house, and there he may be found. The woman you will know with certainty by a crescent scar﻿—above the right eye.”
“Beneath the eye,” corrected Weng instantly.
“Assuredly, beneath: I misread the sign,” said the head, appearing to consult the scroll. “Yet, out of a keen regard for your virtues, Thang, let me point a warning that it is antagonistic to our strict rule to remember these ancient scars too well. Further, in accordance with that same esteem, do not stoop too closely nor too long to identify the mark. By our pure and exacting standard no high attainment in the past can justify defection. The pains and penalties of failure you well know.”
“I bow, chieftain,” replied Weng acquiescently.
“It is well,” said the chief. “Your strategy will be easy. To cure this lord’s disorder a celebrated physician is even now travelling from the Capital towards Kien-fi. A day’s journey from that place he will encounter obstacles and fall into the hands of those who will take away his robes and papers.

===Chinese===

“城中便是一条被世人遗忘之幽僻暗巷，在下亦能在漆黑夜色中闭目穿行而无绊跌之虞，”翁超竭力平抑着胸臆间翻江倒海之波澜，垂首答道。
“切莫教如此刺骨锥心之记忆，滋生出比被遗忘之幽巷更为险恶的障壁，”首领目光如炬细细打量着他，沉声道，“你可知晓城中有一处挂着金鹮（golden ibis）徽记之府邸？”
“知晓，在下昔年曾留意过，”翁超变换了站姿，倚靠在身侧的一处岩壁上，“彼处曾居有一位位分卑微之低阶老吏，膝下无子，唯育有多名千金。”
“那老吏早已下世；而其中一名女眷——唤作迢（Tiao）者，”首领翻阅着手头一卷羊皮密卷，续道，“凭着机巧心机将一众姊妹尽数驱逐出门，独霸了这份家业。此妇狡黠贪婪野心勃勃，近来竟改嫁给一位向来与吾帮势同水火之当朝显贵。出于一桩私人私怨，这毒妇处心积虑意欲谋害受吾帮最神圣誓约庇佑之两条性命，如今正借用其夫君之万贯家财与滔天权势推行此番毒计。吾帮之雷霆一击必须落在其夫君头上——盖因大丈夫只杀汉子不斩妇孺，且此妇既无子嗣，一旦丧夫势必威信扫地无能为力。”
“关于这位显贵大人……？”翁超探询道。
“帮中深觉眼下挑明其名讳尚不合时宜，”首领答道，“此人近来身染重疾，痛苦万分却无性命之忧，遂暂时退隐至其新妇府中幽居将养，尊驾往彼处自能寻得其踪迹。至于那妇人，你凭其右眼上方一道新月形伤疤，定能一眼辨识分明。”
“伤疤生在眼眶下方，”翁超脱口纠正道。
“正是，生在眼下；乃是老夫适才看岔了密卷，”首领佯装查验卷轴，目光却意味深长，“然而念及对唐堂主品行武功之由衷器重，老夫不得不严词警诫：将这些前尘旧疤记得过于真切，可是大违吾帮铁律之事！再者，同样念在对阁下之器重，届时切莫俯身太近、凝视太久去验看这道记号。依吾帮纯洁严苛之至高纲领，昔日立下何等滔天功勋，亦断难宽宥临阵背叛之罪愆。违令受挫之万劫酷刑，阁下心中自当明镜一般。”
“谨遵法旨，帮主，”翁超神色肃穆躬身应命。
“善哉，”首领点头道，“行刺之策谋划周详，易如反掌。为医治这位贵人之痼疾，京师一位名动天下之神医此刻正星夜兼程赶赴建飞城。距该城尚有一日脚程之处，神医途中必逢阻难，落入吾帮手足之中，其衣冠行头与文牒脉案尽遭缴获。

===Original===

About the same place you will meet one with a bowl on the roadside who will hail you, saying, ‘Charity, out of your superfluity, noble mandarin coming from the north!’ To him you will reply, ‘Do mandarins garb thus and thus and go afoot? It is I who need a change of raiment and a chair; aye, by the token of the Locust’s Head!’ He will then lead you to a place where you will find all ready and a suitable chair with trusty bearers. The rest lies beneath your grinding heel. Prosperity!”
Weng prostrated himself and withdrew. The meeting by the wayside befell as he had received assurance﻿—they who serve the Triad do not stumble﻿—and at the appointed time he stood before Tiao’s door and called for admission. He looked to the right and the left as one who examines a new prospect, and among the azalea flowers the burnished roof of the summerhouse glittered in the sun.
“Lucky omens attend your coming, benevolence,” said the chief attendant obsequiously; “for since he sent for you an unpropitious planet has cast its influence upon our master, so that his power languishes.”
“Its malignity must be controlled,” said Weng, in a feigned voice, for he recognized the one before him. “Does any watch?”
“Not now,” replied the attendant; “for he has slept since these two hours. Would your graciousness have speech with the one of the inner chamber?”
“In season perchance. First lead me to your lord’s side and then see that we are undisturbed until I reappear. It may be expedient to invoke a powerful charm without delay.”
In another minute Weng stood alone in the sick man’s room, between them no more barrier than the silk-hung curtains of the couch. He slid down his right hand and drew a keen-edged knife; about his left he looped the even more fatal cord; then advancing with a noiseless step he pulled back the drapery and looked down. It was the moment for swift and silent action; nothing but hesitation and delay could imperil him, yet in that supreme moment he stepped back, released the curtain from his faltering grasp and, suffering the weapons to fall unheeded to the floor, covered his face with his hands, for lying before him he had seen the outstretched form, the hard contemptuous features, of his father.
Yet most solemnly alienated from him in every degree.

===Chinese===

在同一处地界，道旁自会有一名托着行乞泥碗之人向你高声唱喏：‘行行好罢，自北面而来的尊贵钦差大人，求赏几文余钱！’你须当场答道：‘朝廷钦差岂会着此行头徒步而行？正是在下急需换一身官袍并乘轿而行，然也，凭着蝗虫头（Locust’s Head）为信记！’彼时此人自会引你至一处密所，官袍文牒一应俱全，且备有忠心手足充作轿夫之体面官轿。余下大业，全凭尊驾铁足碾碎仇寇矣。马到成功！”
翁超伏地叩首，肃然退下。道旁相会之机缘一如预言分毫不差——侍奉三合会之信使向无失手——在既定时辰，翁超一身名医官袍稳稳肃立在迢的府邸门前，叩门求见。他如审视全新景致般左右环顾，在漫山杜鹃花海簇拥之中，庭院凉亭被擦拭得锃亮的铜顶在正午烈日下灼灼生辉。
“吉星高照庇佑神医大驾光临，”迎出门来的管家极尽阿谀谄媚道，“自打遣使相请之后，忽有一颗凶煞灾星降祸于我家老爷天庭，以致老爷神气衰竭，痛苦不堪。”
“此番邪气必须立加扼制，”翁超运起伪装变幻之嗓音沉声道，盖因他一眼便认出了面前的家仆，“榻前可有旁人伺候？”
“目下并无旁人，”管家应道，“老爷服药后昏睡已有整整2个时辰了。不知神医大人可愿先移步内室，与我家大夫人叙话？”
“待诊毕之后自有分晓。且速速引老夫至贵主人榻前，随后严加看守门庭，老夫未曾出房之前休教任何人擅入惊扰。老夫须即刻施展独门镇魂神符，刻不容缓。”
转瞬之间，翁超已然孤身一人伫立在病榻之前；两人之间横亘阻隔的，唯有一副绣着暗纹的云锦床幔。他右手顺着袍袖悄然滑下，拔出了一柄寒光凛冽之利刃；左手腕间则牢牢套挽着那道更为致命绝魂之绞索；随后他迈着无声之死神步履欺身向前，抬手猛然掀开罗帐，俯首凝视。此乃电光石火、杀伐决断之瞬息；唯有迟疑游移方能置自身于万劫不复之地；然而便在这决断关头，他却浑身剧颤踉跄后退数步，颤抖的手指松脱了床幔，任凭兵刃绞索跌落泥地亦充耳不闻，他痛苦地以双手死死捂住了自己的面孔——盖因横卧在眼前的躯骸，那僵硬、轻蔑而威严的面容，赫然正是他的生身严父吴齐！
然而这又是与他在人伦法理上早已彻底断绝一切渊源之形骸！

===Original===

By Wu Chi’s own acts every tie of kinship had been effaced between them: the bowl had been broken, the taper blown out, empty air had filled his place. Wu Chi acknowledged no memory of a son; he could claim no reverence as a father.﻿ ﻿… Tiao’s husband.﻿ ﻿… Then he was doubly childless.﻿ ﻿… The woman and her seed had withered, as he had prophesied.
On the one hand stood the Society, powerful enough to protect him in every extremity, yet holding failure as treason; most terrible and inexorable towards set disobedience. His body might find a painless escape from their earthly torments, but by his oaths his spirit lay in their keeping to be punished through all eternity.
That he was no longer Wu Chi’s son, that he had no father﻿—this conviction had been strong enough to rule him in every contingency of life save this. By every law of men and deities the ties between them had been dissolved, and they stood as a man and man; yet the salt can never be quite washed out of seawater.
For a time which ceased to be hours or minutes, but seemed as a fragment broken off eternity, he stood, motionless but most deeply racked. With an effort he stooped to take the cord, and paused again; twice he would have seized the dagger, but doubt again possessed him. From a distant point of the house came the chant of a monk singing a prayer and beating upon a wooden drum. The rays of the sun falling upon the gilded roof in the garden again caught his eyes; nothing else stirred.
“These in their turn have settled great issues lightly,” thought Weng bitterly. “Must I wait upon an omen?”
“… submitting oneself to purifying scars,” droned the voice far off; “propitiating if need be by even greater self-inflictions﻿ ﻿…”
“It suffices,” said Weng dispassionately, and picking up the knife he turned to leave the room.
At the door he paused again, but not in an arising doubt. “I will leave a token for Tiao to wear as a jest,” was the image that had sprung from his new abasement, and taking a sheet of parchment he quickly wrote thereon: “A wave has beat from that distant shore to this, and now sinks in the unknown depths.”
Again he stepped noiselessly to the couch, drew the curtain and dropped the paper lightly on the form. As he did so his breath stopped; his fingers stiffened.

===Chinese===

凭借着吴齐当年的决绝之举，他们父子之间的一切血脉纲常早已被彻底抹杀荡尽：饭碗已然摔碎，香烛已然吹灭，虚空之气早已填补了他的位置。吴齐生前拒绝承认尘世间尚有此子，自然亦无权领受身为严父应得之人伦敬畏……迢的夫君……如此说来，严父竟落得个断子绝孙之后场……那个孟氏毒妇与其孽种已然如翁超当年所诅咒那般枯萎横死矣。
在一面，乃是权势通天、能在任何绝境中庇佑于他、然而却将临阵退缩视作大逆叛国之铁血帮会；对于抗命不遵之叛徒，帮规处置之惨烈冷酷天下皆知。他的肉身或许能寻得痛快一死以避凡尘酷刑，然而依其立下之血誓，他的三魂七魄将永生永世受拘于帮会宗坛，领受无休止之幽冥熬炼。
自己已不再是吴齐之子，自己在这天地间本无生父——这份信念曾强大到足以主宰他经历人世间除眼前这桩死局之外的一切风浪。依人间王法与九天神律，他们之间的父子骨肉早已冰消瓦解，他们此刻不过是两个萍水相逢之凡夫俗子；然而，盐分却永远无法自海水之中彻底淘洗干净。
在一段漫长得不再受时辰或刻度度量、宛若自永恒之中生生掰扯下一截碎片般的死寂时光里，他凝固肃立在原地，通身遭受着最酷烈的撕心裂肺之折磨。他挣扎着俯身欲拾起那道绞索，却再度僵立止步；他接连两次意欲攥起那柄匕首，然而滔天疑惧再度摄住了他的心魄。自宅院极遥远之处，隐隐飘来一名游方和尚敲击木鱼诵念梵呗的悠远经声。正午烈日穿透窗棂，再度洒在后花园凉亭那镀金的鎏金铜顶之上，晃痛了他的双目；天地间别无一丝动静。
“当年正是这些物事，轻巧断送了我的一生，”翁超满腹苦涩暗忖道，“难道我如今竟要坐等神明之凶兆裁断么？”
“……领受洗心涤虑之戒疤创痕，”远处和尚木鱼声单调回荡；“若有必要，纵施以更惨烈之皮肉自残以禳解宿怨……”
“足够了，”翁超面无表情淡淡自语道；他弯腰拾起利刃，转身欲行离去。
行抵房门门首，他再度止步，然而心头已无半点游移疑窦。“且留下一道信物，权当赏给迢穿戴作笑柄罢，”自他那全新的万念俱灰深渊之中，骤然升腾起这样一个念头；他抽出一张素白羊皮纸，提笔在其上疾书一行大字：“巨浪自那遥远彼岸拍打至此，如今终沉入幽冥不测之深渊。”
他再度迈着无声步履欺近病榻，掀开罗帐，将那张字条轻轻抛落在锦被尸骸之上。便在他手落纸下的那一刹那，他的呼吸骤然屏息，指尖瞬间僵硬如铁。

===Original===

Cautiously, on one knee, he listened intently, lightly touched the face; then recklessly taking a hand he raised the arm and suffered it to fall again. No power restrained it; no alertness of awakening life came into the dull face. Wu Chi had already Passed Beyond.

===Chinese===

他单膝跪地，极尽审慎地凝神侧耳倾听，随后以指尖轻触那张枯槁的面孔；紧接着他不顾一切抓住一只手掌，抬起严父的手臂，任其自行滑落。没有丝毫筋肉之力阻遏其下坠；那张灰败僵滞的面容上，更未泛起半丝大梦初醒之生机。吴齐早已先他一步，羽化归天辞世矣。

## VII Not Concerned with Any Particular Attribute of Those Who Are Involved / VII 无关涉局诸君特异品性
===Original===

Unendurable was the intermingling of hopes and fears with which Kai Lung sought the shutter on the next occasion after the avowal of Hwa-mei’s devoted strategy. While repeatedly assuring himself that it would have been better to submit to piecemeal slicing without a protesting word rather than that she should incur so formidable a risk, he was compelled as often to admit that when once her mind had formed its image no effort on his part would have held her back. Doubtless Hwa-mei readily grasped the emotion that would possess the one whose welfare was now her chief concern, for without waiting to gum her hair or to gild her lips she hastened to the spot beneath the wall at the earliest moment that Kai Lung could be there.
“Seven marble tombstones are lifted from off my chest!” exclaimed the storyteller when he could greet her. “How did your subterfuge proceed, and with what satisfaction was the history of Weng Cho received?”
“That,” replied Hwa-mei modestly, “will provide the matter for an autumn tale, when seated around a pine-cone fire. In the meanwhile this protracted ordeal takes an ambiguous bend.”
“To what further end does the malignity of the ill-made Ming-shu now shape itself? Should it entail a second peril to your head﻿—”
“The one whom you so justly name fades for a moment out of our concern. Burdened with a secret mission he journeys to Hing-poo, nor does the Mandarin Shan Tien hold another court until the day of his return.”
“That gives a breathing space of time to our ambitions?”
“So much is assured. Yet even in that a subtle danger lurks. Certain contingencies have become involved in the recital of your admittedly ingenious stories which the future unfolding of events may not always justify. For instance, the very speculative Shan Tien, casting his usual moderate limit to the skies, has accepted the Luminous Insect as a beckoning omen, and immersed himself deeply in the chances of every candidate bearing the name of Lao, Ting, Li, Tzu, Sung, Chu, Wang or Chin. Should all these fail incapably at the trials a very undignified period in the Mandarin’s general manner of expressing himself may intervene.”
“Had the time at the disposal of this person been sufficiently enlarged he would not have omitted the various maxims arising from the tale,” admitted Kai Lung, with a shadow of remorse.

===Chinese===

在华梅（Hwa-mei）坦陈其舍身相救之策后，凯龙（Kai Lung）怀着百感交集、难以言表的期冀与惶恐，于次日获准放风之际再度来到木窗前。虽然他一再在心中告诫自己，与其让她冒这等天大奇险，倒不如任由刽子手将自己千刀万剐凌迟碎剐而绝无半句怨言；但他亦不得不屡屡承认，这位佳人一旦心意已决，凭他一己之力断难将其阻拦。华梅显然深知此时此刻这位令她魂牵梦萦之人的心境；她甚至顾不得梳理发髻涂抹唇脂，便在凯龙所能抵达的最早一刻，匆匆赶赴高墙下那处幽闭角落。
“压在小人心头的7块大理石墓碑总算轰然挪开了！”说书人一见佳人便忍不住惊呼，“不知小娘子巧施妙计情形如何？善天县令对那翁超（Weng Cho）的故事听得可还惬意？”
“此间细末，”华梅谦逊答道，“留待深秋时节围坐松果篝火之旁再作长谈罢。眼下这桩旷日持久的磨难，偏偏又生出一桩暧昧难明的转折。”
“莫非那形容猥琐的明恕（Ming-shu）又在酝酿何等歹毒算计？难道他又想对小娘子的项上人头生出第二次危难——”
“你适才所唤之人，眼下倒暂且无暇顾及咱们了。他奉了一道密令远赴兴浦（Hing-poo）公干，在其回衙复命之前，县令善天（Shan Tien）概不开堂问案。”
“如此说来，咱们的宏愿倒有了喘息之机？”
“眼前确是如此。然而即便是这喘息之中，亦暗伏着微妙凶险。在你那些确实精妙绝伦的故事叙说之间，隐隐牵涉出若干机缘偶合；而未来的事态演变，却未必总能印证那些故事中的吉兆。举个例来说，那位生性极喜投机博彩的善天县令，竟将平生适度节制之念抛至九霄云外，将那萤光虫（Luminous Insect）视作上天垂示的明朗吉兆，竟将大把银钱孤注一掷押在所有姓老、丁、李、子、宋、楚、王或金的赴考举子身上。万一这些考生在乡试大比中全数名落孙山，这位县令大人的谈吐做派，怕是免不了要经历一段极不体面、暴躁失态的恶劣光景。”
“倘若当日留给在下的光阴再充裕宽裕几分，小人本绝不致遗漏那则故事所引申出的种种处世箴言警句，”凯龙带着一丝悔意坦承道。

===Original===

“That suited to the need of a credulous and ill-balanced mind would doubtless be the proverb: ‘He who believes in gambling will live to sell his sandals.’ It is regrettable if the well-intending Mandarin took the wrong one. Fortunately another moon will fade before the results are known﻿—”
“In the meantime,” continued the maiden, indicating by a glance that what she had to relate was more essential to the requirements of the moment than anything he was saying: “Shan Tien is by no means indisposed towards your cause. Your unassuming attitude and deep research have enlarged your wisdom in his eyes. Tomorrow he will send for you to lean upon your well-stored mind.”
“Is the emergency one for which any special preparation is required?” questioned Kai Lung.
“That is the message of my warning. Of late a company of grateful friends has given the Mandarin an inlaid coffin to mark the sense of their indebtedness, the critical nature of the times rendering the gift peculiarly appropriate. Thus provided, Shan Tien has cast his eyes around to secure a burial robe worthy of the casket. The merchants proffer many, each endowed with all the qualities, but meanwhile doubts arise, and now Shan Tien would turn to you to learn what is the true and ancient essential of the garment, and wherein its virtue should reside.”
“The call will not find me inept,” replied Kai Lung. “The story of Wang Ho﻿—”
“It is enough,” exclaimed the maiden warningly. “The time for wandering together in the garden of the imagination has not yet arrived. Ming-shu’s feet are on a journey, it is true, but his eyes are doubtless left behind. Until a like hour tomorrow gladdens our expectant gaze, farewell!”
On the following day, at about the stroke of the usual court, Li-loe approached Kai Lung with a grievous look.
“Alas, manlet,” he exclaimed, “here is one direct from the presence of our high commander, requiring you against his thumb-signed bond. Go you must, and that alone, whether it be for elevation on a tree or on a couch. Out of an insatiable friendship this one would accompany you, were it possible, equally to hold your hand if you are to die or hold your cup if you are to feast. Yet touching that same cask of hidden wine there is still time﻿—”
“Cease, mooncalf,” replied Kai Lung reprovingly. “This is but an eddy on the surface of a moving stream.

===Chinese===

“最切合那等轻信偏执、心智失衡之徒的警句，莫过于那句老话：‘嗜赌如命者，终将沦落到鬻履度日。’倘若本心尚善的县令大人偏偏误采歧途，委实令人扼腕叹息。好在金榜题名尚需待又一月月色消褪方见分晓——”
“然而在此时日之间，”少女接言道，眼波流转示意自己所要陈述之事远比他此刻空谈更加紧迫要紧：“善天对你的处境绝无半分不假辞色。你谦逊内敛的品格与渊博深厚的学识，反倒令你在他眼中更添大智慧。明日他便会差人传唤你入堂，以倚仗你满腹经纶指点迷津。”
“莫非眼前又有何等急务，需在下做甚特殊筹谋预备不成？”凯龙探询道。
“此正乃小女子特来示警之原委。近来有一干感恩戴德的故旧好友，赠予县令大人一口精工镶嵌的寿材棺木，以表铭感五内之情；值此多事之秋，这等厚礼委实显得格外合时合宜。得此良棺，善天遂四下探寻物色一件堪与这口华匣相配的得体殉葬袍（Burial Robe）。城中绸缎商贾争相进献，各称其袍兼具诸般神妙功德，然而县令大人心中反倒疑窦丛生。如今善天正要向你求教，探知这寿衣在古制中究竟有何真正要义与玄机，其护佑功德究竟应当蕴于何处。”
“大人垂询，小人断不至笨拙失对，”凯龙应道，“那王浩（Wang Ho）的故事——”
“休要多言，”少女警觉制止道，“眼下尚不是你我二人漫步神游于遐想花园的时节。明恕虽已启程远行，其耳目却必定仍留在此处暗中窥伺。待到明日同一时辰再来抚慰你我殷切期盼之凝睇罢，就此暂别！”
翌日，约莫正值平日开堂的时辰，李罗（Li-loe）带着满面戚容踱向凯龙。
“呜呼哀哉，小可怜，”他长吁短叹道，“高堂统领大人身侧的亲随直接拿了盖有画押指印的拘票前来提你。不论此去是将你吊上悬崖枯树还是奉为座上宾客，你皆是非去不可，且只能孤身前往。若得可能，凭在下这番深厚无厌的交情，无论你是赴死受刑在下执手相送，还是赴宴享乐在下为你把盏斟酒，在下都愿舍命相随。然而提及那一坛暗藏的陈年美酒，眼下尚有片刻功夫——”
“打住罢，你这憨货，”凯龙斥责道，“这不过是奔流长河水面上荡起的一圈微澜罢了。

===Original===

It comes, it goes; and the waters press on as before.”
Then Kai Lung, neither bound nor wearing the wooden block, was led into the presence of Shan Tien, and allowed to seat himself upon the floor as though he plied his daily trade.
“Sooner or later it will certainly devolve upon this person to condemn you to a violent end,” remarked the farseeing Mandarin reassuringly. “In the ensuing interval, however, there is no need for either of us to dwell upon what must be regarded as an unpleasant necessity.”
“Yet no crime has been committed, beneficence,” Kai Lung ventured to protest; “nor in his attitude before your virtuous self has this one been guilty of any act of disrespect.”
“You have shown your mind to be both wide and deep, and suitably lined,” declared Shan Tien, dexterously avoiding the weightier part of the storyteller’s plea. “A question now arises as to the efficacy of embroidered coffin cloths, and wherein their potent merit lies. Out of your well-stored memory declare your knowledge of this sort, conveying the solid information in your usual palatable way.”
“I bow, High Excellence,” replied Kai Lung. “This concerns the story of Wang Ho.”

===Chinese===

水波来去匆匆，江水依然奔流不息。”
于是凯龙既未受绳索捆绑，亦未套木枷枷锁，便这般被径直引至善天的内室私邸之中。那位显赫大员正倚榻而坐，膝上堆满了用厚厚锦缎织就的华丽长袍样品；而在其榻前数步之外，两个头戴低垂便帽、腰束严实绦带的身影正毕恭毕敬垂首侍立在旁。
“退下，李罗，”县令发话道，“待此地事情了结，自会命人将这说书人发还于你。至于你们二位——”善天朝着两名商人挥了挥手，二人随即行大礼拜倒在地，“暂且到前院中候着，待本官听罢这饱学之士的见解后再作定夺。”
待两名商贾退下，善天方转向凯龙，神色间流露出一丝不易察觉的敬意。
“说书人，”县令大人沉声道，“本官近得一口精绝寿材，然选配殉葬袍一事却令本官颇费踌躇。商贾各执一词，或言当求金丝织锦以显尊荣，或言当取五色符纹以御邪祟。你遍历九州，胸罗万卷，依古圣先贤之训，这等关乎万年寿寝之重器，究竟有何精义？”
“大人明察万里，”凯龙躬身答道，“此节奥妙，古人早已备言。正如前朝巨贾王浩与殉葬袍之旧事，其中关窍足以昭示古今天下通达之人。”

### The Story of Wang Ho and the Burial Robe / 王浩与殉葬袍的故事
===Original===

There was a time when it did not occur to anyone in this pure and enlightened Empire to question the settled and existing order of affairs. It would have been well for the merchant Wang Ho had he lived in that happy era. But, indeed, it is now no unheard-of thing for an ordinary person to suggest that customs which have been established for centuries might with advantage be changed﻿—a form of impiety which is in no degree removed from declaring oneself to be wiser or more profound than one’s ancestors! Scarcely more seemly is this than irregularity in maintaining the Tablets or observing the Rites; and how narrow is the space dividing these delinquencies from the actual crimes of overturning images, counselling rebellion, joining in insurrection and resorting to indiscriminate piracy and bloodshed.
Certainly the merchant Wang Ho would be a thousand taels wealthier today if he had fully considered this in advance. Nor would Cheng Lin﻿—but who attempts to eat an orange without first disposing of the peel, or what manner of a dwelling could be erected unless an adequate foundation be first provided?
Wang Ho, then, let it be stated, was one who had early in life amassed a considerable fortune by advising those whose intention it was to hazard their earnings in the State Lotteries as to the numbers that might be relied upon to be successful, or, if not actually successful, those at least that were not already predestined by malign influences to be absolutely incapable of success. These chances Wang Ho at first forecast by means of dreams, portents and other manifestations of an admittedly supernatural tendency, but as his name grew large and the number of his clients increased vastly, while his capacity for dreaming remained the same, he found it no less effective to close his eyes and to become inspired rapidly of numbers as they were thus revealed to him.
Occasionally Wang Ho was the recipient of an appropriate bag of money from one who had profited by his advice, but it was not his custom to rely upon this contingency as a source of income, nor did he in any eventuality return the amount which had been agreed upon (and invariably deposited with him in advance) as the reward of his inspired efforts.

===Chinese===

曾几何时，在咱们这方清明开化的大帝国里，未曾有人对天道既定的秩序与世间万象之定规妄加揣测生疑。人人皆深知自己降生之由与命中之份：或是替官家挖掘水渠，或是去前线冲锋陷阵；抑或遵奉《五大纲领》（The Five Great Principles）与列祖列宗之训诫，规行矩步度此一生；甚或在机缘与祖荫庇佑之下，做一名腰缠万贯、终日饱食无忧的富商巨贾。
在那个太平丰腴的黄金时代，在浙津（Che-t'sin）城墙外两三里处，坐落着富商王浩（Wang Ho）巍峨轩敞的宅邸庄园。这座宅第高墙环护，四周皆布有精心雕琢的镂空花窗，庭院深邃，水榭亭台鳞次栉比。王浩平生专营绸缎、珍禽羽毛、鹿茸及各类昂贵生药，家资累巨万，于远近州府皆设有号栈。
然而王浩虽家财万贯，生平却有一桩极大的隐忧与缺憾：他目不识丁，胸无点墨，连自己的名姓亦只能勉强画出一道形似折断竹竿的粗劣记号。不仅如此，随着年岁渐长，鬓角飞霜，王浩对于身后之事、尤其是如何延年益寿以享尽这泼天富贵，生出了几乎病态的痴迷与惶恐。他坚信只要备下一件合乎古法至道、透发无穷生机瑞气的无瑕殉葬袍，便能蒙蔽冥府勾魂使者之耳目，从而长保阳寿，享尽天年。

===Original===

To those who sought him in a contentious spirit, inquiring why he did not find it more profitable to secure the prizes for himself, Wang Ho replied that his enterprise consisted in forecasting the winning numbers for State Lotteries and not in solving enigmas, writing deprecatory odes, composing epitaphs or conducting any of the other numerous occupations that could be mentioned. As this plausible evasion was accompanied by the courteous display of the many weapons which he always wore at different convenient points of his attire, the incident invariably ended in a manner satisfactory to Wang Ho.
Thus positioned Wang Ho prospered, and had in the course of years acquired a waist of honourable proportions, when the unrolling course of events influenced him to abandon his lucrative enterprise. It was not that he failed in any way to become as inspired as before; indeed, with increasing practice he attained a fluency that enabled him to outdistance every rival, so that on the occasion of one lottery he afterwards privately discovered that he had predicted the success of every possible combination of numbers, thus enabling those who followed his advice (as he did not fail to announce in inscriptions of vermilion assurance) to secure﻿—among them﻿—every variety of prize offered.
But, about this time, the chief wife of Wang Ho having been greeted with amiable condescension by the chief wife of a high official of the Province, and therefrom in an almost equal manner by the wives of even higher officials, the one in question began to abandon herself to a more rapidly outlined manner of existence than formerly, and to involve Wang Ho in a like attitude, so that presently this ill-considering merchant, who but a short time before would have unhesitatingly cast himself bodily to earth on the approach of a city magistrate, now acquired the habit of alluding to mandarins in casual conversation by names of affectionate abbreviation. Also, being advised of the expediency by a voice speaking in an undertone, he sought still further to extend beyond himself by suffering his nails to grow long and obliterating his name from the public announcements upon the city walls.
In spite of this ambitious sacrifice Wang Ho could not entirely shed from his habit a propensity to associate with those requiring advice on matters involving financial transactions.

===Chinese===

面对那些心怀诘难挑剔之人登门探问：为何不凭借通天手段去买通科场主考、为自家儿孙捐纳几个功名及第的金榜探花，反而将大笔银钱耗费在结交各路贪鄙官吏的虚情假意上？王浩总是以他那一贯干练通达的言辞答道：“养育儿孙去考取功名，犹如在自家后院掘井植树：纵使历经23年严霜酷暑浇灌培植，日后亦难保这井水不干涸、树木不遭天雷劈裂。更何况一个区区七品县丞，一年俸禄不过7两白银，却要先在京师礼部花去5000两白银打点通关节。相比之下，老夫膝下虽有17个儿子，个个鲁钝如猪狗，但若能直接结识一位手握生杀大权的当朝二品大员，老夫便能在一夜之间将全省生丝官引揽入怀中。”
这番见解固然透着十足的商贾精明，但在关乎自身寿元这等大事上，王浩却全然失去了昔日的通达。那一年，王浩年届74岁高龄。每逢风雨晦明，骨节酸痛，他便疑心无常鬼差正隐于穿堂门后窃窃私语。为此，他几乎散尽千金，广招天下方士与能工巧匠，意欲访求一件真正能够令肉身不朽、令鬼神辟易的绝世殉葬袍。
然而在他身边，除了一众阿谀奉承的姬妾与愚钝不堪的子嗣，唯有一名年轻文案令他既离不开又暗存戒心——那便是负责为其打理往来书信文书的清贫西席，名唤程林（Cheng Lin）。

===Original===

He could no longer conduct enterprises which entailed many clients and the lavish display of his name, but in the society of necessitous persons who were related to others of distinction he allowed it to be inferred that he was benevolently disposed and had a greater sufficiency of taels than he could otherwise make use of. He also involved himself, for the benefit of those whom he esteemed, in transactions connected with pieces of priceless jade, jars of wine of an especially fragrant character, and pictures of reputable antiquity. In the written manner of these transactions (for it is useless to conceal the fact that Wang Ho was incapable of tracing the characters of his own name) he employed a youth whom he never suffered to appear from beyond the background. Cheng Lin is thus brought naturally and unobtrusively into the narrative.
Had Cheng Lin come into the world when a favourably disposed band of demons was in the ascendant he would certainly have merited an earlier and more embellished appearance in this written chronicle. So far, however, nothing but omens of an ill-destined obscurity had beset his career. For many years two ambitions alone had contained his mind, both inextricably merged into one current and neither with any appearance of ever flowing into its desired end. The first was to pass the examination of the fourth degree of proficiency in the great literary competitions, and thereby qualify for a small official post where, in the course of a few years, he might reasonably hope to be forgotten in all beyond the detail of being allotted every third moon an unostentatious adequacy of taels. This distinction Cheng Lin felt to be well within his power of attainment could he but set aside three uninterrupted years for study, but to do this would necessitate the possession of something like a thousand taels of silver, and Lin might as well fix his eyes upon the great sky-lantern itself.
Dependent on this, but in no great degree removed from it, was the hope of being able to entwine into that future the actuality of Hsi Mean, a very desirable maiden whom it was Cheng Lin’s practice to meet by chance on the river bank when his heavily-weighted duties for the day were over.

===Chinese===

程林虽身为王浩身侧不可或缺之人，负责执笔书写一切关涉公堂讼案、官场拜帖的文书墨迹，但他却不得不忍受这位暴发巨贾无休无止的猜忌与苛待。王浩因自身不通文墨，每次命程林草拟信函，哪怕区区10行便笺，亦要命他翻来覆去研读解说3或4个时辰之久。而到了月底结算修金之时，王浩却往往以墨锭磨损过多或纸张受潮为由，克扣克削，最终仅丢下区区25文铜钱，还口口声声宣称这25文铜钱已足够一个穷酸秀才买下数日糙米与咸芥。
不仅如此，程林与王浩府邸隔邻一位名叫敏（Mean）的端庄秀雅少女早已互许终身。然而敏的严父早有明训：若无足足100两聘银以备六礼，休想将女儿迎娶过门。程林每每抚琴长叹，自忖依眼下每月积攒数文铜钱之微薄进项，即便苦候至两鬓如霜，亦休想凑齐这笔巨款，更惶论奢望有朝一日能坐拥1000两白银之巨资以自立门户。
他再也无法像那些家底丰厚之士一样四处经商投机，只能困守在这座深宅大院之中，整日面对王浩那张因贪婪与多疑而扭曲的油腻面孔，在屈辱与绝望中苦苦寻觅脱身逆袭之良机。

===Original===

To those who will naturally ask why Cheng Lin, if really sincere in his determination, could not imperceptibly acquire even so large a sum as a thousand taels while in the house of the wealthy Wang Ho, immersed as the latter person was with the pursuit of the full face of high mandarins and further embarrassed by a profuse illiteracy, it should be sufficient to apply the warning: “Beware of helping yourself to corn from the manger of the blind mule.”
In spite of his preoccupation Wang Ho never suffered his mind to wander when sums of money were concerned, and his inability to express himself by written signs only engendered in his alert brain an ever-present decision not to be entrapped by their use. Frequently, Cheng Lin found small sums of money lying in such a position as to induce the belief that they had been forgotten, but upon examining them closely he invariably found upon them marks by which they could be recognized if the necessity arose; he therefore had no hesitation in returning them to Wang Ho with a seemly reference to the extreme improbability of the merchant actually leaving money thus unguarded, and to the lack of respect which it showed to Cheng Lin himself to expect that a person of his integrity should be tempted by so insignificant an amount. Wang Ho always admitted the justice of the reproach, but he did not on any future occasion materially increase the sum in question, so that it is to be doubted if his heart was sincere.
It was on the evening of such an incident that Lin walked with Mean by the side of the lotus-burdened Hoang-keng expressing himself to the effect that instead of lilies her hair was worthy to be bound up with pearls of a like size, and that beneath her feet there should be spread a carpet not of verdure, but of the finest Chang-hi silk, embroidered with five-clawed dragons and other emblems of royal authority, nor was Mean in any way displeased by this indication of extravagant taste on her lover’s part, though she replied:
“The only jewels that this person desires are the enduring glances of pure affection with which you, O my phoenix one, entwined the lilies about her hair, and the only carpet that she would crave would be the embroidered design created by the four feet of the two persons who are now conversing together forever henceforth walking in uninterrupted harmony.”
“Yet, alas!”

===Chinese===

若有人顺理成章探问：倘若程林真有脱贫自立之决意，为何身处巨富王浩府中——且明知主人终日汲汲于逢迎高官之面子、又目不识丁大字不识一个——却未曾设法在这经年累月间神不知鬼不觉地暗中挪借乃至巧取一笔多达1000两白银之巨款？对于这等疑窦，只需引述古训便足以释疑：“切莫试图自盲骡之食槽中盗取豆料。”
盖因王浩虽终日俗务缠身，但凡涉及银两钱财，其心智机敏警觉却从无半分涣散；且正因他无法凭文字记号表达心意，其警醒的大脑中反倒时刻绷紧一根弦，誓死不落入任何文字圈套之中。程林便屡屡在桌角案头、廊下砖缝间撞见数枚散落的碎银，其摆放之姿态宛若主人无意遗忘一般；然而程林每次细加端详，必能在银锭隐蔽之处发现暗自刻画的识别记号，一旦遗失立时便可对质拿人。于是程林毫不迟疑，每次皆恭恭敬敬将银钱捧还王浩，口中更不忘义正辞严地进言，痛陈东家这等大意遗落钱财委实荒唐不可信，且这般试探不仅是对他程林清白操守之莫大侮辱，更是把一个坐怀不乱的正人君子看扁为能被区区微末小钱所诱惑之鄙夫。王浩每次闻言皆点头称是承认责备得当，但在日后却从未见他哪怕一次实质性地增加试探之银码，由此足见其试探之心何其虚伪狡诈。
正是在又一次经历这等试探的傍晚，程林与敏相携漫步于荷香四溢的黄坑（Hoang-keng）溪畔。程林情不自禁抒怀道：在他眼中，佳人发间所佩之百合何等素淡，唯有同等大小之硕大南珠方配束挽其青丝；而她莲步之下亦不该践踏寻常凡草，而当铺陈由常州（Chang-hi）最上等丝绸织就、其上以金线密绣五爪金龙与皇家至尊图腾的华贵毡毯。敏听闻情郎这般奢华痴情之言，心中非但全无半分不悦，反而柔声应道：
“小女子所渴盼的唯一珍宝，唯有凤郎将百合缠绕于妾身发端时，眼中所流露之矢志不渝之深情凝视；而妾身所企求的唯一地毯，乃是此刻你我二人相对倾诉之际，前后相随的四足在人世间永结同心、携手并肩漫步所踏出的锦绣轨迹。”
“然而，唉！”

===Original===

exclaimed Lin, “that enchanting possibility seems to be more remotely positioned than ever. Again has the clay-souled Wang Ho, on the pretext that he can no longer make his in and out taels meet, sought to diminish the monthly inadequacy of cash with which he rewards this person’s conscientious services.”
“Undoubtedly that opaque-eyed merchant will shortly meet a revengeful fire-breathing vampire when walking alone on the edge of a narrow precipice,” exclaimed Mean sympathetically. “Yet have you pressingly laid the facts before the spirits of your distinguished ancestors with a request for their direct intervention?”
“The expedient has not been neglected,” replied Lin, “and appropriate sacrifices have accompanied the request. But even while in the form of an ordinary existence the venerable ones in question were becoming distant in their powers of hearing, and doubtless with increasing years the ineptitude has grown. It would almost seem that in the case of a person so obtuse as Wang Ho is, more direct means would have to be employed.”
“It is well said,” assented Mean, “that those who are unmoved by the threat of a vat of flaming sulphur in the Beyond, rend the air if they chance to step on a burning cinder here on earth.”
“The suggestion is a timely one,” replied Lin. “Wang Ho’s weak spot lies between his hat and his sandals. Only of late, feeling the natural infirmities of time pressing about him, he has expended a thousand taels in the purchase of an elaborate burial robe, which he wears on every fit occasion, so that the necessity for its ultimate use may continue to be remote.”
“A thousand taels!” repeated Mean. “With that sum you could﻿—”
“Assuredly. The coincidence may embody something in the nature of an omen favourable to ourselves. At the moment, however, this person has not any clear-cut perception of how the benefit may be attained.”
“The amount referred to has already passed into the hands of the merchant in burial robes?”
“Irrevocably. In the detail of the transference of actual sums of money Wang Ho walks hand in hand with himself from door to door. The pieces of silver are by this time beneath the floor of Shen Heng’s inner chamber.”
“Shen Heng?”
“The merchant in silk and costly fabrics, who lives beneath the sign of the Golden Abacus. It was from him﻿—”
“Truly. It is for him that this person’s sister Min works the finest embroideries.

===Chinese===

程林长叹道，“这般如梦似幻的美好前景，如今看来却显得比以往任何时刻都要遥不可及。那个泥胎木塑般心肠的王浩，近来竟又以收支无法相抵、亏空日甚为借口，意欲削减他平日打发在下恪尽职守之微薄铜钱修金。”
“那个瞎了眼的奸商，日后独自在逼仄断崖边缘行路之时，必定会迎头撞上一尊喷吐烈焰的索命厉鬼！”敏同仇敌忾宽慰道，“不知凤郎可曾备下祭品，将这满腹冤屈急迫呈报于列祖列宗之显灵神位前，乞求祖宗英灵直接降下天谴施以援手？”
“此法在下何尝敢有丝毫疏漏，”程林苦笑道，“亦曾备下合宜祭品伴随祷词焚香叩拜。然而那些列祖列宗当年前尚在凡尘之时，耳背失聪之症便已颇为深重；如今历经千百年冥府岁月，这等老朽昏聩之态怕是只增不减。依在下之见，对付王浩这等顽钝无耻之徒，恐怕唯有施展更为直接凌厉的人间手段方能奏效。”
“先贤有云，”敏深以为然道，“‘听闻阴司地狱有一镬沸腾硫磺烈火而面无惧色之人，若在人间脚底踩着一块燃烧木炭，立时便会痛得撕心裂肺号啕大哭。’”
“娘子此言委实一针见血，”程林点头道，“王浩全身上下最脆弱的软肋，便生在他顶门乌纱帽与足下布履之间。近来他自感年老体衰大限将至，竟一掷千金，花去整整1000两白银，四处物色并买下了一件奢华绝伦的殉葬袍；每逢初一十五吉凶之日，他便将这件寿衣套在身上四处招摇，自以为如此便能将最终入殓躺进棺木之凶煞远远驱离。”
“整整1000两白银！”敏失声惊呼，“若能得此巨款，你我便可——”
“正是如此。这等巧合冥冥之中或许正蕴含着上天赐予你我的转机吉兆。然而眼下，在下尚未参透究竟该如何从这件寿衣上谋取真金白银。”
“这笔银钱当真已然全数落入那售卖寿衣的商贾囊中了？”
“已然覆水难收。凡涉及银两交割过手之事，王浩一向事必躬亲、步步紧盯。那一锭锭白花花的银子，此刻恐怕早已深深埋入沈衡（Shen Heng）内室的地砖之下了。”
“沈衡？”
“正是城中那家挂着金算盘（the Golden Abacus）招牌、专营绸缎珍贵面料的大掌柜。王浩便是从他手中——”
“哎呀！实不相瞒，家姐小敏（Min）平日里便是专程为这位沈大掌柜刺绣最上乘精致的寿衣袍料呢。”

===Original===

Doubtless this very robe﻿—”
“It is of blue silk edged with sand pearls in a line of three depths. Felicitations on long life and a list of the most venerable persons of all times serve to remind the controlling deities to what length human endurance can proceed if suitably encouraged. These are designed in letters of threaded gold. Inferior spirits are equally invoked in characters of silver.”
“The description is sharp-pointed. It is upon this robe that the one referred to has been ceaselessly engaged for several moons. On account of her narrow span of years, no less than her nimble-jointed dexterity, she is justly esteemed among those whose wares are guaranteed to be permeated with the spirit of rejuvenation.”
“Thereby enabling the enterprising Shen Heng to impose a special detail into his account: ‘For employing the services of one who will embroider into the fabric of the robe the vital principles of youth and long-life-to-come﻿—an added fifty taels.’ Did she of your house benefit to a proportionate extent?”
Mean indicated a contrary state of things by a graceful movement of her well-arranged eyebrows.
“Not only that,” she added, “but the sordid-minded Shen Heng, on a variety of pretexts, has diminished the sum Min was to receive at the completion of the work, until that which should have required a full hand to grasp could be efficiently covered by two attenuated fingers. From this cause Min is vindictively inclined towards him and, steadfastly refusing to bend her feet in the direction of his workshop, she has, between one melancholy and another, involved herself in a dark distemper.”
As Mean unfolded the position lying between her sister Min and the merchant Shen Heng, Lin grew thoughtful, and, although it was not his nature to express the changing degrees of emotion by varying the appearance of his face, he did not conceal from Mean that her words had fastened themselves upon his imagination.
“Let us rest here a while,” he suggested presently. “That which you say, added to what I already know, may, under the guidance of a sincere mind, put a much more rainbow-like outlook on our combined future than hitherto appeared probable.”
So they composed themselves about the bank of the river, while Lin questioned her more closely as to those things of which she had spoken. Finally, he laid certain injunctions upon her for her immediate guidance.

===Chinese===

想必正是眼前这件寿衣无疑了——”
“此袍乃是用天青色上等湖绸精工裁剪而成，领口袖口边缘皆密密缝缀着三层细密如砂的滚圆东珠。其上更以金丝银线绣满‘福寿双全’之吉祥吉语；而其最深邃神妙之处，乃是沈衡立下重誓立据画押，宣称此袍在绣制之时，已由一位生辰八字纯阳、德行无瑕的贞烈处子将一股延年益寿之返老还童灵气悉数贯注其中。”
“这正是家姐小敏耗费半年心血日夜刺绣之物啊！”敏抚掌惊叹道，“然而那沈衡在结算工钱之时，非但只给了家姐几两碎银，反倒将王浩那整整1000两白银的大头尽数收入私囊。更可恶的是，沈衡为谋取更大利益，竟还在立约时巧立名目，向王浩额外索要了整整50两白银，美其名曰此乃用以担保那股返老还童灵气纯粹无瑕之‘通灵谢仪’。”
程林闻听此言，双眸之中骤然迸射出一道精明锐利的异彩。他一把拉住敏的纤纤柔荑，急切询道：
“娘子，令姐小敏近来身子骨与运道究竟如何？在那寿衣绣制将成之际，可曾发生过何等不同寻常之变故？”
敏闻言微微一怔，随即压低嗓音，面露凄然之色附耳低语道：
“凤郎有所不知。家姐小敏自幼体弱多病，积劳成疾。就在那件寿衣飞针走线落下最后一针、剪断丝线的那一刹那，她老人家便一口气没能倒上来，当场合眼‘仙逝’过去了——至少在沈衡闻讯赶来、急于将寿衣强行取走之时，家姐已然面色如纸、气绝身亡，任凭沈衡如何呼唤皆全无半分声息了。”

===Original===

Then, it being now the hour of middle light, they returned, Mean accompanying her voice to the melody of stringed wood, as she related songs of those who have passed through great endurances to a state of assured contentment. To Lin it seemed as though the city leapt forward to meet them, so narrow was the space of time involved in reaching it.
A few days later Wang Ho was engaged in the congenial occupation of marking a few pieces of brass cash before secreting them where Cheng Lin must inevitably displace them, when the person in question quietly stood before him. Thereupon Wang Ho returned the money to his inner sleeve, ineptly remarking that when the sun rose it was futile to raise a lantern to the sky to guide the stars.
“Rather is it said, ‘From three things cross the road to avoid: a falling tree, your chief and second wives whispering in agreement, and a goat wearing a leopard’s tail,’ ” replied Lin, thus rebuking Wang Ho, not only for his crafty intention, but also as to the obtuseness of the proverb he had quoted. “Nevertheless, O Wang Ho, I approach you on a matter of weighty consequence.”
“Tomorrow approaches,” replied the merchant evasively. “If it concerns the detail of the reduction of your monthly adequacy, my word has become unbending iron.”
“It is written: ‘Cho Sing collected feathers to make a garment for his canary when it began to moult,’ ” replied Lin acquiescently. “The care of so insignificant a person as myself may safely be left to the Protecting Forces, esteemed. This matter touches your own condition.”
“In that case you cannot be too specific.” Wang Ho lowered himself into a reclining couch, thereby indicating that the subject was not one for hasty dismissal, at the same time motioning to Lin that he should sit upon the floor. “Doubtless you have some remunerative form of enterprise to suggest to me?”
“Can a palsied finger grasp a proffered coin? The matter strikes more deeply at your very existence, honoured chief.”
“Alas!” exclaimed Wang Ho, unable to retain the usual colour of his appearance, “the attention of a devoted servant is somewhat like Tohen-hi Yang’s spiked throne﻿—it torments those whom it supports. However, the word has been spoken﻿—let the sentence be filled in.”
“The full roundness of your illustrious outline is as a display of coloured lights to gladden my commonplace vision,” replied Lin submissively.

===Chinese===

彼时，恰逢正午日上中天之际，二人携手折返回城；敏和着木弦琴筝的悠扬旋律引吭清歌，而程林则步履轻快若有所思，二人一路走得好不欢欣畅快。
次日清晨，程林依约来到王浩书房伺候笔墨。王浩正身披那件天青色殉葬袍在房中来回踱步，面带得色，仿佛每走一步便能从这件锦袍中汲取无尽生机一般。
“程林啊，”王浩停下脚步，颇为自得地伸展双臂炫耀道，“你且看看老夫身上这件袍服！领口这三层东珠，在晨光下泛出何等璀璨之宝气！更有那注入其中的返老还童灵气，令老夫今晨只觉筋骨舒泰，直如返老还童重回弱冠之年。老夫虽花去千金，又额外打赏了沈衡整整25两白银作为谢仪，但这笔银钱花得委实值当！”
程林闻言，脸上非但全无半分谄媚赞许之色，反而缓缓垂下头颅，面露深沉凝重与痛心疾首之悲戚。
“东家，”程林长叹一声，语气中满是难以遏制的哀惋，“小人侍奉东家多年，深蒙东家厚恩。有些逆耳忠言，小人纵使甘冒触怒东家之奇险，今日亦不得不拼死直言相告了。”
王浩见状，心头猛地一沉，脸上的得色瞬间凝固，额头冷汗涔涔而下，急声喝问道：
“你这酸秀才休要在此装神弄鬼！有话快讲，有屁快放！莫非这件宝袍有何不妥不成？”
程林缓缓抬起头，目光直视王浩那双因惊恐而瞪大的鼠目，压低声音幽幽说道：
“东家昨夜子时，当窗外更夫敲响九击更鼓之时，东家身披此袍沉睡之际，可曾察觉寝榻四周阴风阵阵？可曾发觉那领口原本温润洁白的东珠之上，隐隐泛起一层幽暗发青之死气？东家若是自感筋骨轻健，恐非返老还童之生机，而是那黄泉引路之阴气已然悄然渗入骨髓矣！据在下昨夜占验星象所得，那金算盘沈衡卖给东家的，哪里是什么延年益寿之宝物，分明是一领暗藏横死怨气之凶兆夺命煞衣！沈衡私吞了东家整整160两白银之定金与尾款，却将一桩天大祸事硬生生扣在了东家头上！”

===Original===

“Admittedly of late, however, an element of dampness has interfered with the brilliance of the display.”
“Speak clearly and regardless of polite evasion,” commanded Wang Ho. “My internal organs have for some time suspected that hostile influences were at work. For how long have you noticed this, as it may be expressed, falling off?”
“My mind is as refined crystal before your compelling glance,” admitted Lin. “Ever since it has been your custom to wear the funeral robe fashioned by Shen Heng has your noble shadow suffered erosion.”
This answer, converging as it did upon the doubts that had already assailed the merchant’s satisfaction, convinced him of Cheng Lin’s discrimination, while it increased his own suspicion. He had for some little time found that after wearing the robe he invariably suffered pangs that could only be attributed to the influence of malign and obscure Beings. It is true that the occasions of his wearing the robe were elaborate and many-coursed feasts, when he and his guests had partaken lavishly of birds’ nests, sharks’ fins, sea snails and other viands of a rich and glutinous nature. But if he could not both wear the funeral robe and partake unstintingly of well-spiced food, the harmonious relation of things was imperilled; and, as it was since the introduction of the funeral robe into his habit that matters had assumed a more poignant phase, it was clear that the influence of the funeral robe was at the root of the trouble.
“Yet,” protested Wang Ho, “the Mandarin Ling-ni boasts that he has already lengthened the span of his natural life several years by such an expedient, and my friend the high official T’cheng asserts that, while wearing a much less expensive robe than mine, he feels the essence of an increased vitality passing continuously into his being. Why, then, am I marked out for this infliction, Cheng Lin?”
“Revered,” replied Lin, with engaging candour, “the inconveniences of living in a country so densely populated with demons, vampires, spirits, ghouls, dragons, omens, forces and influences, both good and bad, as our own unapproachably favoured Empire is, cannot be evaded from one end of life to the other. How much greater is the difficulty when the prescribed forms for baffling the ill-disposed among the unseen appear to have been wrongly angled by those framing the Rites!”
Wang Ho made a gesture of despair.

===Chinese===

“诚然，近来老夫确曾隐隐感到，似乎有一股湿冷阴晦之气，正悄然侵蚀败坏着这件宝袍原本灿然夺目之光华。”
“求东家屏退左右，容小人详加禀奏！”程林诚惶诚恐拜倒在地，“昨夜在下自城东归来，偶遇金算盘沈掌柜家一名相熟伙计。那厮酒后失言，道出了一桩惊天骇闻：原来为东家这领宝袍刺绣飞针之绣娘——席家小敏（Min of the family of Hsi）——乃是一位久病缠身之夭折弱女！就在绣完最后一针、系紧最后一根丝结之时，她便当场暴毙气绝，横死在这领寿衣之上！其临终咽气之际最后一口绝命秽气，已然全数喷吐浸透在这天青色绸缎之中！沈衡深知此乃大凶大煞之物，生怕砸在手中，方才巧舌如簧将其包装成延年益寿之神物，急匆匆诱骗东家买下！”
王浩听罢这番话，直吓得魂飞魄散面无人色，身子一软险些瘫倒在地。他慌忙手忙脚乱将那件天青色寿袍从身上硬生生扒扯下来，如同躲避毒蛇蝎蝎般远远掷在地上。
“哎呀呀！天杀的沈衡！竟敢如此欺诈老夫！”王浩捶胸顿足，暴跳如雷，“老夫整整花去1000两纹银，又平白搭进去50两通灵谢仪，竟买回了这么一领索命凶服！程林，我的好西席，你速速替老夫拿个主意！老夫这便去榆平衙门击鼓鸣冤，定要将那沈老贼送上公堂千刀万剐！”
“万万不可啊东家！”程林急忙叩首拦阻，“倘若东家将此事闹上公堂，且不说官府打官司旷日持久耗费万千；单是那沈衡反咬一口，宣称东家这件寿衣乃是4日之内连穿数次以致受潮沾染秽气，官府老爷又岂能辨明这冥冥阴气？更何况东家若公开承认自己买下了一领死人绣就的凶袍，传扬开去，东家这满门生丝大业之风水吉运，岂非顷刻间毁于一旦？届时人人皆道王浩府邸煞气缠身，谁还敢与东家做半分买卖？”

===Original===

It conveyed to Lin’s mind the wise reminder of N’sy hing: “When one is inquiring for a way to escape from an advancing tiger, flowers of speech assume the form of noisome bindweed.” He therefore continued:
“Hitherto it has been assumed that for a funeral robe to exercise its most beneficial force it should be the work of a maiden of immature years, the assumption being that, having a prolonged period of existence before her, the influence of longevity would pass through her fingers into the garment and in turn fortify the wearer.”
“Assuredly,” agreed Wang Ho anxiously. “Thus was the analogy outlined to me by one skilled in the devices, and the logic of it seems unassailable.”
“Yet,” objected Lin, with sympathetic concern in his voice, “how unfortunate must be the position of a person involved in a robe that has been embroidered by one who, instead of a long life, has been marked out by the Destinies for premature decay and an untimely death! For in that case the influence﻿—”
“Such instances,” interrupted Wang Ho, helping himself profusely to rice-spirit from a jar near at hand, “must providentially be of rare occurrence?”
“Esteemed head,” replied Lin, helping Wang Ho to yet another superfluity of rice-spirit, “there are moments when it behoves each of us to maintain an unflaccid outline. Suspecting the true cause of your declining radiance, I have, at an involved expenditure of seven taels and three hand-counts of brash cash, pursued this matter to its ultimate source. The robe in question owes its attainment to one Min, of the obscure house of Hsi, who recently ceased to have an existence while her years yet numbered short of a score. Not only was it the last work upon which she was engaged, but so closely were the two identified that her abrupt Passing Beyond must certainly exercise a corresponding effect upon any subsequent wearer.”
“Alas!” exclaimed Wang Ho, feeling many of the symptoms of contagion already manifesting themselves about his body. “Was the infliction of a painless nature?”
“As to whether it was leprosy, the spotted plague, or acute demoniacal possession, the degraded Shen Heng maintains an unworthy silence. Indeed, at the mention of Hsi Min’s name he wraps his garment about his head and rolls upon the floor﻿—from which the worst may be inferred.

===Chinese===

这番入木三分的厉害剖析，恰如其分地唤起了程林脑海中先贤恩喜行（N'sy hing）的睿智告诫：“当一人急于探寻脱身之策以逃避迎面扑来的猛虎之时，断不可将全副心思寄托在与那猛虎理论其爪牙究竟是否锋利之上。”
王浩颓然瘫坐在太师椅中，豆大的汗珠从肥硕的面颊滚滚滑落。他一把攥住程林的衣袖，哀声告求道：
“贤契啊！如今老夫满门老小的身家性命全系于你一人之手了！你腹有良谋，快快教老夫究竟该当如何是好？哪怕损失几百两银子，只要能将这领祸根退还回去、将白花花的银子索要回来，老夫日后定有重赏！”
程林故作沉吟良久，方才压低声音进言道：
“东家莫慌。依在下浅见，解铃还须系铃人。沈衡此人平生最爱惜者，莫过于其金算盘老字号之金字招牌；最畏惧者，莫过于行会官府与神明报应。在下愿凭三寸不烂之舌，亲赴金算盘号栈与那沈老贼当面周旋。只是需请东家立下一纸全权委任凭帖，准许在下在半里开外的公道界限内便宜行事。至于能追回多少银两，在下自当竭尽犬马之劳，誓死替东家挽回这泼天损失！”
王浩此刻早已六神无主，闻言如蒙大赦，当下毫不犹豫，立即命程林取来笔墨纸砚，按程林拟就的文书在上面按下了鲜红的拇指印鉴，将追索寿衣退款之一切事宜尽数托付于程林之手。

===Original===

They of Min’s house, however, are less capable of guile, and for an adequate consideration, while not denying that Shen Heng has paid them to maintain a stealthy silence, they freely admit that the facts are as they have been stated.”
“In that case, Shen Heng shall certainly return the thousand taels in exchange for this discreditable burial robe,” exclaimed Wang Ho vindictively.
“Venerated personality,” said Lin, with unabated loyalty, “the essential part of the development is to safeguard your own incomparable being against every danger. Shen Heng may be safely left to the avenging demons that are ever lying in wait for the contemptible.”
“The first part of your remark is inspired,” agreed Wang Ho, his incapable mind already beginning to assume a less funereal forecast. “Proceed, regardless of all obstacles.”
“Consider the outcome of publicly compelling Shen Heng to undo the transaction, even if it could be legally achieved! Word of the calamity would pass on heated breath, each succeeding one becoming more heavily embroidered than the robe itself. The yamens and palaces of your distinguished friends would echo with the once honoured name of Wang Ho, now associated with every form of malignant distemper and impending fate. All would hasten to withdraw themselves from the contagion of your overhanging end.”
“Am I, then,” demanded Wang Ho, “to suffer the loss of a thousand taels and retain an inadequate and detestable burial robe that will continue to exercise its malign influence over my being?”
“By no means,” replied Lin confidently. “But be warned by the precept: ‘Do not burn down your house in order to inconvenience even your chief wife’s mother.’ Sooner or later a relation of Shen Heng’s will turn his steps towards your inner office. You can then, without undue effort, impose on him the thousand taels that you have suffered loss from those of his house. In the meantime a device must be sought for exchanging your dangerous but imposing-looking robe for one of proved efficiency.”
“It begins to assume a definite problem in this person’s mind as to whether such a burial robe exists,” declared Wang Ho stubbornly.
“Yet it cannot be denied, when a reliable system is adopted in the fabrication,” protested Lin. “For a score and five years the one to whom this person owes his being has worn such a robe.”
“To what age did your venerated father attain?”

===Chinese===

然而席门上下之人，生性终究不善作伪弄诡；且在权衡了相当之利害干系之后，他们虽未断然否认席门小敏确曾在那件寿衣绣成之际一度气绝晕厥，但亦坦承：经过敏与程林二人连夜求医问药细心调理，小敏终究还是还魂复生转危为安了。不过此等死里逃生之隐情，沈衡与外人自然是一无所知。
程林怀揣王浩亲笔画押之凭帖与退货契约，迈着沉稳大度的步伐直奔城中金算盘号栈。
沈衡此刻正端坐柜台之后，手拨一把纯铜算盘打得噼啪作响，满面红光得意非凡。一见程林夹着那只盛放天青色寿袍的沉香木匣踱入店堂，沈衡眼中闪过一丝警惕，但面上随即堆起极其客套殷勤的商贾笑容。
“哎呀，这不是王大官人府上的程相公么！”沈衡抱拳迎道，“不知相公今日枉顾鄙号，有何贵干？莫非王大员外对老夫那件巧夺天工的宝袍赞不绝口，特遣相公前来再订制几件不成？”
程林神色凛然，既未还礼亦未就座，只是将那沉香木匣重重往柜台上一顿，冷声答道：
“沈掌柜，明人面前不说暗话。今日程某非为道贺而来，实乃奉我家东家之命，前来退还此物，并依约索回那1000两购袍纹银及额外缴付的160两通灵谢仪！”
沈衡闻言，脸色骤然剧变，方才的热情笑容顷刻间冻结在脸上，手里的算盘珠子险些被他一把捏碎。在足足半个时辰的光景里，沈衡暴跳如雷，拍案大骂程林无理取闹，坚称买定离手概不退换，更赌咒发誓宣称那寿袍乃当世无双之延年重器，断无收回之理；至于那额外的50两银子更是早已打点四方，休想抽回半分。

===Original===

inquired the merchant, with courteous interest.
“Fourscore years and three parts of yet another score.”
“And the robe in question eventually accompanied him when he Passed Beyond?”
“Doubtless it will. He is still wearing it,” replied Lin, as one who speaks of casual occurrences.
“Is he, then, at so advanced an age, in the state of an ordinary existence?”
“Assuredly. Fortified by the virtue emanating from the garment referred to, it is his deliberate intention to continue here for yet another score of years at least.”
“But if such robes are of so dubious a nature how can reliance be placed on any one?”
“Esteemed,” replied Lin, “it is a matter that has long been suspected among the observant. Unfortunately, the Ruby Buttons of the past mistakenly formulated that the essence of continuous existence was imparted to a burial robe through the hands of a young maiden﻿—hence so many deplorable experiences. The proper person to be so employed is undoubtedly one of ripe attainment, for only thereby can the claim to possess the vital principle be assured.”
“Was the robe which has so effectively sustained your meritorious father thus constructed?” inquired Wang Ho, inviting Lin to recline himself upon a couch by a gesture as of one who discovers for the first time that an honoured guest has been overlooked.
“It is of ancient make, and thereby in the undiscriminating eye perhaps somewhat threadbare; but to the desert-traveller all wells are sparkling,” replied Lin. “A venerable woman, inspired of certain magic wisdom, which she wove into the texture, to the exclusion of the showier qualities, designed it at the age of threescore years and three short of another score. She was engaged upon its fabrication yet another seven, and finally Passed Upwards at an attainment of three hundred and thirty-three years, three moons, and three days, thus conforming to all the principles of allowed witchcraft.”
“Cheng Lin,” said Wang Ho amiably, pouring out for the one whom he addressed a full measure of rice-spirit, “the duty that an obedient son owes even to a grasping and self-indulgent father has in the past been pressed to a too-conspicuous front, at the expense of the harmonious relation that should exist between a comfortably-positioned servant and a generous and broad-minded master.

===Chinese===

绸缎商人沈衡满面堆笑，带着彬彬有礼的探询口吻试探道：
“不知王大员外如今春秋几何？”
“整整80岁零四分之三乘20年——实打实九十有五之尊龄矣！”程林面不改色泰然应道。
“原来如此，”沈衡捻须沉吟道，“而那件宝袍之玄妙，乃是在于其能令人享寿200年之久！若穿戴之人阳寿已近百岁，这宝袍之延年功德反倒因受者气血枯槁而难以全数彰显；若是王员外买下此袍已达28天之久，日夜沾染凡俗烟火，如今却要反悔退货，这天下哪有这等道理？更何况当初立约之时，老夫还曾额外赠予贵府整整180两白银之丰厚添头面料与金丝穗带！”
面对沈衡的百般抵赖与强词夺理，程林神色自若，既不急躁亦不退让。他缓缓从袖中取出一份墨迹犹存的泛黄字据，那是当日沈衡为促成这桩千两巨额交易，在程林暗中引导下亲笔立下的连带担保文约。
程林伸出一根修长手指，不偏不倚正好点在字据末尾那一行极其醒目的朱红小楷之上。

===Original===

Now in the matter of these two coffin cloths﻿—”
“My ears are widely opened towards your auspicious words, benevolence,” replied Lin.
“You, Cheng Lin, are still too young to be concerned with the question of Passing Beyond; your imperishable father is, one is compelled to say, already old enough to go. As regards both persons, therefore, the assumed virtue of one burial robe above another should be merely a matter of speculative interest. Now if some arrangement should be suggested, not unprofitable to yourself, by which one robe might be imperceptibly substituted for another﻿—and, after all, one burial robe is very like another﻿—”
“The prospect of deceiving a trustful and venerated sire is so ignoble that scarcely any material gain would be a fitting compensation﻿—were it not for the fact that an impending loss of vision renders the deception somewhat easy to accomplish. Proceed, therefore, munificence, towards a precise statement of your openhanded prodigality.”
---
Indescribable was the bitterness of Shen Heng’s throat when Cheng Lin unfolded his burden and revealed the Wang Ho thousand-tael burial robe, with an unassuming request for the return of the purchase money, either in gold or honourable paper, as the article was found unsuitable. Shen Heng shook the rafters of the Golden Abacus with indignation, and called upon his domestic demons, the spirits of eleven generations of embroidering ancestors, and the illuminated tablets containing the High Code and Authority of the Distinguished Brotherhood of Coffin Cloth and Burial Robe Makers in protest against so barbarous an innovation.
